# CRIMINAL CODE

CRIMINAL CODE

Codification 0

Official Registry Supplement 147 of 22-Jan-1971

Last modification: 10-Feb-2014

Status: In Force

GENERAL NOTE:

The Comprehensive Organic Criminal Code shall enter into force one hundred eighty days from its publication in the Official Registry, given by Law No. 00, published in Official Registry Supplement 180 of February 10, 2014, which shall repeal this Code.

THE LEGAL COMMISSION

Whereas:

That subsequent to the year 1959 in which the codification of Common Criminal Law was carried out by the Permanent Legislative Commission, numerous and important reforms have been enacted that need to be adequately incorporated into the Criminal Code; and, In exercise of the authority conferred upon it by Supreme Decree No. 55, of July 8, 1970, issued by the President of the Republic, Doctor Don José María Velasco Ibarra.

Resolves:

First.- To proceed with the codification of the Criminal Code and order that it be published in the Official Registry so that it may have binding force.

Second.- To order that henceforth the new numbering of its articles be cited; and, Third.- To order that this Resolution be inserted in all editions of the Criminal Code codified by the Legal Commission.

## FIRST BOOK

OF OFFENSES, OF PERSONS RESPONSIBLE FOR OFFENSES

AND OF PUNISHMENTS IN GENERAL

#### TITLE I

OF CRIMINAL LAW

##### SOLE CHAPTER

###### Art. 1.- Criminal laws are all those that contain any precept sanctioned with the

threat of a penalty.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 201

LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 124 CODE OF EXECUTION OF SENTENCES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 11 ORGANIC LAW ON LAND TRANSPORT, TRANSIT AND ROAD SAFETY, Arts. 1

###### Art. 2.- No one may be punished for an act that is not expressly declared an

offense by criminal law, nor suffer a penalty that is not established therein. The offense must be declared, and the penalty established, prior to the act. An act ceases to be punishable if a law subsequent to its execution removes it from the number of offenses; and, if a conviction has already been handed down, the penalty shall be extinguished, whether or not it has begun to be served.

If the penalty established at the time of sentencing differs from that which was in force when the offense was committed, the less rigorous one shall be applied.

In general, all subsequent laws regarding the effects and extinction of actions and penalties shall be applied insofar as they are favorable to offenders, even if there is a final judgment.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 2

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 8

ORGANIC CODE OF THE JUDICIAL FUNCTION, Arts. 7, 157

###### Art. 3.- Criminal laws are presumed by law to be known by all those

over whom they govern. Consequently, no one may invoke ignorance thereof as an excuse.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 6, 13

TAX CODE, Arts. 312

###### Art. 4.

Extensive interpretation is prohibited in criminal matters. The judge must adhere strictly to the letter of the Law. In cases of doubt it shall be interpreted in the sense most favorable to the accused.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 3, 18, 19

LABOR CODE, Arts. 7

Jurisprudence:

Judicial Gazette, JURISDICTION IN CRIMINAL MATTERS, 21-Mar-1984

Judicial Gazette, EQUITY CRITERION, 29-Aug-1996

###### Art. 5.- Any offense committed within the territory of the Republic, by Ecuadorians

or foreigners, shall be tried and punished in accordance with Ecuadorian laws, except as otherwise provided by Law.

Offenses committed in the territory of the Republic are deemed to be:

Those executed aboard Ecuadorian warships or merchant vessels or aircraft, except in cases where merchant vessels are subject to foreign criminal law, in accordance with International Law; and those committed within the premises of an Ecuadorian Legation in a foreign country.

An offense is deemed committed in the territory of the State when the effects of the action or omission that constitutes it must occur in Ecuador or in places subject to its jurisdiction.

The national or foreigner who commits outside the national territory any of these offenses shall be punished in accordance with Ecuadorian law:

1a.- Crimes against the personality of the State;

2a.- Crimes of counterfeiting State seals, or use of counterfeit seals; 3a.- Crimes of counterfeiting currency or bank notes of legal tender in the State, or stamped securities, or Ecuadorian public credit securities;

4a.- Crimes committed by public officials in the service of the State, abusing their powers or violating the duties inherent to their functions;

5a.- Offenses against International Law; and, 6a.- Any other offense for which special provisions of the Law or international conventions establish the application of Ecuadorian law.

Foreigners who incur any of the offenses detailed above shall be tried and punished in accordance with Ecuadorian laws, provided they are apprehended in Ecuador, or their extradition is obtained.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 6, 7, 8, 128

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 18

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 13

Constitution, the Law on the matter and the Code of Criminal Procedure. Concordances:

###### Art. 7.

The Ecuadorian who, outside the cases contemplated in the preceding article, commits in a foreign country a crime for which Ecuadorian law has established a penalty of deprivation of liberty greater than one year, shall be punished according to the criminal law of Ecuador, provided he or she is found in Ecuadorian territory.

Note: Article Amended by First Final Provision of Law No. 24 published in Official Registry Supplement 144 of August 18, 2000 .

Note: Article Amended by First Final Provision of Law No. 24, published again in Official Registry 152 of August 30, 2000 .

###### Art. 8.

When the criminal Law makes any legal effect dependent on the passage of time, all days shall be counted for the computation of the legal period.

When two criminal provisions are in opposition, the special one shall prevail. Concordancias:

#### TITULO II

OF OFFENSES IN GENERAL

##### CAPITULO I

Of the Consummated Offense and the Attempt

###### Art. 10.- Offenses are imputable acts sanctioned by criminal laws, and are

divided into crimes and contraventions, according to the nature of the particular penalty.

Concordancias:

LEY DE SUSTANCIAS ESTUPEFACIENTES Y PSICOTROPICAS, Arts. 56

CODIGO TRIBUTARIO, Arts. 314, 315

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CALIFICACION DE INFRACCIONES, 31-ago-1929

###### Art. 11.- No one may be punished for an act provided by Law as an offense, if the

harmful or dangerous event on which the existence of the offense depends is not a consequence of their action or omission.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 76, 88

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 2, 88

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 106

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, ABOLICION DE LA PENA DE MUERTE, 31-mar-1884

Gaceta Judicial, CASACION POR ASESINATO, 11-jun-2009

###### Art. 12.- Failing to prevent an event, when one has the legal obligation to

prevent it, is equivalent to causing it.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO III), Arts. 1010

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 106

LEY ORGANICA DE EMPRESAS PUBLICAS, LOEP, Arts. 60

###### Art. 13.- Whoever voluntarily executes a punishable act shall be responsible for it, and

shall incur the penalty prescribed for the resulting offense, even if the harm the offender intended to cause varies, or falls upon a different person from the one they intended to harm.

In case preexisting, simultaneous or supervening causes, independent of the will of the author, concur with the punishable act, the following rules shall be observed:

If the event, which was not in the intention of the author, occurs as a consequence of the sum of one or more of these causes with the punishable act, the accused shall be liable for preterintentional crime.

If the event occurs as a result of one or more of said causes, without adding to the punishable act, the author shall be liable only for the offense constituted by the act itself.

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO PRETERINTENCIONAL, 24-abr-1974

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO PREINTENCIONAL, 08-mar-1976

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO PRETERINTENCIONAL, 11-may-1979

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO PRETERINTENCIONAL, 30-jul-1981

Gaceta Judicial, RESPONSABILIDAD EN MATERIA PENAL, 19-jul-1993

Gaceta Judicial, TENENCIA ILICITA DE ESTUPEFACIENTES, 16-may-1997

Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDIO SIMPLE Y PRETERINTENCIONAL, 07-ene-2003

###### Art. 14.- The offense is intentional or negligent.

The intentional offense, which is that in which there is the intent to cause harm, is: Intentional, when the harmful or dangerous event, which is the result of the action or omission on which the law makes the existence of the offense depend, was foreseen and intended by the agent as a consequence of their own action or omission; and; Preterintentional, when from the action or omission a harmful or dangerous event more serious than that intended by the agent is derived.

The offense is negligent when the event, being able to be foreseen but not intended by the agent, occurs due to negligence, imprudence, lack of skill, or failure to observe Law, regulations or orders.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 29

CODIGO TRIBUTARIO, Arts. 316

LEY ORGANICA DE EMPRESAS PUBLICAS, LOEP, Arts. 60

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CUERPO DEL DELITO, 23-mar-1955

###### Art. 15.- The action or omission provided by Law as an offense shall not be punishable

when it is the result of fortuitous event or force majeure.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 76

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 30

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 110

###### Art. 16.- Whoever performs suitable acts leading unequivocally to the commission

of a crime is liable for attempt if the action is not consummated or the event does not occur.

If the author voluntarily desists from the action they are subject only to the penalty for the acts executed, provided that these constitute a different offense, except when the Law, in special cases, qualifies the mere attempt as a crime.

If they voluntarily prevent the event, they are subject to the penalty established for the attempt, reduced by one third or one half.

Contraventions are only punishable when they have been consummated.

Concordancias:

CODIGO TRIBUTARIO, Arts. 326

###### Art. 17.- Conspiracy and proposal to commit a crime shall only be punished

in the cases that the Law determines.

It is understood that there is conspiracy when two or more persons agree for the execution of a crime; and there is proposal, when the one who has decided to commit it proposes its commission to another person or persons.

If the conspiracy or proposal, even in the case of being punished by Law, ceases to produce effects because its authors have voluntarily desisted from the execution, before judicial proceedings are initiated against them, no penalty shall be applied to them. Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIAS EXIMENTES, 13-may-1993

##### CAPITULO II

Of the circumstances of the offense

###### Art. 18.- There is no offense when the act is ordered by Law, or determined by

final resolution of competent authority, or when the accused was impelled to commit it by a force they could not resist.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1472

CODIGO DE EJECUCION DE PENAS Y REHABILITACION SOCIAL, Arts. 46

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, COMPETENCIA PENAL MILITAR, 12-abr-1916

Gaceta Judicial, COMPETENCIA PENAL MILITAR, 09-jun-1916

Gaceta Judicial, INEXISTENCIA DE INFRACCION, 30-ago-2004

###### Art. 19.- Whoever acts in necessary defense of their person commits no offense of any

kind, provided the following circumstances concur: actual unlawful aggression; rational necessity of the means employed to repel said aggression, and lack of sufficient provocation on the part of the person defending themselves.

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 09-feb-1884

Gaceta Judicial, MUERTE POR LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 18-abr-1977

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 17-nov-1978

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 19-mar-1980

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 29-jul-1981

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 18-dic-1981

Gaceta Judicial, LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 04-feb-1987

###### Art. 20.- It shall be understood that the circumstances enumerated in the preceding

article are present if the act took place while defending against the perpetrators of robbery or looting carried out with violence; or attacking an arsonist, or one who robs or steals during a fire, when they are apprehended in flagrante delicto; or repelling during the night the scaling or breaking of fences, walls or entrances to an inhabited house or apartment or their dependencies, unless it is established that the perpetrator could not have believed in an attack against persons, whether considering the direct purpose of the individual who was scaling or breaking, or the resistance that such individual's intentions should have encountered. Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 162

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, ROBO. REPARACIONES CIVILES, 27-ene-1887

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE ROBO AGRAVADO, 22-oct-2003

###### Art. 21.- No infraction is committed by one who acts in defense of another person, provided

that the first two circumstances of Art. 19 are present and that, in case of provocation having preceded against the aggressor, the defender did not take part in it. Nota: Incluida Fe de Erratas, publicada en Registro Oficial 165 de 17 de Febrero de 1971 , y nuevamente publicada en Registro Oficial 173 de 3 de Marzo de 1971 .

###### Art. 22.- Nor is there any infraction when a person kills or causes injuries to

another at the moment of being the victim of a crime of sexual abuse or rape. Nota: El adulterio como delito fue suprimido por el artículo final del Código de Procedimiento Penal, Ley No. 143, publicada en Registro Oficial 511 de 10 de Junio de Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 2, publicada en Registro Oficial 45 de 23 de Junio del 2005 .

###### Art. 23.

There is no infraction in blows that are delivered without causing serious wounds or injuries to those guilty of theft or robbery, when they are surprised in flagrante delicto, or with the stolen or robbed items.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 162

###### Art. 24.- No penalty shall be imposed on one who, in the necessity of avoiding a harm, executes

an act that produces damage to another's property, provided that the harm which was sought to be avoided is real, that it is greater than that caused in preventing it, and that there was no other practicable and less harmful means to prevent it.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 194

###### Art. 25.- Homicide, wounds and blows are excusable when they are provoked

by blows, wounds or other serious mistreatment by deed, or strong attacks on the honor or dignity, inflicted in the same act on the perpetrator of the act, or on his or her spouse, ascendants, descendants, siblings, or affines within the second degree.

The infractions determined in the preceding paragraph are also excusable when they are the result of an excess of legitimate defense.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 22, 23

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIAS EXCUSANTES, 24-feb-1981

Gaceta Judicial, EXCESO DE LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 28-ago-1985

Gaceta Judicial, MUERTE POR LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 27-ago-1999

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE ASESINATO Y NO LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 16-abr-2001

Gaceta Judicial, EXCESO DE LEGITIMA DEFENSA, 10-ene-2002

###### Art. 26.- Said infractions are equally excusable when they have been committed

while repelling during the day the scaling or breaking of fences, walls, or entrances of an inhabited house, or its dependencies; except when it is established that the perpetrator of the act could not have believed in an attack against persons, whether it tends to the direct purpose of the individual who was attempting the scaling or breaking, or to the effect of the resistance that such individual's intentions would encounter.

###### Art. 27.

Nota: Suspendido el efecto de este artículo. Dado por Resolución del Tribunal de Garantías Constitucionales, publicado en Registro Oficial 224 de 3 de Julio de 1989 . Nota: Artículo derogado por Ley No. 105, publicada en Registro Oficial 365 de 21 de Julio de 1998 .

###### Art. 28.

The grounds for excuse enumerated in Arts. 25 and 26 are not admissible if the guilty party commits the infraction upon the person of his or her ascendants.

###### Art. 29.- All circumstances are mitigating that, referring to the driving causes

of the infraction, to the physical and intellectual state and capacity of the offender, to his or her conduct with respect to the act and its consequences, diminish the gravity of the infraction, or the alarm caused in society, or make known the slight or no dangerousness of the perpetrator, as in the following cases:

1o.- Provocations, threats or insults by the attacked party preceding, these not being of those qualified as circumstances of excuse;

2o.- The guilty party being over sixty years of age;

3o.- The offender having attempted to repair the harm he or she caused, or to prevent the pernicious consequences of the event, with spontaneity and zeal;

4o.- Having committed the offense out of fear or under surmountable violence;

5o.- Presenting oneself voluntarily to justice, having been able to elude its action through flight or concealment;

6o.- Exemplary conduct observed by the guilty party subsequent to the infraction; 7o.- Prior conduct of the offender that clearly reveals that he or she is not a dangerous individual;

8o.- Rusticity of the offender, of such nature that it clearly reveals that he or she committed the punishable act out of ignorance;

9o.- Acting driven by motives of particular moral or social value;

10o.- Spontaneous confession, when it is truthful;

11o.- In crimes against property, when indigence, a large family, or lack of work have placed the offender in an exceptional situation; or when a public calamity made it very difficult for him or her to obtain means of subsistence honestly, at the time when he or she committed the infraction; and, 12o.- In crimes against property, the small value of the damage caused, relative to the possibilities of the offended party.

###### Art. 29-A.- For crimes of human trafficking and sexual crimes, mitigating circumstances shall not be considered,

except the following:

- l. That contemplated in numeral 5 of article 29; and,
  - 2. That the suspect, accused or defendant effectively collaborates with the authorities in the investigation of the crime.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 2, publicada en Registro Oficial 45 de 23 de Junio del 2005 .

Art. ....- Mitigating circumstances in function crimes of military or police servants.- For the imposition of penalties for function crimes of military or police servants, the mitigating circumstances contemplated in this code for common crimes shall apply, when these circumstances are present in the act. Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 196 de 19 de Mayo del 2010 .

###### Art. 30.- Aggravating circumstances are, when they are not constitutive or modifying elements of

the offense, all those that increase the malice of the act, or the alarm that the offense produces in society, or establish the dangerousness of its perpetrators, as in the following cases:

1o.- To execute the offense with treachery, treason, deceit or with assurance of safety; or for price, reward or promise; or by means of flood, shipwreck, fire, poison, mines, derailment of railways, prohibited weapons, or other means that endanger other persons in addition to the victim; or employing cunning, disguise, fraud; or with cruelty or viciousness, making use of any torture or other means of increasing and prolonging the pain of the victim; or making it impossible for the victim to defend themselves, whether by depriving them of the use of reason, or by employing accomplices in the commission of the crime; or having committed the latter as a means to commit another; or perpetrating the act by the perpetrator taking advantage of their position of authority, or deliberately entering the victim's house, or after having received some benefit from the victim;

2o.- To take advantage of fire, shipwreck, sedition, tumult or popular commotion or other public or private calamity or misfortune, to execute the offense;

3o.- To carry it out with the aid of armed people, or of persons who ensure impunity; or falsely taking the title, insignia or name of authority; or by means of a false order from the latter; or with contempt or offense of those who hold public power; or in the very place where they are exercising their functions; or where a religious ceremony of any cult permitted or tolerated in the Republic is being celebrated; 4o.- To execute the punishable act purposely seeking a deserted place or nighttime; or in a gang; or abusing the friendship or trust granted to the perpetrator; or with climbing or breaking; with lock picks or false and master keys; or with violence; 5o.- The perpetrator being pursued or a fugitive for a previous crime; having increased or tried to increase the harmful consequences of the offense; committing the act against a foreign consular or diplomatic agent; and, in crimes against property, causing damage of significant gravity, in consideration of the victim's conditions.

6o. To execute the offense for reasons of discrimination, referring to place of birth, age, sex, ethnicity, color, social origin, language, religion, political affiliation, economic position, sexual orientation, health status, disability or difference of any other nature.

Note: Item 6o. added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005.

###### Art. 30-A.- In the case of sexual crimes and human trafficking, the following shall be considered as

aggravating circumstances, when they are not constitutive or modifying elements of the offense and shall be applied without prejudice to the general aggravating circumstances indicated in the previous article:

- 1. If the victim is a person over sixty years of age or under eighteen years of age, a person with a disability or one of those whom the Civil Code considers incapable;
- 2. The victim being, at the time of the commission of the crime, in public or private establishments, such as those for health, education, worship, research, assistance or shelter, in social rehabilitation centers or in police or military premises, or other similar ones;
- 3. Taking advantage of the fact that the victim is going through a situation of vulnerability, extreme economic need or abandonment;
- 4. Having infected the victim with a serious, incurable or fatal disease, or having caused injuries that cause permanent incapacity, mutilations, loss or disablement of organs, physical disability, emotional disturbance, psychological or mental disorder;
- 5. If the victim were or became pregnant, or if she were in puerperium, or if she miscarried as a consequence of the commission of the crime;
- 6. If the victim were physically or mentally incapacitated;
- 7. The offender having some type of power and/or authority relationship over the victim, or if they are an adopter, guardian, curator or if they have the victim under their care, for any reason;
- 8. Sharing the family environment with the victim;
- 9. Knowing the victim prior to the commission of the crime;
- 10. If the sexual crime has been committed as a form of torture, or for purposes of intimidation, degradation, humiliation, discrimination, revenge or punishment;
- 11. If the crime has been committed by public officials, teachers or ministers of any cult, who have abused their position to commit it, by health professionals and persons responsible for patient care; or any other class of professional or person who has abused their function or position to commit the crime; and,
- 12. Having used to commit the crime, any substance that alters the knowledge or will of the victim.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005.

Art. ...- Aggravating circumstances of crimes of function of female and male police officers, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, are the following:

- 1. If the crime is committed with the use of weapons, or in serious internal commotion; or,
- 2. If the action caused the failure of a police operation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ...- Aggravating circumstances of crimes of function of female or male military officers, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, are any of the following:

- 1. If the crime is committed with the use of weapons or in armed conflict; or,
- 2. If the action caused the failure of a military operation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of

19 May 2010.

###### Art. 31.- It shall be deemed as an aggravating circumstance of the offense the fact that the

victim is a spouse, cohabitant, relative up to the fourth degree of consanguinity and second degree of affinity, or is an ascendant or descendant of the offender.

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005.

#### TITLE III

OF IMPUTABILITY AND OF THE PERSONS

RESPONSIBLE FOR OFFENSES

##### CHAPTER I

Of responsibility

###### Art. 32.- No one can be punished for an act provided for in the Law as an offense, if they

had not committed it with will and consciousness.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK IV), Arts. 1467

Jurisprudence:

Judicial Gazette, ILLICIT TRAFFICKING OF NARCOTIC SUBSTANCES, 19-Aug-2009


This excerpt sits under: PENAL CODE > BOOK ONE > TITLE II > CHAPTER II

###### Art. 33.- All infractions are deemed to be conscious and voluntary acts, while

the contrary is not proven; except when from the circumstances that preceded or accompanied the act, it can be deduced that there was no harmful intent in committing it. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 32

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1467

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DOCUMENTO FALSO, 19-feb-2009

###### Art. 34.

A person is not responsible who, at the moment the action or omission was performed, was, due to illness, in such a mental state that he or she was unable to understand or to will.

If the act has been committed by a mentally insane person, the judge having jurisdiction over the case shall decree his or her confinement in a psychiatric hospital; and he or she may not be released except with a hearing from the public ministry and following a satisfactory report by two physicians designated by the judge, who shall preferably be psychiatrists, regarding the full recovery of the confined person's intellectual faculties.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 219

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, INIMPUTABILIDAD, 24-nov-1998

Gaceta Judicial, INIMPUTABILIDAD DE PARANOICO, 13-mar-2002

###### Art. 35.- A person who, at the moment of performing the criminal act was, by reason of

illness, in such a mental state that, although the capacity to understand or to will was diminished, it did not absolutely incapacitate him or her from doing so, shall be liable for the infraction committed, but the penalty shall be reduced as established by this Code.

###### Art. 36.- When the action or omission that the Law has provided as an infraction is, as

to the fact and not the law, the result of the deception of another person, the person who determined the deceived person to commit it shall be liable for the act of the deceived person.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1467

###### Art. 37.- In the case of intoxication of the active subject of the infraction, or of

intoxication by narcotic substances, the following rules shall be observed: 1a.- If intoxication resulting from fortuitous event or force majeure deprived the perpetrator of knowledge at the moment he or she committed the act, there shall be no liability;

2a.- If the intoxication was not complete, but greatly diminished knowledge, there shall be mitigated liability;

3a.- Intoxication not resulting from fortuitous event or force majeure neither excludes, nor mitigates, nor aggravates liability;

4a.- Premeditated intoxication, for the purpose of committing the infraction, or of preparing an excuse, is an aggravating circumstance; and, 5a.- Habitual intoxication is an aggravating circumstance. A person who indulges in the use of alcoholic beverages, or frequently walks about in a state of intoxication, is considered a habitual drunkard. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 30

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2218

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 126 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, EXIMENTE DE RESPONSABILIDAD PENAL, 04-dic-1889

Gaceta Judicial, EMBRIAGUEZ, 10-jun-1950

Gaceta Judicial, INIMPUTABILIDAD EN ASESINATO, 30-sep-1999

###### Art. 38.- The rules of the preceding article shall be observed, respectively, in cases of

intoxication by narcotic substances.

Concordancias:

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 126

###### Art. 39.- When a deaf-mute commits a crime, he or she shall not be punished if it is fully

established that he or she acted without consciousness and will; but he or she may be placed in a suitable educational institution, for up to ten years; and if it is established that he or she acted with consciousness and will, a penalty not exceeding one-half nor less than one-quarter of that established for the crime shall be applied.

###### Art. 40.- Persons who have not reached 18 years of age shall be subject to the

Code on Childhood and Adolescence.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 2, publicada en Registro Oficial 45 de 23 de Junio del 2005 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 175

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 21

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 114

##### CAPITULO II

Persons liable for infractions

###### Art. 41.- Those liable for infractions are the perpetrators, the accomplices and the

accessories after the fact.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 310

###### Art. 42.- Those who have perpetrated the infraction are deemed perpetrators, whether in a

direct and immediate manner, or by advising or instigating another to commit it, when the advice determined the perpetration of the crime; those who have prevented or attempted to prevent its execution from being avoided; those who have determined the perpetration of the crime and effected it by availing themselves of other persons, imputable or not imputable, by means of price, gift, promise, order or any other fraudulent and direct means; those who have cooperated in the execution, in a principal manner, by deliberately and intentionally performing some act without which the infraction could not have been perpetrated; and those who, by physical violence, abuse of authority, threat or other coercive means, compel another to commit the punishable act, even though the force employed for such purpose cannot be qualified as irresistible.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 110

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO III), Arts. 1010, 1011

LEY DE SUSTANCIAS ESTUPEFACIENTES Y PSICOTROPICAS, Arts. 79, 80, 81 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, AUTORES DE DELITO, 01-dic-1943

Gaceta Judicial, AUTOR DEL DELITO, 15-feb-1995

###### Art. 43.- Accomplices are those who indirectly and secondarily cooperate in the execution of the

punishable act, by means of prior or simultaneous acts.

If from the particular circumstances of the case it results that the accused of complicity intended to cooperate only in an act less serious than that committed by the perpetrator, the penalty shall be applied to the accomplice only in respect of the act he or she intended to execute. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 110

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO III), Arts. 1010, 1011

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2216

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 136 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, COMPLICE DE HOMICIDIO, 29-jun-1979

Gaceta Judicial, AUTOR DEL DELITO Y COMPLICE, 07-dic-1993

Gaceta Judicial, DISTINCION ENTRE AUTOR O COMPLICE DEL DELITO, 27-ene-1994

###### Art. 44.- Accessories after the fact are those who, knowing the criminal conduct of the

malefactors, habitually provide them with lodging, hiding place, or meeting place; or provide them with the means to take advantage of the effects of the crime committed; or favor them, by concealing the instruments or material evidence of the infraction, or by rendering useless the signs or traces of the crime, to avoid its punishment; and those who, being obliged by reason of their profession, employment, art or trade, to perform the examination of the signs or traces of the crime, or the clarification of the punishable act, conceal or alter the truth, with the purpose of favoring the offender.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO III), Arts. 1011

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 136 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, ENCUBRIMIENTO EN EL DELITO DE ROBO, 28-feb-1994

Gaceta Judicial, OMISION CULPOSA Y ENCUBRIMIENTO, 06-sep-1994

###### Art. 45.- Being an accessory after the fact for the benefit of the accused's spouse is exempt from punishment; or

of his or her ascendants, descendants and siblings, or of his or her relatives by affinity up to the second degree.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 22, 23

###### Art. 46.- Perpetrators of attempt shall suffer a penalty of one to two thirds of that which would

have been imposed on them if the crime had been consummated. For the application of the penalty, the danger run by the passive subject of the infraction and the background of the accused shall necessarily be taken into consideration.

###### Art. 47.- Accomplices shall be punished with half the penalty that would have been

imposed on them if they had been perpetrators of the crime.

###### Art. 48.- Accessories after the fact shall be punished with one quarter of the penalty applicable to the

perpetrators of the crime; but in no case shall this exceed two years, nor shall it be imprisonment.

###### Art. 49.- In cases of crimes against persons, intimate friends and those who have received great

benefits from the person responsible for the crime, before its commission, shall be exempt from liability for concealment.

###### Art. 50.

In the case of limited knowledge due to illness, contemplated in Art. 35, the penalty applicable to the offender shall be from one quarter to one half of that prescribed for the offense, according to the circumstances that shall be duly assessed by the judge.

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, ACTION WITH DIMINISHED CONSCIOUSNESS AND WILL, 07-may-2002

#### TITULO IV

OF PENALTIES

##### CAPITULO I

Of penalties in general

###### Art. 51.- The penalties applicable to offenses are the following:

Penalties specific to crimes:

1.- Major imprisonment;

2.- Minor imprisonment;

3.- Imprisonment from eight days to five years;

4.- Interdiction of certain political and civil rights;

5.- Subjection to the surveillance of the authority;

6.- Deprivation of the exercise of professions, arts or trades; and, 7.- Perpetual incapacity for the performance of any public employment or office. Penalties specific to misdemeanors:

1.- Imprisonment from one to thirty days.

2.- Fine.

Penalties common to all offenses:

1.- Fines.

2.- Special Confiscation.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de Octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 160 de 29 de Marzo del 2010 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 76

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 123 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, THE OFFENSE IS CLASSIFIED BY THE MAGNITUDE OF THE PENALTY, 31-jul-1958

###### Art. 52.- Every conviction carries with it the joint and several obligation to pay the

procedural costs by all those responsible for the crime. Damages shall likewise be paid jointly and severally by all those responsible against whom a private prosecution has been brought with the purpose of obtaining such compensation. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2214

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 309, 312

LEY ORGANICA DE TRANSPORTE TERRESTRE TRANSITO Y SEGURIDAD VIAL, Arts. 108, 118, 175 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, LIABILITY FOR CRIMES, 02-mar-1885

Gaceta Judicial, COMPENSATION FOR DAMAGES, 01-mar-1887

###### Art. 53.- Major imprisonment, which shall be served in the State Social Rehabilitation Centers,

is divided into:

- a) Ordinary from four to eight years and, from eight to twelve years;
- b) Extraordinary from twelve to sixteen years; and
- c) Special from sixteen to twenty-five years.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 103, 110

CODIGO DE EJECUCION DE PENAS Y REHABILITACION SOCIAL, Arts. 20

###### Art. 54.- Minor imprisonment, which shall be served in the aforementioned establishments, is

divided into ordinary from three to six years and from six to nine years, and into extraordinary from nine to twelve years.

Those sentenced to minor imprisonment shall also be subject to re-education work or work in common workshops; and they shall only be made to work outside the establishment when agricultural penal colonies are organized, and they shall not be isolated, except for regulatory punishments, which may not exceed eight days.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

###### Art. 55.- Correctional imprisonment shall be served by the convicted in the jails of the respective

canton, in those of the provincial capital or in appropriate sections of the penitentiaries, and they must engage in regulatory work, in common workshops.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

###### Art. 56.- Every sentence to ordinary or extraordinary major imprisonment, or extraordinary minor

imprisonment, carries with it the interdiction of the convicted person, while the penalty lasts. The interdiction takes effect from the time the sentence becomes final, and deprives the convicted person of the capacity to dispose of their property, except by testamentary act.

Those sentenced to ordinary minor imprisonment, in the case of recidivism, or in the case of concurrence of several crimes that merit imprisonment, shall also be subject to interdiction.

The appointment of the corresponding guardian shall be made in accordance with the rules of the Civil Code for the curatorship of the spendthrift.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 64

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 367, 371, 383, 415, 416, 463, 468

###### Art. 57.

No imprisonment penalty shall be imposed on persons over sixty-five years of age. A person who at that age commits a crime punishable by imprisonment shall serve the time of the sentence in an establishment designated for correctional imprisonment.

If already in imprisonment the person turns sixty-five years of age, they shall be transferred to serve their sentence in a prison house, in accordance with the preceding paragraph.

Judges may resolve the same with respect to weak or sick persons. Nota: Incluida Fe de Erratas, publicada en Registro Oficial 165 de 17 de Febrero de 1971 , y nuevamente publicada en Registro Oficial 173 de 3 de Marzo de 1971 .

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 2, publicada en Registro Oficial 45 de 23 de Junio del 2005 .

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Art. 5 de Ley No. 1, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 393 de 31 de Julio del 2008 .

###### Art. 57-A.- Article 57 as substituted in the Criminal Code shall be interpreted as follows:

"Correctional imprisonment" and "prison house" shall be understood as specialized places for the rehabilitation of older adults, which shall be administered by the Ministry of Economic and Social Inclusion in coordination with the Ministry of Justice and Human Rights.

Nota: Artículo dado por Art. 6 de Ley No. 1, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 393 de 31 de Julio del 2008 .

###### Art. 58.- No pregnant woman may be deprived of her liberty, nor shall she be notified

with a sentence imposing penalties of imprisonment, except 90 days after childbirth.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 105, publicada en Registro Oficial 365 de 21 de Julio de 1998 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 43

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 61

###### Art. 59.- The duration of a day for computing the time of the sentence is twenty-four

hours; and that of a month, thirty days.

All detention, before the judgment becomes final, shall be credited toward the duration of the penalty of deprivation of liberty, if such detention has been occasioned by the offense that is punished.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE EJECUCION DE PENAS Y REHABILITACION SOCIAL, Arts. 56

###### Art. 60.- Every sentence that condemns to reclusion or to prison causes the suspension of

citizenship rights for a time equal to that of the sentence; but in the cases expressly determined by this Code, the judges and courts may impose the suspension of such rights, for a term of three to five years, even if the prison does not exceed six months.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 64, 113

###### Art. 61.- By virtue of subjection to the special surveillance of the authority, the judge may

prohibit the convicted person from appearing in the places that he designates, after the sentence has been served; for which, before being released, the convicted person shall indicate the place he chooses for his residence, and shall receive a travel pass, in which the mandatory itinerary and the duration of his stay in each place of transit shall be determined. Furthermore, he shall be obliged to appear before the police authority of the place of his residence within twenty-four hours following his arrival, and may not move to another place, without written permission from said authority, which has the right to impose on the person under surveillance an occupation and way of life, if he does not have them.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE EJECUCION DE PENAS Y REHABILITACION SOCIAL, Arts. 57

###### Art. 62.- Those condemned to a penalty of reclusion may be placed, by the condemnatory

sentence, under the surveillance of the authority, for five to ten years; and if they recidivate in the same crime or commit another that merits the penalty of reclusion, that surveillance shall last for life.

###### Art. 63.

Fines for crimes belong to the Treasury; and shall be imposed on each of those condemned for the same infraction.

The fine shall be collected by real attachment.

###### Art. 64.

In the sentence the judge may authorize the convicted person to pay the fine in installments; the amount and the date of the payments being fixed, according to the economic condition of the convicted person.

###### Art. 65.

Special confiscation falls: on the things that were the object of the infraction; on those that have served, or have been destined to commit it, when they are the property of the author of the punishable act, or of the accomplice; and on those that have been produced by the infraction itself.

Special confiscation shall be imposed for a crime, without prejudice to the other penalties established by Law; but, in the case of a contravention, it shall be imposed only in the cases expressly determined by Law.

Concordancias:

LEY DE SUSTANCIAS ESTUPEFACIENTES Y PSICOTROPICAS, Arts. 83

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, PENAS DE COMISO Y MULTAS, 16-sep-1913

###### Art. 66.- Work is mandatory in establishments destined for reclusion and correctional

prison, and its product shall be invested in the manner indicated in the respective Law. The product of the work of the convicted person is not susceptible to embargo or sequestration, except for the payment of compulsory alimony.

Nota: Incluida Fe de Erratas, publicada en Registro Oficial 165 de 17 de Febrero de 1971 , y nuevamente publicada en Registro Oficial 173 de 3 de Marzo de 1971 . Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 349

###### Art. 67.- The condemnation to the penalties established by this Code is independent of the

indemnification for damages and losses in accordance with the norms of the Civil and Civil Procedure Codes. Once the amount of indemnification is determined, it shall be collected by real attachment.

The injured party or whoever exercises his legal representation may claim before the criminal jurisdiction the indemnification for damages and losses caused by the crime, through the corresponding private accusation filed for such purpose. The liquidation of indemnifications declared in final judgment shall be carried out in summary oral trial, as prescribed by the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Collection shall be made by real attachment against the debtor or the civilly liable party.

In case of proven insolvency, there shall be no attachment for procedural costs. Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 31, 159, 176, 186, 245, 309, 312, 391, 402 CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 879, 972, 974

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1453, 1572, 2218, 2229, 2231, 2232, 2233, 2234, 2351 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DAÑOS Y PERJUICIOS, 11-ene-1886

Gaceta Judicial, INDEMNIZACION DE DAÑOS Y PERJUICIOS, 31-jul-1929

###### Art. 68.- When the property of the convicted person is not sufficient to pay the damages and

losses, the fine and the restitutions, the first two condemnations shall be preferred; and in concurrence of fine and costs owed to the Treasury, the payments made by the convicted persons shall be imputed first to the costs.

###### Art. 69.- No penalty may be executed while an appeal or clarification

of the sentence is pending.

###### Art. 70.- Civil obligations derived from infractions are not extinguished by the

death of the offender.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2216

###### Art. 71.

The guilty party is obliged to publish, at his cost, the condemnatory sentence, when the publication constitutes the means of repairing the non-pecuniary damage caused by the crime.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2231

##### CAPITULO II

Of the application and modification of penalties

###### Art. 72.- When there are two or more mitigating circumstances and no aggravating circumstance,

not constitutive or modificatory of the infraction, the penalties of reclusion shall be reduced or modified in the following manner:

Special major reclusion of sixteen to twenty-five years shall be replaced with extraordinary major reclusion of twelve to sixteen years.

Extraordinary major reclusion of twelve to sixteen years shall be replaced with ordinary major reclusion of eight to twelve years.

Ordinary major reclusion of eight to twelve years shall be replaced with ordinary major reclusion of four to eight years.

Ordinary major reclusion of four to eight years shall be replaced with ordinary minor reclusion of three to six years.

Extraordinary minor reclusion of nine to twelve years shall be replaced with ordinary minor reclusion of six to nine years.

Ordinary minor reclusion of six to nine years shall be replaced with correctional prison of two to five years.

Ordinary minor reclusion of three to six years shall be replaced with correctional prison of one to three years.

Nota: Agregado inciso final por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de Octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIAS ATENUANTES, 18-nov-1963

Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIA AGRAVANTE DE LA INFRACCION, 10-nov-1993

Gaceta Judicial, PENA DE RECLUSION MAYOR ORDINARIA, 28-ene-1994

Gaceta Judicial, ATENUANTES EN LA PENA, 15-feb-1995

Gaceta Judicial, MODIFICACION DE LA PENA, 29-sep-2000

###### Art. 73.- If there are two or more mitigating circumstances and no aggravating circumstance not constitutive

or modificatory of infraction, the correctional penalties of prison and fine shall be reduced, respectively, to as little as eight days and six dollars of the United States of North America, and the judges may apply only one of these penalties, separately, or replace that of prison with a fine, up to twelve dollars of the United States of North America, if only the former is prescribed by Law.

7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, EXISTENCIA DE UNA SOLA CIRCUNSTANCIA ATENUANTE, 26-jul-1993

###### Art. 74.- When there is in favor of the accused a single mitigating circumstance of transcendental character and it

concerns a subject whose background does not reveal dangerousness, there being no aggravating circumstances not constitutive or modificatory of infraction, the judges may appreciate it for the modification of the penalty, in accordance with the rules of the preceding articles.

###### Art. 75.

When any of the circumstances of excuse, determined in Arts. 25, 26 and 27, exist, the penalties shall be reduced in the following manner:

If it concerns a crime that merits extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years, the penalty shall be substituted by correctional imprisonment of one to five years and a fine not exceeding thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America;

If it concerns an infraction punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, the penalty of correctional imprisonment of one to four years and a fine not exceeding nineteen dollars of the United States of North America shall be applied;

If the infraction is punished with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years, this penalty shall be substituted with correctional imprisonment of one to three years and a fine not exceeding sixteen dollars of the United States of North America;

If the penalty designated for the infraction is extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, it shall be replaced with correctional imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine not exceeding twelve dollars of the United States of North America;

If the infraction is punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the penalty of correctional imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine not exceeding nine dollars of the United States of North America shall be applied;

If the penalty to be applied is minor imprisonment of three to six years, it shall be replaced with correctional imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America; and, If it concerns a crime punished with correctional imprisonment, the penalty shall be reduced to imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of five dollars of the United States of North America, or only one of these penalties.

7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 76.- The reduction of the imprisonment penalty, by virtue of mitigating circumstances, does not

prevent the convicted person from being placed under the special surveillance of the authority for at least three years, and at most six.

###### Art. 77.- There is recidivism when the guilty party commits a crime again after

having committed a previous one for which they received a conviction sentence. Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, REINCIDENCIA PENAL, 22-may-1925

Gaceta Judicial, FIGURA DE REINCIDENCIA ESPECIFICA, 28-jul-1994

###### Art. 78.- In contraventions there is recidivism when the same

contravention or a greater one is committed, in the ninety days following the conviction for the first offense.

Recidivism, in the case of the contravention established in numeral 1 of article 607, shall be considered as a crime, in accordance with Chapter I of Title X of

## Libro II de este Código.

Nota: Inciso segundo agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento

160 de 29 de Marzo del 2010 .

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, REINCIDENCIA PENAL, 23-jul-1952

###### Art. 79.- Conviction sentences issued abroad shall be taken into account

for recidivism.

Likewise, conviction sentences pronounced by military tribunals shall be taken into account but only when dealing with crimes of the same nature; and, in this case, only the minimum of the penalty that could have been imposed in the first conviction and not what was actually applied shall be considered.

###### Art. 80.- In case of recidivism the penalty shall be increased according to the following rules:

1a.- Whoever having been previously convicted to a penalty of imprisonment commits a crime punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years, shall suffer the same penalty, but from eight to twelve;

2a.- If the new crime is punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, the offender shall be sentenced to extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years; 2b.- Whoever having been previously convicted to a penalty of imprisonment commits a crime punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years; and, if the new crime is punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years, the penalty shall be twenty-five years, not subject to modification.

3a.- If an individual, after having been convicted to a penalty of imprisonment, commits a crime punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years, shall suffer the same penalty, but from six to nine;

4a.- If the new crime committed is of those that the Law punishes with minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the transgressor shall be sentenced to extraordinary minor imprisonment; 5a.- If whoever was convicted to extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years commits another infraction punished with the same penalty, shall be sentenced to major imprisonment of twelve years;

6a.- If whoever has been convicted to imprisonment commits a crime punished with correctional imprisonment, shall be punished with the maximum of the penalty for the newly committed crime; and, in addition, they shall be subjected to the surveillance of the authority for a time equal to that of the conviction;

7a.- If whoever has been convicted to a correctional penalty recidivates in the same crime, or commits another that also merits a correctional penalty, shall be punished with the maximum of the penalty designated for the most recently committed crime; and, 8a.- If an individual convicted to a correctional penalty commits a crime punished with imprisonment, the penalty designated for the last infraction shall be applied, without being able to recognize mitigating circumstances.

Nota: Agregado numeral por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

###### Art. 81.- In case of concurrence of several infractions, the following rules shall be observed:

1.- If several crimes punished with correctional penalties concur, or one or more of these crimes with one or more contraventions, all fines and correctional and police imprisonment penalties shall be accumulated; but in such manner that the fine may not exceed double the most severe; and correctional imprisonment, six years;

2.- When a crime punished with imprisonment concurs with crimes punished with correctional prison or one or more contraventions, the penalty established for the most serious crime shall be imposed;

3.- When several crimes punished with imprisonment concur, the greater penalty shall be imposed.

When several crimes punished with special major imprisonment concur, the penalties shall be accumulated for a maximum of thirty-five years;

4.- Special confiscation penalties by virtue of several concurrent infractions shall always be accumulated;

5.- When there is concurrence of several contraventions, all penalties deserved by the contravener shall be accumulated, but they may not exceed the maximum of the police penalty; and, 6.- When a single act constitutes several infractions, only the most severe penalty shall be imposed, which shall be up to thirty-five years, if it concerns infractions sanctioned with special major imprisonment.

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 318

LAW ON NARCOTIC AND PSYCHOTROPIC SUBSTANCES, Arts. 87

Judicial Gazette, PENAL ABSORPTION SYSTEM, 17-jun-1983

Judicial Gazette, ACCUMULATION OF PENALTIES, 22-may-1984

###### Art. 82.- In cases of conviction for the first time, if it is caused by a crime sanctioned

with a penalty whose maximum does not exceed six months of correctional prison or for a crime to which only a fine is applied, the judges may order in the same sentence that the execution of the penalty be suspended. This decision shall be based on the criterion regarding the integral personality of the convicted person, the nature of the crime and the circumstances that have surrounded it, insofar as they may serve to assess said personality. The judges shall require the information they deem pertinent to form a criterion.

Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 170, 174, 312

###### Art. 83.- In the case of concurrence of infractions, conditional conviction shall proceed

if the maximum penalty applicable to the offender does not exceed six months of imprisonment or were only a fine.

###### Art. 84.- The conviction shall be held as not pronounced if within the time set for the

prescription of the penalty and two more years, the convicted person does not commit a new infraction.

###### Art. 85.- If the convicted person, during the time indicated in the preceding article, commits

a new infraction, he shall suffer the penalty imposed in the first conviction and that which corresponds to the new act committed.

###### Art. 86.- Conditional conviction does not suspend the reparation of damages and losses

caused by the crime, the payment of procedural costs, nor special confiscation.

###### Art. 87.- Every convicted person who has served three-fourths of the sentence, in

the case of imprisonment, and two-thirds, in the case of correctional prison, may be placed on conditional release, by resolution of the corresponding authority, provided that he has regularly complied with prison regulations and observed very good conduct, revealing repentance and amendment, under the following conditions:

1a.- To reside in the place determined in the respective order, not being able to leave that residence except with permission of the authority that granted him freedom;

2a.- That, when he obtains said permission, upon moving to another place, he make known the permission to the first police authority of his new residence;

3a.- That he prove having a profession, art, trade or industry, or goods of fortune, or other means that allow him to live honestly;

4a.- That the time remaining to serve the penalty does not exceed three years;

5a.- That, having been convicted to pay civil indemnities, he prove having fulfilled this obligation, unless having proven impossibility to do so; and, 6a.- That the Institute of Criminology in the Capital of the Republic or the institution or authority designated, in the Provincial Capitals, grant a favorable report to conditional release.

Note: Sixth numeral substituted by Supreme Decree 1458, published in Official Register 323 of October 4, 1971.

Note: Final paragraph added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Concordances:

CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31 REGULATION TO THE CODE OF EXECUTION OF PENALTIES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 40, 41

###### Art. 88.- If he who obtained his conditional release, during the time remaining to

serve the sentence and up to two more years, observes bad conduct, or does not live from honest work if he lacks goods, or frequents gambling dens or taverns, or ordinarily accompanies himself with vicious people or of bad reputation, the respective authority shall revoke conditional release, so that he serves the part of the penalty that was missing when obtaining freedom, whatever time has elapsed since then.

If he commits a new crime, in addition to the time remaining from the first conviction, he shall suffer the penalty for the newly committed crime.

###### Art. 89.- Once the time of the conviction and two more years has elapsed, without the

conditional release having been revoked, the penalty shall be extinguished.

###### Art. 90.- No convicted person whose conditional release has been revoked may obtain it

again.

###### Art. 91.- Upon notifying the offender of the conviction sentence, the

provisions contained in the preceding articles shall be read to him, in any case.

###### Art. 92.- The offender who obtains his conditional release shall remain subject to the

special surveillance of the authority for the time remaining to serve the sentence and two more years.

###### Art. 93.- The discovery of a prior crime, duly proven, suspends the

effects of conditional conviction.

##### CHAPTER III

Of the exercise of actions and of the extinction and prescription of the same and of penalties

###### Art. 94.- The pardon of the offended party or the transaction with such party, does not extinguish the

public action for an infraction that must be prosecuted ex officio. Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 32, 34, 36

###### Art. 95.- The crime committed to the detriment of several persons shall be punished even though the

accusation or complaint is filed only by one of them.

Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 58

###### Art. 96.

The death of the offender, occurring before conviction, extinguishes the criminal action.

###### Art. 97.- Every penalty is personal and is extinguished with the death of the convicted person.

###### Art. 98.- The criminal action is extinguished by amnesty, or by waiver of the offended party in

crimes of private action, or by prescription.

The waiver by the offended party of the exercise of the criminal action only prejudices the renouncer and their heirs.

When an accusation or complaint has been filed, as the case may be, by several persons offended by the same crime, the waiver of one of them shall not prejudice the others. Concordances:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 120

Jurisprudence:

Gaceta Judicial, AMNISTIA, 27-sep-1932

###### Art. 99.- Amnesty shall not only terminate the criminal action but also the conviction and

all its effects, with the exception of civil indemnifications. Concordances:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 120

###### Art. 100.- The penalty is also extinguished by declaration of the Senate Chamber

rehabilitating the honor and establishing the innocence of those unjustly convicted, in accordance with the provisions of the Political Constitution of the State and the Code of Criminal Procedure.

Concordances:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 120

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 416, 417, 420

###### Art. 101.- Every criminal action prescribes in the time and under the conditions that the Law

indicates.

In the exercise of the right that prescription establishes, the following rules shall be observed:

Both in crimes of public action and in crimes of private action, it shall be distinguished above all whether, once the crime has been committed, prosecution has been initiated or not. With the exception of cases of imprescriptibility of actions and penalties provided for in the last paragraph of number 2 of article 23 and in the second paragraph of article 121 of the Political Constitution of the Republic, in other crimes punished with imprisonment, whose exercise of action is public, if there is no prosecution, the action to pursue them shall prescribe in ten years; in the case of crimes punished with special major imprisonment, the action to pursue them shall prescribe in fifteen years. In the case of crimes punished with prison, the action to pursue them shall prescribe in five years. The time shall be counted from the date on which the offense was perpetrated.

In the same crimes of public action, if prosecution has been initiated before those periods are fulfilled, the action to continue the case shall prescribe in the same periods, counted from the date of the opening order of proceedings.

If the accused appears voluntarily before justice within the maximum period of six months after the commencement of the investigation, the respective periods shall be reduced to ten years in crimes punished with special major imprisonment; to eight years in other crimes punished with imprisonment; and, to four years in crimes punished with prison. In these cases, the periods shall be counted from the date of commencement of the investigation. This rule shall have no effect in case of recidivism.

In crimes of private action, the action to pursue them shall prescribe within the period of one hundred eighty days, counted from when the offense was committed.

Once the action has been initiated and the defendant has been summoned, before the expiration of that period, prescription shall occur after two years from the date of service of the complaint.

The criminal action for crimes punished only with a fine shall be extinguished at any stage of the trial, by voluntary payment of the maximum fine corresponding to the crime, and of the indemnifications, in cases where applicable.

If prescription has occurred due to the lack of timely dispatch by the judges, they shall be punished by the superior with a fine of forty-four to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, without prejudice to the action for damages that may be brought against said officials, in accordance with the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure.

The same penalty shall be incurred by officials of the public ministry and secretaries of courts and tribunals through whose negligence the prescription has occurred.

If there is a private prosecutor, or if it is a complaint, the fine shall be divided in equal parts between the administration of justice and the accuser. The part of the fine that corresponds to the administration of justice shall be invested by the Supreme Court as the case may be, or by the respective Superior Court that imposed the fine, in general expenses of the administration of justice.

Note: Fourth and sixth paragraphs substituted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Fourth and sixth paragraphs substituted by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001 .

August 7, 2002 .

###### Art. 102.- If, once the sentence that becomes final has been issued by the superior, the

case is not sent to the judge in charge of executing it within a time equal to or greater than that necessary for the prescription of the penalty, the employees or officials responsible for the delay shall incur the sanction established in the preceding article.

###### Art. 103.- The incapacities annexed to certain convictions, by Law or judicial sentence, do not

cease by the pardon that may be granted in accordance with the Constitution and the laws, unless the decree of grace expressly states so.

Concordances:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 120

###### Art. 104.- Every person convicted to major or minor imprisonment who obtains a pardon or

commutation of the penalty shall remain under special surveillance by the authority, for up to a term of ten years, if the decree of grace does not provide otherwise.

###### Art. 105.- Civil interdiction shall cease when the convicted person has obtained a pardon of

the penalty, or when it has been commuted for another that does not carry such interdiction.

Concordances:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 367, 371

###### Art. 106.- The authority designated by the Constitution may pardon, or commute, or

reduce the penalties applied by final judicial sentence, subject to the special provisions of the Constitution and the Law of Grace.

The pardon, commutation, or reduction of the penalty shall not extend to exonerate the guilty party from payment of damages and costs to the Treasury, or to interested third parties. Concordances:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 147

LEY DE GRACIA, Arts. 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7

###### Art. 107.- Custodial sentences for crimes prescribe in a time equal to that of the sentence, and in no case may the prescription period be less than six months.

The prescription of the sentence shall begin to run from midnight of the day on which the sentence became final and the time that the offender has been imprisoned, arrested or detained for the same crime shall be credited toward the time necessary for prescription. Excepted are cases in which constitutional guarantees have been violated by public officials or employees, in accordance with the provisions of the Political Constitution. Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, PRESCRIPTION OF THE ACTION AND THE SENTENCE, 26-may-1955

Judicial Gazette, PRESCRIPTION OF CRIMINAL ACTION, 24-feb-1959

Judicial Gazette, PRESCRIPTION OF THE SENTENCE, 30-jul-1965

###### Art. 108.- Both the prescription of the action and that of the sentence are interrupted by the fact that the offender commits another offense that merits an equal or greater sentence, before the time for prescription expires.

###### Art. 109.- Police action and penalty prescribe within the periods indicated in Book III of this Code.

###### Art. 110.- Every person sentenced to imprisonment whose sentence has prescribed shall, in fact and for ten years, be subject to special surveillance by the authorities, and may not reside in the place where the crime was committed, if the aggrieved party or their relatives live there.

###### Art. 111.- Fines and special confiscation penalties shall prescribe within the periods established for the prescription of the principal sentences; and civil sentences imposed for an offense shall prescribe according to the rules of the Civil Code.

When only a fine or special confiscation has been imposed, it shall prescribe in one year. Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK IV), Arts. 2392, 2393, 2394, 2395, 2396, 2397

###### Art. 112.- Prescription shall run or be interrupted separately for each of the participants in a crime.

###### Art. 113.- By the pardon of the offended party, the sentence ceases in the case of the offenses of adultery and slanderous and non-slanderous serious injury.

If there are several participants, the pardon in favor of one of them benefits the others. Note: Adultery as a crime was abolished by the final article of the Code of Criminal Procedure, Law No. 143, published in Official Registry 511 of June 10,

###### Art. 114.- Prescription may be declared at the request of a party, or ex officio, necessarily, when the conditions required in this Code are met.

###### Art. 114-A.- Persons who have remained detained without having received an order of dismissal or order opening the trial for a time equal to or greater than one-third of that established by the Criminal Code as the maximum sentence for the crime for which they are being prosecuted, shall be immediately released by the judge hearing the case.

Likewise, persons who have remained detained without having received a sentence, for a time equal to or greater than half of that established by the Criminal Code as the maximum sentence for the crime for which they are being prosecuted, shall be immediately released by the criminal court hearing the case.

Excluded from these provisions are those being prosecuted for crimes punished by the Law on Narcotic and Psychotropic Substances.

Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Official Registry Supplement 22 of September 9, 1992.

Note: Declare the unconstitutionality on substantive grounds and suspend the effects of the last sentence of this Article, which reads: "Excluded from these provisions are those being prosecuted for crimes punished by the Law on Narcotic and Psychotropic Substances." Given by Constitutional Court Resolution No. 119, published in Official Registry Supplement 222 of December 24, 1997.

Note: Article 32 of the Constitutional Control Law, published in Official Registry 99 of July 2, 1997, establishes the Habeas Corpus remedy before the Mayor of the Canton, to enforce the right of the detainee regulated by this Article added to the Code, with an appeal to the Constitutional Court from the Mayor's denial.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 114-B.- In either case, the director of the social rehabilitation center where the detainee is located shall notify, on the day following that on which the periods indicated in the previous article are completed, the judge or court of the case of said circumstance, so that they may order the immediate release of the detainee.

In the event that the release order issued by the judge or court is not received within twenty-four hours following the notice given to them, the director of the rehabilitation center shall immediately release the detainee, which shall be communicated in writing to the criminal judge or court and to the President of the Superior Court of the district.

Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Official Registry Supplement 22 of September 9, 1992.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 114-C.- The Supreme Court of Justice, in accordance with budgetary provisions, within a period of six months from the effective date of this Law, based on the statistics and resources available to it, shall create, in the judicial districts where necessary, as many criminal courts and tribunals as are required so that each of those offices has to process at most four hundred cases annually.

Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Official Registry Supplement 22 of September 9, 1992.

###### Art. 114-D.- Judges and members of criminal courts who do not process the cases in their charge within the periods established in articles 231, 251, 260, 271 and 324 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, shall be immediately sanctioned by the President of the Superior Court of Justice with a fine equal to fifty percent of the value of the minimum wage for workers in general for each of the first three times they incur such delay and with removal from office the next time, being disqualified from returning for a period of five years. These sanctions shall be imposed on the same day that the President of the Superior Court becomes aware of the delay, under their personal and pecuniary responsibility. Note: Article added by Law No. 4, published in Official Registry Supplement 22 of September 9, 1992.

#### Title V

Common Rules for the Application of Penalties in Functional Offenses Committed by Military or Police Officers

Note: Title added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (114.3).- Military and police officer.- Permanent personnel on active duty listed in the registers of the Armed Forces, reservists incorporated into active service, and citizens who perform voluntary military service are considered military officers.

A person who has acquired the police profession and is on active service is considered a police officer.

Under no circumstances may civilian citizens be tried under these rules.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (114.4).- Military or police functional offenses.- Military or police functional offenses are actions or omissions typified in this Code, committed by a military or police officer on active duty, that are in direct, concrete, proximate and specific relation to their function and legal position according to the mission established in the Constitution and other applicable laws, which affect persons, property or operations of the Armed Forces or the National Police. Functional offenses may be committed in peacetime or during international or non-international armed conflict.

Military or police officers shall be tried and sanctioned with strict observance of constitutional rights and guarantees.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ...
