(114.5).- Joint operations of military and police officers.- When, due to reasons of non-international or international conflict, states of exception or any other exceptional situation duly established by law, military and police services operate jointly and a functional offense is committed, the penalty corresponding to the function or service ordinarily responsible for responding in the situation shall apply.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (114.6).- Subsidiary liability of the State.- For purposes of the subsidiary and civil liability of the State for functional offenses committed by military or police officers, the general provisions provided in the Constitution and the law shall apply.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (114.7).- Due obedience.- The commanders of the Armed Forces and the National Police shall be responsible for the orders they issue. Obedience to superior orders shall not exempt those who execute them from responsibility. No subordinate may evade their criminal responsibility by obedience given to their superior in the perpetration of a crime.

Every superior is imputable for the responsibility for the orders they issue and the consequences of failure to fulfill their duties.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (114.8).- Imprescriptibility.- The actions and penalties provided for the crimes of genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes, forced disappearance, extrajudicial execution, torture, or crimes of aggression against a State are imprescriptible. Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

SECOND BOOK

OF OFFENSES IN PARTICULAR

#### TITLE I

OF OFFENSES AGAINST STATE SECURITY

##### CHAPTER I

Of Offenses that Compromise the External Security of the Republic

###### Art. 115.- Anyone who, within the territory of the Republic, conspires against its external

security, inducing a foreign power to declare war on Ecuador, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment, from twelve to sixteen years, subjected to special surveillance by the authority, for ten years, and disqualified for the same period from exercising the rights of citizenship.

If the machinations were not followed by the outbreak of hostilities, the offender shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

These penalties shall apply to Ecuadorians, even if the machinations to declare war on the Republic took place outside its territory.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders a return to the previous legal text. Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 5, 6, 83

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 255

###### Art. 116.- Ecuadorians who, under enemy flag, take up arms against the

Republic, shall be punished with the same penalties established in the preceding article.


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > Libro II de este Código.

###### Art. 117.- Shall incur the same penalties:

1o.- Ecuadorians who facilitate the entry or passage of enemies of the Republic into the territory of the State;

2o.- Ecuadorians who have delivered to them cities, fortresses, squares, ports, forces, warehouses, arsenals, military plans or designs, ships, vessels, or aircraft belonging to Ecuador;

3o.- Ecuadorians who provide to an enemy power assistance of soldiers, men, guides, money, provisions, horses or vehicles, arms, ammunition or other objects known to be useful to the enemy;

4o.- Ecuadorians who have favored the progress of enemy arms in the Republic, against Ecuadorian land, sea or air forces, by corrupting the loyalty of officers, soldiers, sailors, or other citizens; or by giving notice concerning the number, state or strategic movements of Ecuadorian forces; or by directing, as pilots, the enemy army, air forces or navy; or by intentionally giving false direction or false news to the Armed Forces of the Republic;

5o.- Ecuadorians who have concealed or caused to be concealed enemy spies or soldiers, knowing them as such;

6o.- Any Ecuadorian who, officially entrusted or instructed, by reason of his employment or office, with measures taken against the enemy, with the secrecy of a negotiation, or of an expedition, has maliciously revealed it to an enemy power or its agents;

7o.- Ecuadorians who, for the purpose of favoring the enemy, destroy or set fire to warehouses, depots, arms, ammunition, ships, aircraft, fortresses, crops or other objects that the forces of the Republic could make use of;

8o.- Ecuadorians who prevent the troops of the Republic, in time of international war, from receiving assistance of funds, arms, war and food ammunition, equipment, vessels or aircraft, or plans, instructions or news convenient for the better progress of the war; and, 9o.- Ecuadorians who deliver to a bordering power or its agents, maps or documents, proof of the dominion of the Republic, over disputed border territories.

In the cases of numbers 2o. and 9o. of this article, if the malicious delivery of plans, military designs, maps or documents has been made to a power other than the enemy, the penalty of eight to twelve years of major imprisonment shall be imposed on the guilty party.

If said plans, designs, maps or documents are delivered to foreign powers or their agents, by a culpable act of an Ecuadorian, the penalty shall be one to five years of imprisonment.

If the revelation contemplated in number 6o of this article, made maliciously, is carried out to a non-enemy power, it shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and if it is culpable, with imprisonment of one to five years.

###### Art. 118.- Conspiracy to commit any of the infractions detailed in the three

preceding articles, shall be punished with eight to twelve years of major imprisonment, in the event that some act has been undertaken to prepare the execution of said infractions; and in the contrary case the penalty shall be four to eight years of the same imprisonment.

###### Art. 119.- The penalties indicated in the four preceding articles shall also apply if

the mentioned infractions are committed against a nation allied with Ecuador.

###### Art. 120.

If the acts mentioned in Arts. 115, 117, 118 and 119, are committed by foreigners, in the territory of the Republic, the penalties established in said articles shall be applied to them.

The foreigner convicted of espionage shall be punished with the penalty indicated in Art.

###### 115. Art. 121.- Any individual who has maintained with subjects of another nation a

correspondence that, without having in mind any of the infractions determined in Arts. 115 and 117, has nevertheless had as a result the provision to the enemies of Ecuador or its allies acting against the common enemy, of instructions detrimental to their military situation, shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

###### Art. 122.- In every conviction sentence for treason to the Homeland, there shall be imposed the

obligation to compensate the nation for the damages and losses caused by the perpetration of the crime being punished.

The sentence referred to in the preceding paragraph carries with it the loss of Ecuadorian nationality.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 8

##### CHAPTER II

Of crimes that compromise the peace and dignity of the State

###### Art. 123.

Whoever maliciously violates a truce or armistice entered into with the enemy, after it has been duly published; or violates, in the same manner, any treaty in force between Ecuador and another nation, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year. Jurisprudence:

Judicial Gazette, WOUNDS, 08-May-1906

###### Art. 124.

Whoever commits hostilities against any foreign power or its subjects, without knowledge or authorization of the Government of the Republic, if said hostilities occasion a declaration of war or reprisals, shall be punished with eight to twelve years of major imprisonment.

If the hostilities committed are such that they may produce reprisals or a declaration of war, even if this effect does not follow, the author of said hostilities shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

###### Art. 125.- Whoever improperly

draws plans of fortifications, ships, aircraft, establishments, roads or other military works, or enters with such purpose, clandestinely or deceitfully, into such places, when access to them has been prohibited to the public, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

###### Art. 126.- Anyone who in the territory of Ecuador attacks the life, the

immunity or the personal freedom of the Head of a foreign State, shall be punished: in case of an attack on life, with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and in other cases, with major imprisonment of four to eight years. If the act results in the death of the Head of the foreign State, the guilty party shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Anyone who in the territory of Ecuador offends the honor or prestige of the Head of a foreign State visiting the country, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree w/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.

###### Art. 127.

The provisions of the preceding article shall apply to the acts provided for therein, when they are committed against representatives of foreign States accredited in Ecuador, in their capacity as heads of diplomatic mission.

###### Art. 128.- Whoever publicly, and outside the cases provided for in this Code, incites or

foments by any means separatism, or whoever offends or vilifies public institutions or the Public Force, whoever commits any mockery or contempt, with words or actions, against the Flag, the Coat of Arms or the National Anthem, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven United States of North America dollars.

August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 2

###### Art. 129.- Whoever illegally prevents the free transit of vehicles, persons or

goods on the public roads of the country, shall be sanctioned with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven United States of North America dollars.

August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO III

Crimes against the Internal Security of the State

###### Art. 130.

Whoever in any form or by any means rises up against the Government, with the purpose of disregarding the Constitution of the Republic, deposing the constituted Government, preventing the meeting of Congress or dissolving it, or provoking civil war, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years.

The act exists from the moment there is a punishable attempt.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 4, 424

###### Art. 131.- Conspiracy aimed at achieving any of the purposes mentioned in

the preceding article, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.

The guilty party shall, in addition, be subjected to surveillance by the authority for a time equal to that of the sentence.

###### Art. 132.- Whoever verbally or in writing attacks in a subversive manner the Constitution

or the laws of the Republic, or incites their non-observance, shall be punished with six months to three years of imprisonment.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 133.- The authors of pastoral lessons, preachings or sermons, whatever the

form in which they are given to the people, if they are aimed at discrediting the authority, presenting it as contrary to the dogmas, or to the discipline, or to the religious interests of any church or cult, accepted or tolerated in the Republic, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 134.- If the author of the pastoral lessons, preachings or sermons referred to in

the preceding article, intends with them to instill disobedience to the Constitution, or to the laws, or to the orders of the authority, the penalty shall be one to five years of imprisonment. If the purpose intended by the author is to incite the people to rebellion, or to arm one part of the citizens against the other, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years.

In this case, if the rebellion or civil war takes place, the person guilty of having provoked them shall suffer the penalty of minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 135.- Those who promote discord among the citizens, arming or inciting to

arm themselves against one another, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years, even if they do not intend, in any way, to alter the constitutional order.

Conspiracy to perpetrate these offenses, if it has been followed by some preparatory act, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years.

###### Art. 136.- If the attack has as its purpose to bring devastation, carnage or pillage to

one or many places, it shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years. Conspiracy to execute such attacks, if it has been followed by some preparatory act, shall be punished with four to eight years of major imprisonment.

###### Art. 137.- Those who, armed and

organized militarily, alter by force the constitutional order, disregarding the Government, the National Congress, or the Constitution of the Republic itself, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of six to nine years. Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 426

###### Art. 138.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years:

1o.- Those who have taken command of an army corps, a troop, a warship, an aircraft, a square, a guard post, a port, a city, without right or legitimate motive;

2o.- Those who have retained any military command, against the order of the Government; and, 3o.- The commanders who have assembled their army or troop after having knowledge that the order to discharge that force has been issued.

###### Art. 139.

Any individual who, whether to seize public funds; or to invade properties, squares, cities, fortresses, guard posts, warehouses, arsenals, ports, ships, vessels or aircraft belonging to the State; or to attack or resist the public force acting against the authors of these crimes, has placed himself at the head of armed factions or has exercised in them any function or command, shall be punished with the maximum penalty indicated in Art. 137.

If these factions have had as their purpose to plunder and divide among themselves public or national properties, or those of a generality of citizens, or to attack or resist the public force pursuing the authors of these crimes, those who have placed themselves at the head of those factions, or have exercised in them any employment or command, shall also be punished with the aforementioned penalty.

###### Art. 140.- The penalties established in the two paragraphs of the preceding article shall be applicable

to those who have directed the association, raised or caused to raise, organized or caused to organize the factions.

###### Art. 141.

In the event that one of the crimes mentioned in Art. 130 has been committed by a faction, the penalties indicated by that article shall be applied to all individuals who form part of the faction and who have been apprehended at the place of the seditious gathering.

###### Art. 142.- Except in the case where the seditious gathering has had as its purpose or as

result one of the crimes enumerated in Art. 130, individuals who form part of such factions, without exercising in them any command or employment, and who have been apprehended in the same place, shall be punished with the penalty immediately lower than that which should be imposed on the directors or commanders of said factions.

###### Art. 143.- Those who, having been part of a faction, without exercising

in it employment or command, have separated spontaneously, or at the first admonishment of the authority, shall not be punished.

###### Art. 144.- In every conviction sentence for the offenses determined in this

chapter, the obligation to compensate for the damages and losses caused to the Treasury, with the perpetration of the punished acts, shall also be imposed.

###### Art. 145.- Conspirators who reveal to the authority the

existence of the conspiracy, provided that no punishable preparatory act has been executed, are exempt from penalty.

###### Art. 146.- Whoever incites rebellion or indiscipline of the Public Force, shall be punished

with imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of seventeen to forty-four dollars of the United States of North America.

If as a consequence of the incitement a conflict results in which injuries to persons are produced, the penalty shall be minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America; and if the death of one or more persons is produced, the penalty shall be six to nine years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

August 7, 2002 .

###### Art. 147.

Whoever promotes, directs or participates in organizations of guerrillas, commandos, combat groups or terrorist groups or cells, intended to subvert public order, replace the Public Force, attack it or interfere with its normal performance, shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

If these activities are carried out with weapons, or obeying foreign instructions, or with the intervention, support or economic assistance from abroad, the penalty shall be eight to twelve years of ordinary major imprisonment and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Two paragraphs added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree w/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders to return to the previous legal text. August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 148.- Whoever disseminates by any means or sends abroad propaganda,

news or false information, that is intended to alter public order or that affects national honor, shall be sanctioned with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America. August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 149.- Whoever establishes or maintains deposits of weapons or ammunition for military

or police use, and of any other similar type, without legal authorization from competent authority, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of one thousand to five thousand dollars of the United States of America.

August 7, 2002.

Note: Article substituted by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 150.- The existence of three or more of said weapons, whatever their model or class,

even when they are in disassembled pieces, is deemed a deposit.

###### Art. 151.- Whoever has introduced into the country money or securities with subversive

purposes or to alter public order, shall be sanctioned with imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of seventeen to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, and the money or securities shall be confiscated for the benefit of National Defense.

Note: Article amended by Art. 10 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 152.- Whoever facilitates the entry into the country, the permanence or concealment

therein or the evasion of foreign subversive agents, knowing their condition, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years and a fine of twenty-six to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 11 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 153.- Whoever promotes, directs or organizes parades or public demonstrations

in streets, squares or other open places, provided they are carried out without written permission from competent authority, in which the purpose of the meeting, the site, day and time at which it is to take place are determined, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three months and a fine of nine to twenty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

Directors, promoters and organizers, those who appear as such, by the speeches they deliver, by the printed materials they have published or distributed, by the words of command they pronounce, by the insignia they display or by the initial voluntary contribution to the funds of the parade or demonstration or by any other significant fact, shall also be punished.

The penalty shall be three to six months of imprisonment and a fine of seventeen to thirty-five dollars of the United States of North America, when the parade or demonstration is done against the prohibition issued by competent authority.

Note: Article amended by Art. 12 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Cross-references:

ORGANIC ELECTORAL LAW, CODE OF DEMOCRACY, Arts. 288

###### Art. 154.

Those who attend the demonstration referred to in the preceding article, carrying weapons, shall be sanctioned with deprivation of liberty of three months to one year and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 13 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 155.- Those who, with the purpose of altering public order, invade buildings,

facilities or public or private lands, or those who by committing such acts with the same proposed purposes seize property of others, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of forty-four to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 14 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO IV

Of the crimes of sabotage and terrorism

###### Art. 156.

Physicians, nurses, pharmacists, practitioners, employees of health houses or owners of pharmacies or drugstores who, disobeying orders from competent authority, paralyze services or abstain from providing their collaboration to those who need them, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of thirty-five to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.

The maximum penalties provided in this article shall be applied to members of professional organizations who have incited the commission of such acts, if these have been consummated.

Note: Article amended by Art. 15 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

See CRIME OF INTERRUPTING OR PARALYZING PUBLIC SERVICES, Judicial

Gazette. Year I. Series XVII. No. 5. Page 1. (Quito, March 28, 2001).

###### Art. 157.- Whoever removes, conceals or renders useless on the occasion of a fire, flood,

shipwreck or other calamity, any material object or other means intended for relief, rescue or to combat the danger, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of nine to twenty-six dollars of the United States of North America. The same penalty shall be incurred by whoever hinders the service provided for such purpose. If persons are injured as a result of the act, the penalty shall be six to nine years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to two hundred sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America; and if the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of two hundred sixty-two to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree w/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders to return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.

Note: Article amended by Art. 16 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 158.- Shall be punished with major ordinary imprisonment of eight to twelve years and a fine of

eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five United States dollars, whoever, outside the cases contemplated in this Code, destroys, deteriorates, renders useless, interrupts or paralyzes public services, industrial or manufacturing facilities, commercial centers, ports, canals, reservoirs, mines, powder magazines, vehicles or any other element of transport, public or private installations of electrical energy, potable water, gas or other similar ones, or installations of radio, telephone, telegraph, television or any other transmission system; deposits of merchandise, explosives, lubricants, fuels, raw materials destined for national production or consumption, or any other type of similar supply, with the purpose of producing collective alarm.

If, as a consequence of the act, injuries to persons are produced, the penalty shall be the maximum indicated in the preceding paragraph; and if the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be sixteen to twenty-five years of special major imprisonment, and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven United States dollars.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 17 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 326

###### Art. 159.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of seventeen to forty

-four United States dollars, whoever, outside the cases contemplated in this Code, impedes, disorganizes or disturbs the collection, production, transport, storage or distribution of raw materials, manufactured or extracted products, machinery or any other means necessary for production, with the purpose of producing collective alarm.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 18 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 160.- Whoever with the purpose of committing crimes against the common security of persons

or property, manufactures, supplies, acquires, steals, throws, uses, or introduces into the country weapons, ammunition or explosive bombs, explosive, asphyxiating or toxic materials, or substances or materials destined for their preparation, shall be punished with major ordinary imprisonment of 4 to 8 years and a fine of four hundred forty-two to eight hundred eighty-four United States dollars. If, by effects of the indicated acts, injuries to persons are produced, the maximum of the penalty indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed; and, if one or more persons result dead, the sanction shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of eight hundred eighty-four to one thousand seven hundred sixty-seven United States dollars.

If the criminal acts affect exclusively property, in addition to the penalty indicated in the first paragraph, the perpetrator shall be sentenced to compensation for damages and losses caused.

Nota: Artículo sustituido por Decreto Supremo No. 1273, publicado en Registro Oficial 705 de 19 de Diciembre de 1974 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo No. 1273 reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior y sustituye, además el inciso primero dado por Decreto Supremo No. 1273.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 19 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 31, publicada en Registro Oficial 231 de 17 de Marzo del 2006 .

###### Art. 160-A.- Those who, individually or forming associations, such as guerrillas,

organizations, gangs, commandos, terrorist groups, armed mobs or some other similar form, armed or not, pretexting patriotic, social, economic, political, religious, revolutionary, vindicatory, proselytizing, racial, localist, regional purposes, etc., commit crimes against the common security of persons or human groups of any kind or their property: whether by assaulting, forcing or destroying buildings, banks, warehouses, storage facilities, markets, offices, etc., whether by breaking into or invading domiciles, residences, schools, institutes, hospitals, clinics, convents, installations of the public force, military, police or paramilitary, etc., whether by stealing or seizing property or assets of any nature and amount; whether by kidnapping persons, vehicles, ships or airplanes to demand ransom, pressure and demand the change of laws or legally issued orders and provisions or require competent authorities to release persons prosecuted or sentenced for common or political crimes, etc.; whether by occupying by force through threat or intimidation, places or public or private services of any nature and type; whether by raising barricades, parapets, trenches, obstacles, etc. with the purpose of confronting the public force in support of their intentions, plans, theses or proclamations; whether by attacking, in any form, against the community, its property and services, shall be punished with major ordinary imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of one thousand seven hundred sixty-seven to four thousand four hundred eighteen United States dollars.

If by the enumerated criminal acts, injuries to persons are produced, the maximum of the penalty indicated in the preceding paragraph shall be imposed on their perpetrators and, if the death of one or more persons is produced, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years and a fine of four thousand four hundred eighteen to eight thousand eight hundred thirty-five United States dollars.

If the acts referred to in the first paragraph of this article, affect only property, in addition to the sanction imposed therein, the perpetrator or perpetrators shall be sentenced to compensation for damages and losses they have caused.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 1273, publicado en Registro Oficial 705 de 19 de Diciembre de 1974 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 20 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 161.- Whoever unjustifiably enters premises whose access to the

public or to private individuals has been prohibited, or bases, ships, aircraft, transport vehicles, barracks, military or police factories or depots, or, in general, security zones determined by the competent authority, shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to two years and a fine of forty-four to eighty-seven dollars of the United States of North America

If the act results in injuries to persons, the penalty shall be ordinary minor imprisonment from three to six years and a fine of one hundred seventy-five to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, and if the death of one or more persons occurs, the penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment from twelve to sixteen years and a fine of four hundred thirty-seven to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 21 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 162.- Private individuals who, without the necessary permit and without due explanation,

carry weapons of military or police use and of any other similar type, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to five years and a fine of nine to forty-four dollars of the United States of America.

Repeated willful commission of this type of conduct shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment from three to six years.

It is the responsibility of the Ministry of National Defense, through the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, to issue permits to carry weapons; this power may be delegated in accordance with the regulations on the matter.

This obligation extends to weapons used in industries and trades. Duly authorized military and Police authorities are obliged to seize and remit, after preparing the corresponding report of the action taken, to the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, any weapon or ammunition of domestic or foreign origin that does not have legally granted permits.

All seized weapons shall be registered in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces and if the owner does not justify their origin within a period of thirty days, they shall be delivered to the weapons depots of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces to be destroyed.

Seized weapons that serve as evidence of the commission of criminal offenses shall be maintained as such under the custody of the Judicial Police and once the respective criminal trial is concluded, they shall be registered in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces and subsequently delivered to the competent authority.

In the Provincial Police Commands throughout the country, a register shall be kept in which the type, caliber and characteristics of the weapon or weapons seized and sent to the corresponding military authority are recorded.

Manufacturers of weapons, explosives and ammunition of any type must register their factories or workshops in the Logistics Directorate of the Joint Command of the Armed Forces, and inform this entity monthly about the quantity, type, caliber and characteristics of the weapons produced and the code assigned to each of them, which must also be registered without exception.

Note: Article amended by Art. 22 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Register 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 163.- Whoever provides or receives military instruction without permission from the competent

authority shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to one year and a fine of thirty-five to seventy dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 23 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 164.

Terrorist aggression, provided that the act does not constitute a more serious crime, perpetrated against officials or employees of public institutions or against their property, shall be punished with three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment and a fine of eighty-seven to one hundred seventy-five dollars of the United States of North America.

If injuries to persons are caused, the penalty shall be minor imprisonment from six to nine years and a fine of eighty-seven to four hundred thirty-seven dollars of the United States of North America. If the death of one or more persons results, the penalty shall be sixteen to twenty-five years of special major imprisonment and a fine of two hundred sixty-two to eight hundred seventy-three dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

Note: Article amended by Art. 24 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 165.- Outside the cases contemplated in this Code, terrorist threat, by

any means it is made, shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to one year and a fine of nine to forty-four dollars of the United States of North America. Note: Article amended by Art. 25 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 166.- When the crimes provided for in this Chapter are committed by naturalized foreigners

in Ecuador, in addition to the imposed penalty, the naturalization certificate shall be cancelled and they shall be expelled from the country, after the sanction imposed on them has been served.

Art. ...- Those who willfully, individually or collectively, directly or indirectly, by any means, provide, offer or collect funds or assets to finance in whole or in part, with lawful or unlawful resources, the commission of the crimes defined in this Chapter, shall be punished with the same penalties established for the financed crime.

The crime defined in this article shall be investigated, prosecuted, ruled or sentenced by the court or competent authority as an autonomous crime from other crimes defined in this Chapter, committed within or outside the country.

Note: Article added by Law No. 00, published in Official Register Supplement 352 of December 30, 2010.

#### TITULO II

OF CRIMES AGAINST CONSTITUTIONAL GUARANTEES

AND RACIAL EQUALITY

Note: Heading of Title II, amended by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in

Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

##### CAPITULO I

Of crimes relating to the exercise of suffrage

###### Art. 167.

Those who, by means of riots, violence or threats, have prevented one or more citizens from exercising their political rights, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America. Note: Article amended by Art. 26 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

ORGANIC ELECTORAL LAW, DEMOCRACY CODE, Arts. 122, 290

###### Art. 168.- Members of Electoral Boards and other officials or

corporations that, by Law, are charged with verifying the scrutiny of an election, and who steal or falsify ballots, or annul an election partially or totally, against express laws, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years and deprivation of political rights for two years.

###### Art. 169.- If the offenses provided for in the two previous articles have been committed

by prior agreement to extend and execute them throughout the Republic, or in several cantons, the penalty shall be ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years and interdiction of political rights for two years.

###### Art. 170.

Any individual who is caught stealing ballots from voters, by means of cunning or violence, or fraudulently substituting another ballot for the one held by the voter, or who presents himself to vote with a false name, or who votes in two or more parishes, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and with one year of interdiction of political rights.

Concordancias:

LEY ORGANICA ELECTORAL, CODIGO DE LA DEMOCRACIA, Arts. 288

###### Art. 171.- Those who disturb a popular election alleging religious motives, whether

in favor of their candidates, recommending them; or discrediting opposing candidates, shall be punished with imprisonment of thirty to ninety days.

Concordancias:

LEY ORGANICA ELECTORAL, CODIGO DE LA DEMOCRACIA, Arts. 49, 50

###### Art. 172.- Anyone who has received something in exchange for his vote, or who has given or

promised something for the vote of another, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and interdiction of political rights for an equal period of time.

##### CAPITULO II

Crimes against freedom of conscience and thought

###### Art. 173.- Those who, employing violence or threats, prevent one or more individuals

from exercising any religion permitted or tolerated in the Republic, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 174.- Private individuals or ministers of a religion who provoke riots or tumults

against the followers of another religion, whether by word or in writing, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 27 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 175.

If the offenders exercise ecclesiastical, political, civil or military authority, the penalty shall be six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of sixteen to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 28 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 176.- Those who have impeded, delayed, or interrupted the exercise of a religion, or its public ceremonies, not expressly prohibited by Law, by means of disorder or tumult promoted in the place intended for such religion, but without committing violence or uttering threats against anyone, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 29 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 177.- Those who offend the corpse of a person, with actions, words,

emblems or writings, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

The civil or ecclesiastical authority that denies burial to a corpse, in public cemeteries, alleging religious motives, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Those who place on the tomb of a person injurious emblems or writings, shall be punished with imprisonment of thirty to ninety days and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 30 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 178.- The authority that, by arbitrary or violent means, restricts the power to

freely express thought, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and interdiction of political rights for a period of time equal to that of the sentence.
Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 179.- Anyone who impedes or hinders the free circulation of a book, newspaper or

printed material, that are not anonymous, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 16

##### CAPITULO III

Crimes against individual freedom

###### Art. 180.- Public employees, depositaries and agents of the authority or of the

public force who, illegally and arbitrarily, have arrested or caused to arrest, detained or caused to detain one or more persons, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of twelve to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America. They may, in addition, be sentenced to interdiction of citizenship rights for two to three years.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 31 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 166

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PRISION ARBITRARIA, 01-jun-2001

Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO, 30-ene-2009

###### Art. 181.- The authority that orders the confinement of a person in violation of

constitutional precepts, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 182.- The official who retains a detainee or prisoner whose freedom he should have decreed or executed shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years; and he who unduly prolongs the detention of a person, without placing him at the disposal of the competent judge.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 89

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 165

###### Art. 183.- Those who, without order from the constituted authorities and outside the cases in which the Law and regulations permit or order the arrest or detention of private individuals, have arrested or caused to arrest, detained or caused to detain any person, provided that this arrest or detention does not constitute a crime more severely punished, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 32 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO ARBITRARIO, 10-abr-1889

Gaceta Judicial, ARRESTO ILEGAL Y ARBITRARIO, 20-dic-1920

###### Art. 184.- The imprisonment shall be six months to three years and the fine six to sixteen dollars

of the United States of North America, if the illegal and arbitrary detention has lasted more than ten days.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 33 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 185.- If the illegal and arbitrary detention has lasted more than one month, the guilty party

shall be punished with imprisonment of one to four years and a fine of sixteen to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 34 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

###### Art. 186.- The penalty of minor imprisonment from three to six years shall apply if the arrest had

been executed with a false order from public authority, or with the uniform or under the name of one of its agents, or if the arrested or detained person had been threatened with death.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 77

###### Art. 187.

When the arrested or detained person has suffered bodily torments, the guilty party shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

The penalty shall be minor imprisonment from six to nine years if the torments resulted in any of the permanent injuries detailed in the chapter on injuries. If the torments caused death, the guilty party shall be punished with special major imprisonment from sixteen to twenty-five years.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 188.- The crime of kidnapping is committed by taking possession of another person by means of

violence, threats, seduction or deception, whether to sell them or place them against their will in the service of another, or to obtain any benefit, or to force them to pay ransom or deliver a movable thing, or extend, deliver or sign a document that produces or may produce legal effects, or to force them to do or omit doing something, or to force a third party to execute one of the indicated acts aimed at the release of the kidnapped person. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PLAGIO, 31-may-1977

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PLAGIO O TENTATIVA, 04-abr-1983

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PLAGIO, 19-feb-1998

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE PLAGIO, 27-abr-1998

###### Art. 189.- Kidnapping shall be punished with the penalties indicated in the following

numbers:

1.- With imprisonment from six months to two years, if the victim is returned to freedom spontaneously by the kidnapper, before judicial proceedings are initiated, without having suffered mistreatment, nor any of the conditioning acts determined in the previous article being carried out;

2.- With imprisonment from one to three years, if the return of freedom, with the conditions of the preceding number, has been carried out after prosecution has begun, the kidnapper not being detained or imprisoned;

3.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from three to six years, if the release takes place under the terms of number 2 of this article, the kidnapper being detained or imprisoned; 3-A.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from three to six years to whoever, by means of threats, violence, seduction, deception or other illegitimate means, takes possession of a motor vehicle; holding against their will its driver and/or its occupants, to ensure the commission of the crime;

3-B.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years, when the offender, in the case and circumstances established in the previous numeral, starts the vehicle or forces the driver or another of the detained persons to do so, in order to, in the company of these, although without their participation, use the motor vehicle to commit or attempt to commit other crimes.

4.- With ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years, if in the case of number 1, the victim has suffered mistreatment;

5.- With extraordinary minor imprisonment from nine to twelve years, in the case of number 2, if the victim has suffered mistreatment;

6.- With ordinary major imprisonment from eight to twelve years, in the case of number 3, if there has been such mistreatment; and, 7.- With special major imprisonment from sixteen to twenty-five years, when the victim has not regained freedom until the date of the sentence, the maximum penalty being imposed if before conviction the victim appears raped, dead or dies as a consequence of the kidnapping.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo sustituido por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Numerales 3-A y 3-B, agregados por Ley No. 21, publicada en Registro Oficial 154 de 28 de Noviembre del 2005 .

###### Art. 190.- Even if there is a final conviction sentence, in the case of number 7o

of the previous article, the penalty shall be reduced by half if the kidnapper restores the freedom of the victim.

Art. ... .- The following shall be considered aggravating circumstances of the crime of kidnapping, in addition to the circumstances indicated in article 30, when one or more of the following circumstances concur: Prolonging the deprivation of freedom of the kidnapped person for a period longer than fifteen days. Committing the infraction using a false detention order, or simulating having one or abusing authority in the case of members of the public force or judges.

Note: Article added by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001 .

CHAPTER ....

ON THE CRIME RELATING TO HUMAN TRAFFICKING

Art. ..- It constitutes the crime of human trafficking, even if there is consent from the victim, to promote, induce, participate, facilitate or favor the recruitment, transfer, harboring, reception or delivery of persons by resorting to threat, violence, deceit or any other fraudulent form, for purposes of illicit exploitation, with or without profit motive. For purposes of this infraction, exploitation is considered to be any form of forced labor or services, labor slavery, sale and/or use of persons for begging, armed conflicts or recruitment for criminal purposes.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005 .

Art. ..- Human trafficking shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years, provided that it does not constitute sexual exploitation. If the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the penalty shall be extraordinary minor imprisonment from nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005 .

Art. ..- The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment from twelve to sixteen years when one or more of the following circumstances concur in the commission of the crime established in the previous article:

- 1. That the victim is under fourteen years of age;
- 2. That, as a consequence of the crime, the victim suffers serious or permanent bodily injury, or irreversible psychological damage;
- 3. If the offender is a spouse, cohabitant or relative up to the fourth degree of consanguinity or second degree of affinity, ascendant or descendant of the victim; and,
- 4. When the offender takes advantage of the vulnerability of the victim or the victim is incapacitated to resist the aggression.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005 .

Art. ..- Whoever sells, buys or carries out any transaction, by virtue of which a person is delivered, for payment or any other means, for purposes of exploitation, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment from six to nine years. The offer for sale constitutes an attempt.

If the victim is a person under eighteen years of age, the penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment from nine to twelve years; and, if under twelve years of age, the penalty shall be twelve to sixteen years of extraordinary major imprisonment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005 .

CHAPTER ...

ON CRIMES RELATING TO ILLEGAL EXTRACTION AND TRAFFICKING OF

ORGANS

Art. ... (1).- Whoever willfully fails to comply with the obligations or violates the prohibitions provided for in articles 11, 19, 58, 69, 74, 76, 77, 78 and 79 of the Organic Law on Donation and Transplantation of Organs, Tissues and Cells; whoever falsifies or adulterates a donation and transplant protocol, a document defined in the same Law, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment from three (3) to six (6) years.

In case of non-compliance or non-observance of the obligations or prohibitions indicated above, when referring to institutions or legal entities, the criminal liability of the highest authority of the institution or legal representative of the legal entity shall be presumed.

Whoever willfully trades or traffics in organs, tissues, bodily substances or any anatomical material from human corpses shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment from nine (9) to twelve (12) years. The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever willfully selects, extracts, evaluates, prepares, obtains, stores, transports, assigns, distributes or transplants them.

The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment from twelve (12) to sixteen (16) years, if the activities referred to in the previous paragraph are carried out with organs, tissues, bodily substances or any anatomical material that come from living persons.

A penalty of extraordinary major imprisonment from twelve (12) to sixteen years shall be imposed on persons who extract or transplant any human anatomical component, in medical centers not accredited by the National Health Authority.

If the anatomical components willfully extracted or transplanted come from children or adolescents or persons with disabilities, the penalty shall be special major imprisonment from sixteen (16) to twenty-five (25) years.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 2, published in Official Registry 45 of June 23, 2005 .

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry 398 of March 4, 2011 .

Art. ... (2).- Whoever transfers organs, donors, recipients or health professionals specialized in transplants, outside their original jurisdictional borders, with the purpose of performing one or more transplants in the Republic of Ecuador, without having immigrant status in the country or the corresponding permits from the National Health Authority, shall be punished with the penalty of extraordinary major imprisonment from twelve

(12) to sixteen (16) years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry 398 of March 4, 2011 .

CHAPTER...

ON HATE CRIMES

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009 .

Art. ...- Whoever publicly or through any means suitable for public dissemination incites hatred, contempt, or any form of moral or physical violence against one or more persons on the basis of their skin color, race, sex, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years. Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009 .

Art. ...- Whoever commits acts of moral or physical violence of hatred or contempt against one or more persons on the basis of their skin color, race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to two years.

If any person is injured as a result of the acts of violence referred to in this article, the perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment from two to five years. If such acts of violence result in the death of a person, their perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment from twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009 .

Art. ...- Whoever, in the exercise of their professional, commercial or business activities, denies a person a service or benefit to which they are entitled, or excludes a person or denies or violates or restricts the rights enshrined in the Constitution, by reason of their skin color, race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009 .

Art. ...- Whoever, being a public servant, engages in any of the conducts provided for in this chapter or denies or delays a procedure or service to which a person is entitled, shall be sanctioned as provided in the previous article. In these cases the official shall be disqualified from performing any public office, employment or commission for up to the same period of time as the deprivation of liberty imposed.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009 .

##### CHAPTER IV

On crimes against the inviolability of the dwelling

###### Art. 191.- Employees of the administrative or judicial order, court or police officers,

commanders or agents of the public force who, acting as such, have entered the dwelling of an inhabitant against their will, outside the cases provided for and without the formalities prescribed by Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 35 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 194

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 45

###### Art. 192.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years and a fine of six to twelve dollars

of the United States of North America, whoever without order of the authority and outside the cases in which the Law allows entry into the dwelling of private individuals, against their will, has entered a house, apartment, room or dwelling inhabited by another, or its enclosed dependencies, either by means of threats or violence, or by means of breaking, scaling or lock picks.

Note: Article amended by Art. 36 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF DWELLING, 09-Apr-1907

Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF DWELLING, 06-Sep-1949

###### Art. 193.- The imprisonment shall be from six months to five years and the fine from twelve to thirty-one

dollars of the United States of North America, if the act has been committed with a false order from the public authority, or with the uniform or under the name of one of its agents or with one of the following three circumstances:

If the act has been executed at night;

If it has been executed by two or more persons; and, If the guilty parties or any of them carried weapons.

Note: Article amended by Art. 37 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 194.- Those guilty of the infractions provided for in the two preceding articles shall be

placed under the surveillance of the authority for a time equal to that of the sentence.

###### Art. 195.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to six months and a fine of five to twelve

dollars of the United States of North America, whoever has entered, without the consent of the owner or the lessee, but without violence or threats, into the places designated in Art. 192, and has been found therein during the night. Note: Article amended by Art. 38 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 196.- In the violation of dwelling, it is presumed that there is no consent of the owner

or their agent when they are not present at the act that constitutes the violation.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 355

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 32

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF PRIVATE DWELLING, 07-Oct-2003

##### CHAPTER V

On crimes against the inviolability of secrecy

###### Art. 197.- Shall be sanctioned with penalties of 2 months to one year of imprisonment, those who

intercept without judicial order, telephone conversations or those made by similar means and those who remove or open envelopes of correspondence belonging to another without express authorization.

The responsibility of whoever did so is exempted when the telephone interception or the opening of envelopes occurs by mistake, accidentally or fortuitously.

Note: Article amended by Art. 39 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Note: Articles 197 and 198 unified and replaced by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 555 of March 24, 2009.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 150, 151

###### Art. 199.- Whoever being in possession of correspondence not intended for

publication, causes it to be published, or presents it in court without judicial order, even though it has been addressed to them, shall be punished with a fine of six to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America, if the act may cause harm to third parties; unless it concerns correspondence in which obligations in favor of the holder thereof are stated, in which case it may be presented in court.

Note: Article amended by Art. 41 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 199

###### Art. 200.- The same penalty shall be incurred by whoever, without being a public employee, divulges

proceedings or procedures of which they have had knowledge and which, by Law, must remain confidential.

Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 158

###### Art. 201.

Whoever having knowledge, by reason of their state or office, employment, profession or art, of a secret whose disclosure may cause harm, reveals it without just cause, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 42 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

LABOR CODE, Arts. 45

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 36

###### Art. 202.- Those who remove letters entrusted to the mail shall be punished with imprisonment of

fifteen to sixty days, except parents, husbands or guardians who take letters from their children, spouses or wards, respectively, who are under their dependence.

Art. ...- Whoever using any electronic, computer or similar means, violates codes or security systems, to access or obtain protected information, contained in information systems; to violate the secrecy, confidentiality and reserve, or simply violate the security, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of five hundred to one thousand dollars of the United States of North America.

If the information obtained refers to national security, or to commercial or industrial secrets, the penalty shall be one to three years of imprisonment and a fine of one thousand to one thousand five hundred dollars of the United States of North America.

The disclosure or fraudulent use of the protected information, as well as of commercial or industrial secrets, shall be sanctioned with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years and a fine of two thousand to ten thousand dollars of the United States of North America.

If the disclosure or fraudulent use is carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be sanctioned with a penalty of minor imprisonment of six to nine years and a fine of two thousand to ten thousand dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Official Register Supplement 557 of April 17, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

LABOR CODE, Arts. 45, 310

Art. ...- Obtaining and unauthorized use of information.- The person or persons who obtain information about personal data to later transfer it, publish it, use it or transfer it under any title, without the authorization of its owner or owners, shall be sanctioned with a penalty of imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of one thousand to two thousand dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article added by Law No. 67, published in Official Register Supplement 557 of April 17, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

##### CHAPTER VI

On crimes relating to the declarations of the accused or their relatives

###### Art. 203.

The judge or authority who compels a person to testify against themselves, against their spouse, their ascendants, descendants, or relatives within the fourth civil degree of consanguinity or second degree of affinity, in matters that may result in criminal liability, shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years. Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 77

CIVIL CODE (PRELIMINARY TITLE), Arts. 22, 23

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 126, 143

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, PRESUMPTIONS OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY, 27-Jan-1914

###### Art. 204.- The judge or authority who extracts statements or confessions from the

persons indicated in the previous article, by means of whipping, imprisonment, threat or torture, shall be punished with imprisonment from two to five years and deprivation of citizenship rights for the same time as the sentence.

Police officers or members of the public force who commit the violation indicated in the previous paragraph shall be punished with the same penalty.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

##### CAPITULO VII

Of crimes against prisoners or detainees

###### Art. 205.- Those who issue or execute the order to torture prisoners or detainees, with

incommunication for a longer time than that established by Law, with shackles, stocks, bars, handcuffs, ropes, unhealthy dungeons, or other torture, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to five years and disqualification from political rights for the same time. Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 76, 77

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 72, 211

###### Art. 206.- Neither the insecurity of the prisons, nor the dangerousness of the detainee or prisoner, nor the

rebellious conduct of the latter, may serve as an excuse in the cases of the previous article.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

###### Art. 207.- The head of prison or other penal establishment, or whoever replaces him, who

receives any convict without testimony of a final sentence in which the penalty has been imposed, or without the constitutional order or warrant, in case of detention, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to six months.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77

CODE OF EXECUTION OF SENTENCES AND SOCIAL REHABILITATION, Arts. 38

###### Art. 208.- Judges and other employees who have detained or caused to detain a person in places

other than those determined by Law shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 43 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO VIII

Of crimes against freedom of work, association and petition

###### Art. 209.- The political, civil, ecclesiastical or military authority who demands services not

imposed by Law, or compels to work without prior stipulation, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to six months.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

LABOR CODE, Arts. 3

###### Art. 210.- Whoever exerts violence on another, or threatens him to compel him to take part in a strike or

boycott, shall be punished with imprisonment from one month to one year. The same penalty shall be suffered by the employer, entrepreneur or employee who, by himself or on behalf of someone, suspends work in whole or in part in his establishments, agencies or offices, with the purpose of imposing on his dependents modifications in the established agreements; and those who, out of solidarity, do the same in other establishments.

Cross-references:

LABOR CODE, Arts. 467, 513, 534, 536

###### Art. 211.- The same penalty shall apply to the employer, entrepreneur or employee who, by himself or on

behalf of someone, exercises coercion to compel another to intervene in any of the acts determined in the second part of the previous article, or to abandon or join a particular workers' association.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 326

LABOR CODE, Arts. 440, 510, 513, 534

###### Art. 212.- The authority who, in any manner, impedes the free exercise of the right to petition, shall be

punished with a fine from six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America and imprisonment from one to six months.

Note: Article amended by Art. 44 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 19, 66, 326

##### CAPITULO VIII-A

Of Crimes Relating to Racial Discrimination

Note: Chapter added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register

769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212-A.- Shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years:

1) Whoever, by any means, disseminates ideas based on racial superiority or racial hatred;

2) Whoever incites, in any form, to racial discrimination;

3) Whoever commits acts of violence or incites to commit them against any race, person or group of persons of any color or ethnic origin; and,

4) Whoever finances, assists or aids any kind of racist activities. If the crimes specified in this article are ordered or executed by public officials or employees, the penalty shall be imprisonment from one to five years. Note: New article inserted by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree unnumbered, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders to return to the previous legal text. Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 416

###### Art. 212-B.- If from the acts of violence referred to in numeral 3 of the

preceding article, any person is injured, the perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment from two to five years. If such acts of violence result in the death of a person, their perpetrators shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 16 years.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212-C.- Organizations as well as all propaganda and dissemination activities that promote

racial discrimination or incite to it are declared illegal and consequently prohibited in the Republic. Therefore, whoever participates in such organizations or in such activities shall be punished with imprisonment from two months to two years.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212-D.

National, regional or local authorities and public institutions are prohibited from promoting or inciting racial discrimination. For the violation of this prohibition, the aforementioned authorities, legal representatives or directors of such institutions shall be held responsible, who shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years and loss of political rights for the same time as the sentence. Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

###### Art. 212-E.- Public officials or employees who commit any of the crimes of racial

discrimination defined in this Decree shall have the special norms provided in the Political Constitution for the case of violation of the guarantees declared therein applied to them.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 3194, published in Official Register 769 of February 8, 1979.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 11, 46

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, CLASSIFICATION OF SMUGGLING BY THE EXECUTIVE, 08-May-1914

##### CHAPTER IX

Common provisions to this title

###### Art. 213.- Any other arbitrary act that violates the freedoms and rights

guaranteed by the Constitution, ordered or executed by a public employee or official, by a depositary or agent of the authority or of the public force, shall be punished with imprisonment from three to six months.

With respect to the accusation; pardon, reduction and commutation of the penalty; prescription of actions, and civil liability, the rules especially specified by the Constitution shall also apply with respect to officials or employees who violate any of the guarantees declared in the Constitution.

Note: Article substituted by Supreme Decree No. 2060, published in Official Registry 495 of December 30, 1977.

Note: Supreme Decree No. 2060, repealed by Supreme Decree No. 2124, published in Official Registry 508 of January 18, 1978.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 11, 66

###### Art. 214.- Disciplinary obedience may exempt from liability whoever has

executed an order contrary to the rights guaranteed by the Constitution, provided that such order, emanating from the respective hierarchical superior and in a matter within their competence, could not have been disobeyed by the subordinate without breach of discipline.

Once these circumstances are proven, all responsibility for the act shall fall upon the superior who issued the order to execute it.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 80, 159

###### Art. 215.- If any of the arbitrary acts mentioned in this Chapter has been

committed by means of the forged signature of a public employee, the authors of the forgery and those who, maliciously and fraudulently, have used it shall be punished with major imprisonment from four to eight years.

###### Art. 216.- Judges and other employees who, without the authorizations prescribed by the Constitution, have solicited, issued or signed a writ or judgment against the President of the Republic or the person substituting them, or against the Ministers of State, the Magistrates of the Supreme Court, or the Members of the Tribunal of Constitutional Guarantees; or an order whose purpose is to prosecute them or bring them to trial; or who have given or signed the order or mandate to apprehend or arrest them, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America and imprisonment from one to three years.

Note: "State Councilors" is replaced by "Members of the Tribunal of Constitutional Guarantees".

Provision given by Legislative Decree No. 104, published in Official Registry 315 of August 26, 1982.

Note: The name changes to "Constitutional Tribunal". Given by First Transitory Provision of the Law of Constitutional Control, Official Registry No. 99 of July 2, Note: Article amended by Art. 45 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 129, 130
