###### Art. 217.- Judges and other employees who proceed in the manner indicated in the previous article with respect to Senators and Deputies, while they enjoy immunity, shall incur the same penalties, except in the case of a flagrant crime provided for by the Constitution.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 77, 128

#### TITLE III

CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC ADMINISTRATION

##### CHAPTER I

Rebellion and attacks against officials

###### Art. 218.- Rebellion is any attack, any resistance made with violence or threats to

public employees, to depositaries or agents of the public force, to commissioners for the collection of taxes and contributions, to executors of decrees and judicial rulings, to guards of customs and collection offices, and to police agents, when they act in execution of the laws, or of the orders or regulations of the public authority.

Rebellion is, likewise, any attack, any resistance with violence or threats, by individuals admitted to hospices, not being deprived of knowledge, or by prisoners or detainees in jails and other places of correction or repression. Jurisprudence:

Judicial Gazette, CRIMES AGAINST PUBLIC ADMINISTRATION, 12-Jun-2007

###### Art. 219.- Rebellion is also any attack, any resistance made with violence or

threats to employees or agents of the telegraph service, when they transmit dispatches of the public authority.

###### Art. 220.- Rebellion committed by a single person armed with weapons shall be punished

with imprisonment from three months to two years. If it took place without weapons, with imprisonment from eight days to six months.

###### Art. 221.- If the rebellion has been committed by many persons and as a consequence of a

prior agreement, the rebels who carry weapons shall be punished with minor imprisonment from three to six years; and the others, with imprisonment from one to three years.

If the rebellion has not been the result of a prior agreement, the guilty parties who carry weapons shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to one year; and the others, with imprisonment from fifteen days to three months.

Jurisprudence:

###### Art. 222.- In case of rebellion in a gang or mob, no penalty shall be applied to

the rebels who did not exercise functions or positions in the gang, if they withdrew at the first warning from the public authority, or if they were apprehended outside the place of the rebellion, without new resistance and without weapons.

###### Art. 223.- In all cases where, for the act of rebellion, the penalty of deprivation of

liberty is applied, the guilty parties may be placed under the surveillance of the authority for up to one year, and fined six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America. The leaders of the rebellion, and those who provoked it, may be sentenced to surveillance by the authority for two years at least, and five at most.

Note: Article amended by Art. 46 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 224.- The attempt to assassinate the President of the Republic or the person

exercising the Executive Function shall be punished with major imprisonment from eight to twelve years, even if no harm is inflicted.

Note: Last paragraph added by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree without number, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 225.

The perpetrator of the same attempt against a Senator or Deputy, Minister of State, Magistrate or Judge, Governor or any other public official who exercises jurisdiction or civil or military authority, when in the actual exercise of their functions, or by reason of their ministry, shall be punished with four to eight years of major imprisonment, even if no harm is inflicted.

If the attack is committed against the life of any other public official who does not exercise jurisdiction, the perpetrator shall be punished with minor imprisonment from three to six years.

###### Art. 226.- The provocation to duel directed at the public officials referred to in the

two preceding articles shall be punished with the penalty immediately inferior to that established for the attempt to assassinate the aforementioned officials, and according to the distinctions established in the same articles.

###### Art. 227.- Whoever wounds or strikes, or physically mistreats, or commits other material violence

against the President of the Republic or against whoever acts in their stead, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

###### Art. 228.- Whoever wounds, or strikes or physically mistreats any of the officials

enumerated in Art. 225, when they are in the actual exercise, or by reason of the exercise of their functions, shall be punished with one to three years of imprisonment.

Whoever, in the same case, commits this crime against any other public official who does not exercise jurisdiction, or civil or military authority, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year.

###### Art. 229.- If the wounds, strikes or mistreatment, by their nature and according to the provisions

of this Code, merit another penalty, the penalty of the immediately higher degree shall be applied to the guilty party.

Case law:

Judicial Gazette, WOUNDS, 08-mar-1946

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF WOUNDS, 28-feb-1994

###### Art. 230.- Whoever with threats, menaces or insults, offends the President of the

Republic or whoever exercises the Executive Function, shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 47 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 231.

Whoever with threats, insults, menaces or violence, offends any of the public officials enumerated in Art. 225, when they are exercising their functions, or by reason of such exercise, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to three months and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Those who commit the infractions detailed in the preceding paragraph against another official who does not exercise jurisdiction, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree unnumbered, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders the return to the previous legal text.

Note: Article amended by Art. 48 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Case law:

Judicial Gazette, THREATS AND INSULTS AGAINST PUBLIC AUTHORITIES, 22-nov-2002

###### Art. 232.- Whoever fails to show respect to any court, corporation or public official

when in the exercise of their functions, with words, gestures or acts of contempt, or disturbs or interrupts the act in which they are engaged, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree unnumbered, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders the return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 233.- The same penalty shall be applied to whoever insults or offends any person who is

present and in the presence of the courts or public authorities.

###### Art. 234.- Those who, outside of the cases expressed in this Code, disobey the

authorities when they order something for the better public service, in matters of their respective jurisdiction and in accordance with their legal powers, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one month.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree unnumbered, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders the return to the previous legal text.

###### Art. 235.- Those who, without legitimate cause, refuse to provide the service required of them in the

profession, art or trade they practice, or in any other manner necessary for the administration of justice, or public service, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to two months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America, without prejudice to being compelled to provide the service that had been required of them.

Note: Article amended by Art. 49 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 96

CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 254, 255, 261

LABOR CODE, Arts. 514, 515, 516

##### CHAPTER II

On the usurpation of functions, titles and names

###### Art. 236.

Whoever, without legitimate title, pretends to be a civil, military or ecclesiastical public employee, government agent or commissioner, and exercises some function as such, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 50 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 237.- Whoever, having been appointed as a public official, has entered to

exercise their functions without having taken the oath that the Law provides, shall be punished with a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 51 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 238.- The public official dismissed, suspended or legally declared in

interdiction, who continues in the exercise of their functions after having been notified of the dismissal, suspension or interdiction, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

The public official who has continued exercising their functions after the period for which they were appointed shall be punished with the same penalties, except in legal cases.

Note: Article amended by Art. 52 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 9

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 367, 371

###### Art. 239.- Whoever has publicly taken a name that does not belong to them shall be

punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, or with only one of these penalties.

Note: Article amended by Art. 53 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

GENERAL LAW ON CIVIL REGISTRY IDENTIFICATION AND IDENTITY CARDS, Arts. 83, 116

###### Art. 239-A.

Whoever improperly uses military ranks, insignia, uniforms, denominations or emblems shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years. The maximum penalty shall be imposed on the head of any Department or Institution who authorizes such use to their subordinates.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 1114, published in Official Register 159 of October 5, 1972.

###### Art. 239-B.- If the improper use of military ranks, insignia, uniforms, denominations or

emblems is done for criminal purposes or as a facilitating means for the commission of a crime, the responsible party shall be sentenced to imprisonment of three to five years. If the crime has been committed under these circumstances, the offender shall be given the maximum penalty for the most serious of the crimes they have perpetrated.

Note: Article added by Supreme Decree No. 1114, published in Official Register 159 of October 5, 1972.

##### CHAPTER III

On the violation of seals and documents

###### Art. 240.- When seals placed by order of the public

authority have been broken, the guardians shall be punished, for simple negligence, with imprisonment of eight days to six months.

###### Art. 241.- Those who have intentionally broken the seals shall be punished with imprisonment

of six months to two years; and if the guilty party is the guardian themselves or the public official who ordered or executed the affixing, they shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK IV), Arts. 2128, 2129

###### Art. 242.- If the broken seals were those affixed on papers or effects of an individual

accused of a crime that carries the penalty of reclusión mayor or reclusión menor extraordinaria, or of an individual convicted to any of these penalties, the negligent guardian shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to one year.

###### Art. 243.- Whoever has intentionally broken the seals placed on papers or effects

of the nature stated in the preceding article, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years; and if the guilty party is the guardian or the public official who ordered or executed the affixing, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to five years.

###### Art. 244.- If the breaking of the seals has been committed with violence, the guilty party

shall be punished with the maximum of the penalties established for the offense.

###### Art. 245.

In the cases of Arts. 241, 242, 243 and 244, the guilty party may be sentenced, in addition, to a fine of six to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America. Note: Article amended by Art. 54 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO IV

Of the obstacles placed to the execution of public works

###### Art. 246.- Whoever has opposed, by acts of fact, the execution of public works

ordered by the competent authority, shall be punished with imprisonment from eight days to three months.

###### Art. 247.

Those who, by means of riot, violence, acts of fact or threats, have opposed the execution of said works, shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to two years.

The leaders and promoters shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to three years.

###### Art. 248.- In the cases provided for by the two preceding articles, the guilty parties may

in addition, be sentenced to a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 55 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO V

Of the violation of the duties of public officials, of the usurpation of attributions and of abuses of authority

###### Art. 249.- The public official to whom corresponds, as such, the compliance and

execution of a law, regulation or superior order that is legally communicated to him, does not comply with and execute it, or does not have it complied with and executed, as the case may be, by delay, omission or negligence, shall be punished with a fine of sixteen to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 56 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordancias:

LEY ORGANICA DE REGIMEN TRIBUTARIO INTERNO, LORTI, Arts. 109

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, FUNCIONARIOS PUBLICOS, 22-nov-1920

###### Art. 250.- The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever defers executing or having executed the

superior order, even if it is under the pretext of making representations about it, except in the following cases:

1o.- When the superior order is manifestly contrary to the Constitution; 2o.- When it is not communicated with the formalities required by the Constitution and the laws, or there is some reason to prudently doubt the authenticity of the order; 3o.- When it is a resolution obtained by deceit, or given against the Law and with prejudice to third parties; and, 4o.- When from the execution of the order result or are feared, probably, serious evils that the superior could not have foreseen.

Although in these cases the executor of the order may suspend, under his responsibility, the execution, to make representations to the one who has given it, he shall be punished with the respective penalties, in accordance with the two preceding articles, if he does not adduce in the same representation, the founded motives that he alleges.

If the superior, after becoming aware of the representation, repeats the order, the subordinate must comply with and execute it immediately, except in the sole case of it being manifestly contrary to the Constitution and the laws.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 11, 80, 171

###### Art. 251.- When, colluding two or more public officials or bodies depositary

of some part of the public authority, whether in a meeting, or by deputation, or correspondence between them, they agree on some measure to prevent, suspend or hinder the execution of a Law, regulation or superior order, they shall be punished with imprisonment from six months to five years.

If the agreement has taken place between the civil authorities and the military corps or their chiefs, those who have provoked it shall be punished with three to six years of reclusión menor; and the others, with three to five years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 252.- In case the civil authorities have formed with the military corps

or their chiefs, a conspiracy threatening the security of the State, the provocateurs shall be punished with reclusión menor extraordinaria of nine to twelve years, and the others, with six to nine years of the same reclusión.

###### Art. 253.- Shall be punished with imprisonment from one month to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen

dollars of the United States of North America the officials who, as a consequence of the agreement, have resigned with the purpose of preventing or suspending, either the administration of justice, or the fulfillment of a legitimate service.

Note: Article amended by Art. 57 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 254.- The public employee who dictates regulations or provisions, exceeding

his attributions, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 58 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE USURPACION DE FUNCIONES, 23-ago-1884

###### Art. 255.- The judge who arrogates to himself attributions proper to the political or

administrative authorities, or prevents them from the legitimate exercise of theirs, shall be punished with a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

The same penalty shall be incurred by any employee of the political or administrative order who exercises judicial attributions, or prevents the execution of a ruling or decision issued by the competent judge.

Note: Article amended by Art. 59 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 256.- The public employee or judge, who legally required to recuse,

continues proceeding before the competence or recusation is decided, shall be punished with a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America. Note: Article amended by Art. 60 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordancias:

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 21, 848, 856

###### Art. 257.- Shall be punished with ordinary major imprisonment of eight to twelve years,

servants of the organisms and entities of the public sector and any person in charge of a public service, who, for their own benefit or that of third parties, shall have abused public or private monies, effects representing them, pieces, titles, documents, movable or immovable property that were in their possession by virtue of or by reason of their position, whether the abuse consists of embezzlement, arbitrary disposal or any other similar form. The penalty shall be extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years if the infraction refers to funds destined for national defense.

Misappropriation shall be understood as the application of funds to purposes other than those provided for in the respective budget, when this fact also implies abuse for personal benefit or that of third parties, for purposes unrelated to public service.

Included in this provision are servants who manage funds of the Ecuadorian Social Security Institute or of state and private banks. Also included are servants of the Office of the Comptroller General and of the Superintendency of Banks who have intervened in audits, examinations or special reviews, provided that the reports issued implied complicity or concealment in the crime being investigated.

Those found guilty against whom a conviction is issued shall be, moreover, perpetually disqualified from holding any public office or function; for this purpose, the first instance judge shall communicate, immediately upon becoming final, the judgment to the National Personnel Directorate and to the appointing authority of the respective servant, and likewise to the Superintendency of Banks if it concerns a banking servant. The National Director of Personnel shall refrain from registering appointments or contracts granted in favor of such disqualified persons, for which purpose a registry shall be kept in the National Personnel Directorate recording their names.

The criminal action shall prescribe in double the time indicated in article 101.

With the same penalty shall be sanctioned the servants of the General Directorate of Revenue and the customs servants who have intervened in Acts of Determination.

Also included in the provisions of this article are officials, administrators, executives or employees of institutions of the private national financial system, as well as the members or directors of the boards and administration councils of these entities, who have contributed to the commission of these offenses.

Note: Article replaced by Art. 396 of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control, No. 1429, published in Official Register 337 of May 16. Note: By numeral 1) of article 99 of Law No. 73, published in Supplement Official Register 595 of June 12, 2002, which contains the Organic Law of the Office of the Comptroller General, Title XI of the Organic Law of Financial Administration and Control is repealed, title which contained article 396 that replaced article 257 of the Criminal Code, the latter being repealed together with Title XI.

Note: Art. 16 of Supreme Decree No. 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978, reforms Art. 257, regarding the length of penalties, and suppressing "misappropriation" as a crime. By Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, said Art. 16 is repealed, however, in Art. 1 it is not restored to its original text, but rather it is addressed in Art. 3, restoring the original penalties. Consequently, there was apparently an intention in the Legislative Decree to eliminate "misappropriation" as a criminal classification. We point this out as a matter of inquiry for the User.

Note: Article reformed by Law No. 93, published in Supplement Official Register 764 of August 22, 1995.

Note: Article reformed by Art. 19 of Law No. 26, published in Official Register 190 of May 13, 1999.

Note: Article reformed by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 131, 231, 233

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 574

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2374

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 233
