Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE CONTRABANDO, 18-mar-1909

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO DE AGUARDIENTE, 16-oct-1911

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO, 16-abr-1912

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO, 01-may-1912

Gaceta Judicial, JUICIO DE CONTRABANDO, 01-may-1912

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO, 27-nov-1912

Gaceta Judicial, JUICIOS DE CONTRABANDO, 09-jul-1913

Gaceta Judicial, TIPIFICACION DE CONTRABANDO, 23-mar-1915

Gaceta Judicial, APREHENSION DE COSAS DE CONTRABANDO, 19-jun-1915

Gaceta Judicial, JUICIO DE CONTRABANDO, 11-mar-1916

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO, 11-mar-1916

Gaceta Judicial, CONTRABANDO, 08-jul-1918

Gaceta Judicial, COMISO DE CONTRABANDO, 23-ago-1918

Gaceta Judicial, IMPUESTOS AL AGUARDIENTE, 06-nov-1918

SEPTIMA.- Given that by state policy that promotes subsidies for fuel consumption, border citizens engage in selling small quantities of fuel at the southern border and at the northern border, the State shall establish an emergency program of training and support for microenterprises with promotional credit and facilities for micro-commerce and exchange in border areas.

Nota: Disposición agregada por Ley No. 85, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 170 de 14 de Septiembre del 2007 .

Disposición Transitoria

The President of the Republic, within ninety days from the date of promulgation of this Law, shall issue the corresponding Regulations.

Nota: Disposición agregada por Ley No. 85, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 170 de 14 de Septiembre del 2007 .

##### CAPITULO VI

Del pago con cheques sin provisión de fondos

Nota: Capítulo reformado por Art. 96 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Nota: Capítulo y su artículo derogado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 555 de 24 de Marzo del 2009 .

#### TITULO V

DE LOS DELITOS CONTRA LA SEGURIDAD PUBLICA

##### CAPITULO I

De las asociaciones ilícitas

###### Art. 369.- Any association formed with the purpose of attacking persons or

property is a crime that exists by the mere fact of the organization of the group. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1961

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE ASOCIACION ILICITA, 15-oct-2001

Gaceta Judicial, DELITOS POR TENENCIA DE ARMAS Y ASOCIACION ILICITA, 20-nov-2002

###### Art. 370.- If the association has had as its purpose the perpetration of crimes that merit a

penalty of major imprisonment, the instigators of the association, its leaders and those who have exercised any command in it, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

They shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years, if the association has been formed to commit crimes punished with minor imprisonment; and with correctional imprisonment of six months to three years, if the association has been formed to commit crimes of another nature. Case law:

Judicial Gazette, CLASSIFICATION OF CRIME DIFFERENT FROM THE OPENING OF THE PLENARY, 03-sep-2002

###### Art. 371.- Any other individuals who have taken part in the association and

those who fraudulently have supplied the party or its divisions with weapons, ammunition, instruments to commit the crime, lodging, hiding place or meeting place, shall be punished:

In the first case of the preceding article, with imprisonment of one to five years; In the second case, with imprisonment of three months to three years; and, In the third case, with imprisonment of two months to one year.

###### Art. 372.

Those condemned to imprisonment by virtue of Arts. 370 and 371, may be placed under special surveillance of the authority for at least two years and at most five years.

##### CAPITULO II

Of the improper preservation of explosives

###### Art. 373.- It is categorically prohibited for private individuals to have in their homes, warehouses

or installations of any type materials and explosives that constitute elements of danger for persons and properties and that must be kept or stored in technically adequate places and sites.

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 374.- Those who have such explosive materials in homes, warehouses or

installations of any type, are obliged to deliver them to the corresponding military or police authorities, with the exception of artisanal activities dedicated to the manufacture of pyrotechnic games and mining activities, provided that they are stored in unpopulated places.

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

###### Art. 375.- Those who contravene the previous provisions, shall be punished with

imprisonment of 2 to 4 years.

Note: Article replaced by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of

March 2006.

###### Art. 376.- Attacks against persons or property by means of explosives, shall be

punished with sixteen years of extraordinary major imprisonment.

##### CAPITULO III

Of intimidation

###### Art. 377.- Whoever in writing, anonymous or signed, threatens another with any

attack against persons or properties, which merits a penalty of minor imprisonment, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, if the threat has been accompanied by order or condition.

Otherwise, the penalty shall be three months to one year and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article reformed by Art. 97 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 378.- If the threat made with order and under condition has been verbal, the guilty party shall be

punished with imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article reformed by Art. 98 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 379.- Whoever in writing, anonymous or signed, threatens another with an attack

against persons or properties, which merits a penalty of major imprisonment, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America, if the threat has been accompanied by order or condition; and, otherwise, with imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of six to twelve dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article reformed by Art. 99 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 380.

In the cases provided for in the three preceding articles, the guilty party may, in addition, be placed under special surveillance of the authority for a time not exceeding four years.

###### Art. 381.- Exempted from the previous provisions are threats made in the

act of any quarrel or fight, aggression, offense, provocation or insult, which shall not be subject to any penalty other than that incurred for the same aggression, offense, or quarrel.

###### Art. 382.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years whoever, to instill public fear

or incite tumults or disorders, makes signals, gives alarm cries, detonates bombs or explosive materials or places them for that purpose, or threatens with a disaster of common danger, when these events do not constitute a more severely punished crime.

##### CAPITULO IV

Of vagrants and beggars

###### Art. 383.- Vagrants are those who have no fixed domicile or means of subsistence, and

those who, without illness or injury that disables them, do not habitually exercise a trade or profession.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 103

###### Art. 384.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

Vagrant beggars who have been found carrying false certificates or passports, or who feign injuries or illnesses;

Vagrants or beggars who have been found carrying weapons, or have threatened with an attack on persons or properties, or exercised an act of violence against them; and, Vagrants or beggars who are found provided with files, lock picks or other instruments suitable for committing robberies or other crimes, or for procuring the means to enter houses.

###### Art. 385.- The beggar who has been apprehended disguised in any manner, or who

flees from the establishment in which the authority has placed him, shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to one year.

##### CAPITULO V

Of instigation to commit crimes

###### Art. 386.- Whoever publicly instigates to commit a specific crime against a

person or institution, when the instigator cannot be legally considered as an accomplice, shall be punished, for the instigation and even if the crime has not been perpetrated, with imprisonment of fifteen days to two years, according to the gravity of the instigated crime.

##### CAPITULO VI

Of the apology of crime

###### Art. 387.- Shall be punished with a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United

States of North America whoever publicly and by any means makes the apology of crime, or of a person convicted of crime, by reason of the act committed.

The same penalty shall apply to whoever makes the apology of a suicide.

Note: Article reformed by Art. 100 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002. .

##### CAPITULO VII

Of arson and other destructions, of deteriorations and damages

###### Art. 388.- Those who have set fire shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to

sixteen years:

1o.- To vessels, aircraft, warehouses, shipyards, buildings or any other places that serve as dwellings and contain one or more persons at the time of the fire;

2o.- To buildings that serve for gatherings of individuals, during the time of these gatherings; and, 3o.- To any place, even uninhabited, if it contained deposits of gunpowder or other explosive materials; and if, according to the circumstances, the author should have presumed that there were one or more persons in it at the time of the crime, or if the fire could spread to other inhabited buildings nearby.

The penalty shall be six to nine years of minor imprisonment, if the walls of the building were of stone, brick, or other non-combustible materials, and did not contain within their enclosure deposits of explosive materials.

###### Art. 389.- Those who have voluntarily set fire, whether to the objects designated in the preceding article, but outside the cases provided for by said article, or to forests, groves, workshops or sown fields, shall be punished with minor imprisonment of three to six years.

If these objects belonged exclusively to those who have set them on fire, and the fire was set with fraudulent intent, the guilty parties shall be punished, in cases not covered by the preceding article, with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of six to twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America. If there were no fraud or danger that the fire would harm others, the person who sets fire to his own property shall not be punished, unless the fire harms the national economy.

If the fire had been set to harvested crops; cut wood placed in piles, the crime shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years.

If the harvested crops or cut wood have not been gathered, the penalty shall be imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

If the burned crops or wood belonged exclusively to the arsonist, and if the fire was set with fraudulent intent, the penalties shall be: in the first case provided for in this article, imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of six to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America; and in the second case, imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

It shall be understood that there is fraudulent intent when the burning of the objects indicated in this article results in detriment to the national economy and has no reasonable justification.

Note: Article amended by Art. 101 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 390.- In the cases provided for by the preceding articles, the convicted person may be

placed, in addition, under special surveillance of the authority for at least two years and at most five.

###### Art. 391.- Anyone who, with the intention of committing any of the acts provided for in the

preceding articles, has set fire to any objects placed in such a way that the fire could spread to the thing to be destroyed, shall be punished as if he had directly set or attempted to set fire to the latter thing.

###### Art. 392.- When the fire has spread from the object that the guilty party wanted to

burn, to another object whose destruction entails a more severe penalty, the latter shall be applied if the things were so placed that the fire must necessarily spread from one to the other.

###### Art. 393.- When the fire has caused the death of one or more persons, the penalty shall be

special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

If it has caused permanent injuries or lesions, the arsonist shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.

###### Art. 394.- The burning of another's movable or immovable property, which has been caused, whether by the age or lack of repair or cleaning of ovens, chimneys, forges, houses or nearby workshops; or by fires lit in the fields, less than one hundred meters from buildings, forests, thickets, orchards, or plantations, fences, piles of grain, straw, hay, fodder or any other deposit of combustible materials; or by fires or lights carried or left without sufficient precaution; or by fireworks lit or thrown carelessly, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to

sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 102 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 395.- Those who have destroyed or attempted to destroy, by means of an explosion, buildings, vessels, aircraft, carriages, wagons, warehouses, shipyards or other constructions, shall be punished with the penalties indicated in the preceding articles and according to

the distinctions established therein.

The provision of Art. 391 is applicable to the cases provided for in this article.

###### Art. 396.

The burning of huts, haystacks, uninhabited sheds, or any other object whose value, exceeding ten sucres does not reach fifty, and where there is no danger of the fire spreading, shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen to ninety days.

###### Art. 397.

Anyone who has destroyed or demolished, in whole or in part, buildings, bridges, dikes, causeways, roads, railways, aqueducts, aerodromes, or other national, municipal, or other person's constructions, shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years.

The total or partial destruction of installations for fire alarm services, as well as the improper use of such services, shall be punished with the same penalty. Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 608

Case law:

Judicial Gazette, JUSTICE BY ONE'S OWN HAND, 30-Jan-1995

Judicial Gazette, DESTRUCTION OF OTHERS' PROPERTY, 22-Sep-2009

###### Art. 398.- Those who destroy another's aqueducts that do not constitute masonry works shall be punished with imprisonment of three to six months.

When dealing with new works that attack the property or possessory right of the person or persons who have destroyed the aqueduct or intake, the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall not be applicable.

The same penalty as indicated in the first paragraph shall apply to those who destroy living fences or fences of any other kind, when the act does not constitute usurpation. Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 903

###### Art. 399.- Anyone who has destroyed a machine belonging to another, whether hydraulic, steam,

electric, or movable by animal power, if it is intended for manufacturing or agricultural industry, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

There is destruction from the moment the action of the machinery has been paralyzed in whole or in part, whether the act affects the motor apparatus or the apparatus set in motion.

Note: Article amended by Art. 103 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 400.- When the act provided for by the preceding article has been committed in

assembly or gang, or by means of violence, acts of force, or threats, the guilty parties shall be punished with imprisonment of three to five years.

###### Art. 401.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen

dollars of the United States of North America, whoever shall have destroyed, knocked down, mutilated, or damaged the following objects:

1o.- Tombs, commemorative signs, or gravestones;

2o.- Monuments, statues, or other objects intended for public utility or ornamentation and erected by the authority or with its authorization; and, 3o.- Monuments, statues, paintings, or any other object of art, placed in churches, chapels, or other public buildings.

In case of destruction or violation of sepulchers, to steal the mortuary boxes, the objects enclosed with the corpses, or the corpse itself, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years. The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever commits the violation to take advantage of the materials of the destroyed tomb, or to satisfy an act of revenge.

Note: Article amended by Art. 104 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, VIOLATION OF TOMBS, 15-Dec-1943

###### Art. 402.- Whoever shall have destroyed, in any manner, authentic records or

original instruments of public authority, civil or criminal proceedings, shall be punished with major imprisonment of four to eight years.

The destruction of titles, bills of exchange, commercial or bank documents, or any fiduciary issued by virtue of a Law, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America.

The destruction of receipts, obligations, drafts, or other private documents, that contain proof of an act or contract, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years.

If the aforementioned violations have been committed with one or more aggravating circumstances, the penalties shall be replaced as follows:

Major imprisonment of four to eight years, with equal penalty of eight to twelve years;

Imprisonment of one to five years, with minor imprisonment of three to six years; and, Imprisonment of six months to three years, with imprisonment of two to five years.

Note: Article amended by Art. 105 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Cross-references:

CODE OF CIVIL PROCEDURE, Arts. 164, 191

COMMERCIAL CODE, Arts. 410

###### Art. 403.- Any destruction or damage to movable property of another, executed without

violence or threats, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to one year and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 106 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Cross-references:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 585

###### Art. 404.- If the act has been committed in a gathering or in a gang, the penalty shall be three months

to two years of imprisonment.

###### Art. 405.- The destruction or damage to movable property of another, executed with

violence or threats, in an inhabited house or in its dependencies, and with any of the aggravating circumstances present, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

The penalty shall be minor imprisonment of six to nine years, if the crime has been committed in a gathering or in a gang.

Art. ...- The destruction or damage to movable or immovable property belonging to third parties, committed inside sports venues or in their surroundings or other places that by their capacity, may host mass public gatherings, shall be punished with the penalty of three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 63, published in Official Register 412 of December 7, 2006 .

###### Art. 406.- If the violence or threats with which the destruction or damage have been

committed caused an incurable disease, or permanent bodily injury, the guilty parties shall be punished with the penalty immediately superior to that in which they would have incurred, according to the preceding article; and if they caused death, they shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in the Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in the Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders the return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001 .

###### Art. 407.- Whoever shall have removed, cut or destroyed the moorings, or the obstacles that

held a vessel, a wagon or a carriage, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to two years.

###### Art. 408.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to three years and a fine of eight to sixteen

dollars of the United States of North America, whoever shall have cut or felled crops, or plantations owed to the industry of man or to nature. Note: Article amended by Art. 107 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, THEFT OF POTATO CROPS, 10-May-1955

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF DESTRUCTION OF CROPS, 20-Feb-2001

###### Art. 409.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen

dollars of the United States of North America, whoever shall have felled or destroyed a sown field; or spread weeds, or any harmful herb or plant in the seeds; or broken or damaged agricultural instruments, animal pens, or the guardians' huts.

Note: Article amended by Art. 108 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF DESTRUCTION OF PLANTINGS, 09-Sep-1963

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF DESTRUCTION OF PLANTINGS, 21-Oct-2002

###### Art. 410.- Whoever shall have knocked down, mutilated, or debarked one or more trees, so

that they perish; or destroyed one or more grafts, shall be punished, for each tree, with imprisonment of eight days to one month and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

In no case shall the totality of the penalty exceed three years regarding imprisonment, nor thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America regarding the fine. Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in the Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in the Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders the return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 109 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

###### Art. 411.- Whoever shall have poisoned horses or other draft or burden beasts, horned animals,

sheep, goats or pigs, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 110 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

###### Art. 412.

Whoever shall have thrown into a river, canal, stream, pond, fish pond, or water deposit, substances suitable for destroying fish, shall suffer the penalty of imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of eight to sixteen dollars of the United States of North America. Note: Article amended by Art. 111 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

Cross-references:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 612, 624, 627

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 36

###### Art. 413.- Those who without justifiable cause have killed one of the animals

mentioned in Art. 411, or have caused it a serious wound or injury, shall be punished as follows:

If the offense has been committed in the houses, enclosures or outbuildings, or on the lands of which the owner of the killed or wounded animal was the owner, tenant farmer or lessee, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to six months and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America;

If it has been committed in the places of which the guilty party was the owner, tenant farmer or lessee, the penalty shall be imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of six dollars of the United States of North America; and, If it has been committed in another place, the imprisonment shall be fifteen days to four months and the fine six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 625

###### Art. 414.- Whoever, without necessity, kills a domestic animal, other than those

mentioned in Art. 411, or a domesticated animal, or has caused it a serious wound or injury, in a place of which the owner of the animal is the owner, usufructuary, user, lessor or lessee, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to three months and a fine of six to nine dollars of the United States of North America.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 113 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 624, 639

###### Art. 415.- If in the cases provided for by the preceding articles there has been a violation of

enclosure, the penalty shall be doubled.

Art. ...- Computer damage.- Whoever willfully, by any means or using any method, destroys, alters, renders useless, suppresses or damages, temporarily or permanently, the programs, data, databases, information or any data message contained in an information system or electronic network, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to three years and a fine of sixty to one hundred fifty dollars of the United States of North America.

The penalty of imprisonment shall be three to five years and a fine of two hundred to six hundred dollars of the United States of North America, when it involves programs, data, databases, information or any data message contained in an information system or electronic network, intended to provide a public service or linked to national defense. Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 67, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 557 de 17 de Abril del 2002 .

Art. ...- If it does not constitute a more serious offense, the destruction, alteration or rendering useless of the infrastructure or physical installations necessary for the transmission, reception or processing of data messages, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight months to four years and a fine of two hundred to six hundred dollars of the United States of North America. Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 67, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 557 de 17 de Abril del 2002 .

##### CAPITULO VII A

OFFENSES AGAINST CULTURAL HERITAGE

Nota: Capítulo agregado por Ley No. 49, publicada en Registro Oficial 2 de 25 de Enero del 2000 .

###### Art. 415-A.- Whoever destroys or damages property belonging to the Cultural Heritage of

the Nation, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years without prejudice to compensation owed to third parties in good faith and to the judge, if feasible, ordering the reconstruction, restoration or restitution of the property, at the expense of the perpetrator of the destruction or damage.

The same penalty shall be imposed on whoever causes damage to an archive, registry, museum, library, educational center, scientific institute, similar institution, archaeological sites or any property belonging to cultural heritage, without prejudice to the judge ordering the adoption of measures aimed at restoring as far as possible the damaged property at the expense of the perpetrator of the damage.

If the offense is committed through negligence, the penalty shall be three months to one year.

The damage shall be punishable when it does not arise from the normal use that should have been given to the property, according to its nature and characteristics.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 49, publicada en Registro Oficial 2 de 25 de Enero del 2000 .

###### Art. 415 B.- The same penalty provided in the preceding article, if the act does not constitute

a more severely punished offense, shall be applied to the public official or employee who, acting on his own or as a member of a collegiate body, authorizes or permits, contrary to law, modifications, alterations or demolitions that cause the destruction or damage of property belonging to the Cultural Heritage of the Nation; as well as to the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

It does not constitute an offense the authorization given to intervene in the heritage property in order to ensure its conservation, if precautions are taken so that during execution the internationally accepted technical standards are respected.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 49, publicada en Registro Oficial 2 de 25 de Enero del 2000 .

###### Art. 415 C.- The same penalty shall be applicable to those who, in violation of the laws and other

legal provisions on the matter, traffic, commercialize or take out of the country archaeological pieces or objects, property of historical interest or belonging to the Cultural Heritage of the Nation.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 49, publicada en Registro Oficial 2 de 25 de Enero del 2000 .

##### CAPITULO VIII

Offenses against means of transport and communication

###### Art. 416.- Whoever renders useless or destroys, in whole or in part, the routes or works

intended for public communication, by land, by air or by water, or obstructs the measures adopted for their safety, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America.

If injuries or wounds result, the penalty shall be four to eight years of reclusión mayor; and if the death of any person results, the penalty shall be special reclusión mayor of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Ley No. 47, publicada en Registro Oficial 422 de 28 de Septiembre del 2001 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 114 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, CIRCUNSTANCIAS ATENUANTES, 03-mar-1910

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE LESIONES DE HIJO AL PADRE, 09-jul-2001

###### Art. 417.- Whoever uses any means to stop or hinder the movement of a

train, or to cause it to derail, shall be punished:

1o.- With imprisonment of six months to three years, if no derailment or other accident occurs;

2o.- With imprisonment of one to five years, if derailment or other accident occurs; 3o.- With reclusión mayor of four years if any person is wounded or injured; and, 4o.- With extraordinary reclusión mayor of twelve to sixteen years if the death of any person results.

Whoever performs any act aimed at interrupting the operation of a telegraph or telephone intended for the service of a railway shall be punished with the penalties established in this article, and in their respective cases.

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior.

###### Art. 418.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime, whoever throws blunt objects or projectiles at a train, streetcar or other vehicle in motion.

Note: Article amended by Art. 115 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 419.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America, whoever executes any act tending to endanger the safety of a vessel, aerostat, or floating construction; or to stop or hinder navigation.

If the act produces shipwreck, damage, stranding, or any other serious accident, the penalty shall be minor confinement of three to six years; if any person results injured or harmed, the penalty shall be four to eight years of major confinement; and if it produces the death of any person, the penalty shall be special major confinement of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text.
Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001.

Note: Article amended by Art. 116 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 420.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven dollars of the United States of North America, if the act is not punished with a more serious penalty, the conductors, captains, pilots, mechanics and other employees of a train or a ship, or an aerostat who abandon their posts during their respective services, before arriving at port or at the end of the railway or aircraft voyage.

The imprisonment shall be three months to one year, in the case of drivers of motor vehicles intended for international, interprovincial or intercantonal transport.

Note: Article amended by Art. 117 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 421.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of two months to two years and a fine of eight to forty-seven dollars of the United States of North America, whoever, through imprudence or negligence, or through lack of skill in their art or profession, or through non-observance of regulations or ordinances, causes a shipwreck, derailment or other traffic accident. If injury, harm or death of any person results from the act, the penalty shall be six months to five years of imprisonment, according to the gravity of the act and its consequences.
Note: Article amended by Art. 118 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 422.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years whoever interrupts postal, telegraphic, telephone, radio or other system communication, or violently resists the restoration of the interrupted communication.

If the act is carried out in a gathering or in a gang, or the interruption is by violent means, acts of force or threats, the penalty shall be imprisonment of three to five years.
Those who offer, provide or commercialize telecommunications services, without being legally authorized, through concession, authorization, license, permit, agreements or any other form of administrative contract, except for the use of internet services, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

Included in this provision are those who are in clandestine possession of installations that, by their configuration and other technical data, suggest that among their purposes is to use them to offer the services indicated in the previous paragraph, even when they are not being used.

The sanctions indicated in this article shall be applied without prejudice to the administrative and civil liabilities provided for in the Special Telecommunications Law and its Regulations.

Note: Article amended by Law No. 38, published in Official Register 253 of August 12, 1999.

##### CAPITULO IX

Of piracy

###### Art. 423.- The crime of piracy or assault committed with armed hand on the high seas, or in the waters or rivers of the Republic, shall be punished with extraordinary major confinement of twelve to sixteen years.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 657

###### Art. 424.- Those who navigate in armed ships with two or more patents from different nations, or without patents or registration, or other document proving the legitimacy of their voyage, shall be considered pirates, even if they do not commit other acts of piracy; and they shall be punished, the commander or captain, with eight to twelve years of major confinement; and the crew members who are found guilty, with four to eight years of the same penalty.

###### Art. 425.

Whoever, maliciously, delivers to pirates the vessel aboard which they are, shall be punished with extraordinary major confinement of twelve to sixteen years.

###### Art. 426.- All privateers shall be considered and punished as pirates.

###### Art. 427.

Whoever, fraudulently, traffics with pirates in the territory of the Republic, shall be punished as their accomplice.

##### CAPITULO X

Of crimes against public health

###### Art. 428.- Whoever, with the purpose of obtaining a profit, has mixed or caused to be mixed with beverages or foodstuffs, or with substances or food articles, intended to be sold, materials of such nature that they can harm health, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

If the materials mixed with beverages or foodstuffs, or with substances or food articles intended for sale, could cause death, the penalty shall be imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of sixteen to sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 119 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Jurisprudence:

Judicial Gazette, CRIME INVESTIGABLE EX OFFICIO, 31-Aug-1965

###### Art. 429.- Shall be punished with the same penalties and according to the distinctions established in the previous article:

Whoever sells or offers for sale any foodstuffs, beverages, substances or food articles, knowing that they contain materials that can harm health or cause death; and,
Whoever has sold or procured those materials, knowing that they were to be used to falsify substances or food articles.

###### Art. 430.

In the previous cases, if the use of those products, altered or falsified, has caused a permanent injury of those defined in this Code, or death, the penalty shall be that determined in the articles dealing with injuries and unintentional homicide.

###### Art. 431.- The foodstuffs, beverages, substances or food articles shall be confiscated and destroyed.

###### Art. 432.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years and a fine of eight to one hundred fifty-six dollars of the United States of North America, whoever knowingly spreads a dangerous or contagious disease to persons.

Note: Article amended by Art. 120 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 433.- Whoever intentionally poisons or contaminates drinking water, or food

or medicinal substances intended for public use or consumption by the community, shall be punished, for the sole act of poisoning or contamination, with major imprisonment of four to eight years and a fine of sixteen to one hundred fifty-six United States dollars.

If the act has caused illness, the penalty shall be major imprisonment of eight to twelve years; and if it has caused death, special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Registry 422 of September 28, 2001.

Note: Article amended by Art. 121 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 12, 264, 276, 411

###### Art. 434.- When the acts provided for in the preceding articles are committed through

recklessness, or negligence, or lack of skill in one's own art or profession, or through non-observance of regulations or ordinances, a fine of eight to seventy-seven United States dollars shall be imposed, if no illness or death of any person results; and imprisonment of six months to five years, if illness or death results.

Note: Article amended by Art. 122 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 435.- Whoever violates the measures adopted by the competent authorities to prevent

the introduction or spread of an epidemic shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of eight to seventy-seven United States dollars.

Note: Article amended by Art. 123 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 436.

Doctors, pharmacists, or any person who, through lack of precaution or care, prescribe, dispense or provide medications that seriously compromise health, shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to one year; if they have caused an illness that appears to be or is incurable, the imprisonment shall be from one to three years; and in the event of having caused death, the imprisonment shall be from three to five years. Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 362, 363

###### Art. 437.- A doctor who lends his name to someone who does not have a degree to practice

the profession shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to one year and a fine of eight to seventy-seven United States dollars.

Note: Article amended by Art. 124 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO X-A

OF CRIMES AGAINST THE ENVIRONMENT

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437-A.- Whoever, outside the cases permitted by law, produces, introduces,

deposits, markets, possesses, or uses hazardous toxic waste, radioactive substances, or other similar substances that by their characteristics constitute a danger to human health or degrade and contaminate the environment, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to four years.

The same penalty shall apply to whoever produces, possesses, markets or introduces chemical or biological weapons.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 15, 362

###### Art. 437-B.- Whoever violates the norms on environmental protection, by discharging

waste of any nature, above the limits established in accordance with the law, if such action causes or could cause harm or alterations to the flora, fauna, genetic potential, hydrobiological resources or biodiversity, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, if the act does not constitute a more severely punished crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 14, 66

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 612

###### Art. 437-C.- The penalty shall be three to five years of imprisonment, when:

a) The acts provided for in the preceding article cause damage to the health of persons or to their property;

b) The harm or alteration caused is of an irreversible nature;

c) The act is part of activities carried out clandestinely by its author; or,

d) The polluting acts seriously affect natural resources necessary for economic activity.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437-D.- If as a consequence of the polluting activity the death of a person occurs,

the penalty provided for unintentional homicide shall be applied, if the act does not constitute a more serious crime.

In the event that injuries occur as a consequence of the polluting activity, the penalties provided for in articles 463 to 467 of the Penal Code shall be imposed. Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437-E.- A penalty of one to three years of imprisonment shall be applied, if the act

does not constitute a more severely punished crime, to the public official or employee who, acting on his own or as a member of a collegiate body, authorizes or permits, against the law, the discharge of polluting waste of any kind above the limits established in accordance with the law; as well as the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 397, 398

###### Art. 437-F.

Whoever hunts, captures, collects, extracts or markets species of flora or fauna that are legally protected, in violation of the legal and regulatory provisions on the matter, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years. The penalty shall be imprisonment of two to four years when:

- a) The act is committed during the seed production or reproduction or growth period of the species;
- b) The act is committed against endangered species; or,
- c) The act is committed through the use of explosives, toxic, flammable or radioactive substances.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 3, 409

###### Art. 437-G.

Whoever extracts protected aquatic flora or fauna species, in prohibited seasons, quantities or zones, or uses prohibited fishing or hunting procedures, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Concordances:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 642, 645, 646, 657

###### Art. 437-H.- Whoever destroys, burns, damages or cuts down, in whole or in part, forests or other

plant formations, natural or cultivated, that are legally protected, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years, provided that the act does not constitute a more serious crime.

The penalty shall be imprisonment from two to four years when:

- a) The crime results in the reduction of natural waters, soil erosion or modification of the climate regime; or,
- b) The crime is committed in places where there are springs that supply water to a populated center or irrigation system.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 83, 406

CIVIL CODE (BOOK II), Arts. 815, 970

###### Art. 437-I.- Whoever, without authorization or without complying with the procedures provided for in the

applicable regulations, allocates lands reserved as ecological protection or exclusive agricultural use, to become areas of urban expansion, or extraction or processing of construction materials, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years, if the act does not constitute a more serious offense.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 400, 409

###### Art. 437-J.- The same penalty provided for in the previous article shall be applied, if the act does not

constitute a more severely punished crime, to the public official or employee who, acting on their own or as a member of a collegial body, authorizes or permits, against the law, that lands reserved as ecological protection or exclusive agricultural use be improperly allocated to a use different from that which legally corresponds to them; as well as to the official or employee whose report or opinion has led to the same result.

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

###### Art. 437-K.- The criminal judge may order, as a precautionary measure, the immediate suspension

of the polluting activity, as well as the definitive or temporary closure of the establishment in question, without prejudice to what may be ordered by the competent authority in environmental matters".

Note: Article added by Law No. 49, published in Official Registry 2 of January 25, 2000.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 15, 66, 397

##### CAPITULO XI

Of the breach of sentence and certain concealments

###### Art. 438.- The convict placed under special surveillance of the authority, who

violates the provisions of Art. 61, shall be punished with imprisonment from fifteen days to six months and the execution of the sentence may not be suspended.

###### Art. 439.- Those who have concealed or caused to be concealed a person, knowing that they were

being pursued or convicted of a crime punished with incarceration, shall be punished with eight days to two years of imprisonment and a fine of six to twelve United States dollars.

Note: Article amended by Art. 125 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 440.

Exempt from the preceding provision are the ascendants, descendants, spouse, and siblings of the concealed fugitives, and of the accomplices or co-authors of the crime, and their relatives by affinity in the same degrees.

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, CO-AUTHORSHIP AND COMPLICITY IN CRIMINAL MATTERS, Feb-14-2006

##### CAPITULO XII

OF THE ILLEGAL TRAFFICKING OF MIGRANTS

###### Art. 440-A.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 20, published in Official Registry 110 of June 30, 2000. (coyote, coyotes, coyoterismo).

Note: Article repealed by Law No. 70, published in Official Registry Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

###### Art. 440-B.

Note: Article added by Law No. 91, published in Official Registry 716 of December 2, 2002.

Note: Article repealed by Law No. 70, published in Official Registry Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

Art. ...- Whoever, by illegal means, promotes, facilitates, induces, finances, collaborates, participates or assists in the migration of persons, nationals or foreigners, from the territory of the Ecuadorian State to other countries, provided that it does not constitute a more serious offense, shall be punished with the penalty of major incarceration from four to eight years and a fine of twenty to forty unified basic remunerations.

The same penalty shall be imposed on those in charge of the protection and custody of children or adolescents, whether they be father, mother, grandparents, uncles, siblings or guardians or any other person who in any way facilitates the execution of this offense.

The competent Judge shall order the immediate apprehension and seizure of the clandestine transport used, when, in the event of an accident involving it, persons are killed or injured, and the proceeds from the auction of the same must be allocated in the manner provided for in article 186 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

For crimes concurrent with the offense of illegal trafficking of emigrants, an accumulation of penalties shall be established up to a maximum of twenty-five years of special major incarceration.

When the acts of execution of the crime of trafficking of emigrants result in the death of the emigrant, the guilty parties shall be punished with extraordinary major incarceration from twelve to sixteen years and if injuries provided for in articles 465, 466 and 467 of this Code occur, the guilty parties shall be punished with the penalty of ordinary major incarceration from eight to twelve years.

It shall be considered an aggravating circumstance the fact of having known, or that it is something evident, that the means of transport used for the transportation of the emigrants was in poor condition for use or that it did not have sufficient capacity for the number of persons it was transporting.

The same penalty shall be imposed on the owners of air, sea or land transport vehicles and on persons who are part of the crew or in charge of operation and driving, when their knowledge and participation in the offense is established.

The victims who make known the identity of those involved in the commission of this crime shall be protected by the respective authority.

Note: Article given by Law No. 70, published in Official Registry Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, ATTEMPTED ILLEGAL TRAFFICKING OF MIGRANTS, Mar-16-2009

##### CAPITULO XIII

OF CERTAIN CRIMES PROMOTED OR EXECUTED

THROUGH TOURIST ACTIVITIES

###### Art. 440-C.- The person or persons who instigate, promote or execute tourist

activities with the purpose of committing or perpetrating the crime of kidnapping typified in article 188 and contemplated in Chapter III referring to "Crimes against Individual Liberty", of Title II, First Book of the Penal Code; of crimes against persons contemplated in Title VI and particularly typified in Chapter I referring to crimes against life; in Chapter II related to "Injuries" and the

##### Chapter III relating to "Abandonment of Persons" of Book II of the Penal Code; of the

sexual offenses contemplated in Title VIII, in Chapter II relating to "Indecent Assault, rape and statutory rape", Chapter II pertaining to the crimes of procuring and corruption of minors and Chapter IV relating to abduction; Book II of the Penal Code, of crimes against property contemplated in Title X and particularly typified in Chapter I related to the crime of theft, Chapter II concerning the crime of robbery, Chapter IV relating to the crime of extortion and Chapter V referring to fraud and other defrauding of Book II of the Penal Code, the maximum penalty corresponding to the nature of the respective offense shall be imposed. Likewise, those who commit crimes provided in this article against persons having the status of tourists and knowing that they had such status shall be imposed the maximum penalty provided for the offense perpetrated.

Note: Chapter and article added by Law No. 97, published in Official Register Supplement 733 of December 27, 2002.

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF RAPE, 26-Jan-1914

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF INDECENT ASSAULT, 06-Sep-1993

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF INDECENT ASSAULT, 14-Oct-1993

CHAPTER...

OF THE CRIMES OF GENOCIDE AND ETHNOCIDE

Art. ...- Whoever, with purpose to destroy in whole or in part a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, perpetrates any of the following acts, shall be punished:

- 1. Whoever causes the death of its members, shall be punished with a special major imprisonment penalty of sixteen to twenty-five years.
- 2. Whoever causes serious injury to the physical or mental integrity of members of the group, shall be punished with an ordinary minor imprisonment penalty of six to nine years.
- 3. Whoever intentionally subjects the group to conditions of existence that would bring about its total or partial physical destruction, shall be punished with an ordinary minor imprisonment penalty of six to nine years.
- 4. Whoever takes measures intended to prevent births within the group shall be punished with an ordinary minor imprisonment penalty of six to nine years. Information or access to family planning methods, contraceptive methods and sexual and reproductive health services, shall not be considered measures intended to prevent births.
- 5. Whoever forcibly transfers children of the group to another group, shall be punished with an ordinary minor imprisonment penalty of six to nine years.

Note: Chapter and article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

Art. ...- Whoever disrespects the self-determination of a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, or their will to remain in voluntary isolation, shall incur the crime of ethnocide and shall be punished with an ordinary minor imprisonment penalty of three to six years. Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

Art. ...- Whoever carries out, with awareness that the total or partial disappearance of human groups may occur, activities tending to influence, alter, or in any way change the culture, way of life or identity of peoples in voluntary isolation, shall be punished with a prison penalty of two to four years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

Art. ...- The fact that the offenses typified in this chapter have been committed by a subordinate, shall not exempt from criminal liability the superior who ordered it nor the subordinate who executed it.

In all these cases, the attempt shall be punished with half of the penalty provided for the consummated crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

Art. ...- The actions and penalties for the crimes dealt with in this chapter shall be imprescriptible.

Note: Article added by Law No. 1, published in Official Register Supplement 578 of April 27, 2009.

#### TITLE VI

OF CRIMES AGAINST PERSONS

##### CHAPTER I

Of crimes against life

###### Art. 441.- Whoever by means of food, beverages, medicines, violence or any other

means has intentionally caused an abortion in a woman who has not consented to it, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment.

If the means employed have had no effect, it shall be punished as an attempt.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 45

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 60, 61

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 103

GENERAL LAW OF CIVIL REGISTRY IDENTIFICATION AND IDENTITY CARDS, Arts. 130, 132

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, ATTEMPTED HOMICIDE AND INJURIES, 24-Jan-2000

###### Art. 442.- When the abortion has been caused by violence done voluntarily, but

without intention to cause it, the guilty party shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years. If the violence has been committed with premeditation or with knowledge of the woman's condition, the imprisonment shall be from one to five years.

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, PREMEDITATION IN HOMICIDE, 22-Jun-2001

###### Art. 443.- Whoever by means of food, beverages, medicines or any other means has

caused an abortion in a woman who has consented to it, shall be punished with imprisonment of two to five years.

###### Art. 444.- The woman who has voluntarily consented to having an abortion performed on her, or

causes the abortion herself, shall be punished with imprisonment of one to five years. If she consents to having an abortion performed on her or causes the abortion herself, to conceal her dishonor, she shall be punished with six months to two years of imprisonment.

Cross-references:

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 61

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, ABORTION, 30-May-1997

###### Art. 445.- When the means employed for the purpose of causing an abortion in a woman have

caused her death, whoever has applied or prescribed them for said purpose, shall be punished with three to six years of minor imprisonment, if the woman has consented to the abortion; and with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years, if the woman has not consented. Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, ACCOMPLICE TO LETHAL ABORTION, 31-Aug-2009

###### Art. 446.

In the cases provided for by Arts. 441, 443 and 445, if the guilty party is a physician, obstetrician, midwife, practitioner or pharmacist, the penalty of imprisonment shall be replaced with minor imprisonment of three to six years; that of minor imprisonment, with major imprisonment of four to eight years; and that of ordinary major imprisonment with extraordinary imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

###### Art. 447.- Abortion performed by a physician, with the consent of the woman or of her

husband or close relatives, when she is not in a position to give it, shall not be punishable:

1o.- If it has been done to avoid a danger to the life or health of the mother, and if this danger cannot be avoided by other means; and, 2o.- If the pregnancy results from a rape or statutory rape committed upon an idiotic or insane woman. In this case, for the abortion the consent of the woman's legal representative shall be required.

Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66, 69

###### Art. 448.

Homicide, wounds, blows and injuries are classified as voluntary, unless the contrary is proven, or the lack of intention is evident from the circumstances of the act, quality and location of the wounds, or the instruments with which they were made. Cross-references:

CONSTITUTION OF THE REPUBLIC OF ECUADOR, Arts. 66

Case Law:

Judicial Gazette, ATTEMPTED MURDER OR SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 17-Mar-1886

Judicial Gazette, CRIME OF HOMICIDE, 07-Mar-1889

Judicial Gazette, INVOLUNTARY HOMICIDE, 22-Jun-1901

Judicial Gazette, CULPABLE HOMICIDE, 25-Sep-1940

Judicial Gazette, QUALIFIED HOMICIDE, 27-Aug-2001

###### Art. 449.- Homicide committed with intent to kill, but without any of the

circumstances detailed in the following article, is simple homicide and shall be punished with major imprisonment of eight to twelve years.

Cross-references:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 99, 100, 101

CIVIL CODE (BOOK I), Arts. 64

GENERAL LAW OF CIVIL REGISTRATION IDENTIFICATION AND IDENTITY CARDS, Arts. 131

Case law:

Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDE AND MURDER, 25-Oct-1976

Gaceta Judicial, CRIME OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 15-Nov-1976

Gaceta Judicial, MURDER AND SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 23-Mar-1977

Gaceta Judicial, HOMICIDE AND MURDER, 07-Mar-1978

Gaceta Judicial, SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 15-Dec-1982

Gaceta Judicial, CORPUS DELICTI OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 23-Feb-1994

Gaceta Judicial, SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 08-Sep-1998

Gaceta Judicial, EVIDENCE AND PRESUMPTIONS OF HOMICIDE, 03-Nov-1999

Gaceta Judicial, TYPICAL ELEMENTS OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 26-Feb-2002

Gaceta Judicial, CRIME OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 01-Apr-2002

Gaceta Judicial, CRIME OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 07-May-2002

Gaceta Judicial, SIMPLE HOMICIDE AND MURDER, 04-Mar-2004

Gaceta Judicial, CRIME OF SIMPLE HOMICIDE, 24-Apr-2007

###### Art. 450.- It is murder and shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to

twenty-five years, homicide committed with any of the following circumstances: 1a.- With treachery;

2a.- For a price or remunerative promise;

3a.- By means of flooding, poison, fire, or derailment;

4a.- With cruelty, deliberately and inhumanly increasing the pain of the victim; 5a.- When the victim has been rendered unable to defend themselves;

6a.- By any means capable of causing great havoc;

7a.- Purposely seeking nighttime or an uninhabited place to commit the homicide; 8a.- For the purpose of preventing discovery or detention of the offender, except when the killer is an ascendant or descendant, spouse or sibling of the offender whom they intended to favor; and, 9a.- As a means to prepare, facilitate, consummate or conceal another crime, or to ensure its results or impunity; or for not having obtained the results intended when attempting the other punishable act.

10. With hatred or contempt based on race, religion, national or ethnic origin, sexual orientation or sexual identity, age, marital status or disability, of the victim.

11. If it has been committed against members of the Armed Forces or National Police, prosecutors or criminal court judges, in the performance of their duties. Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 47, published in Official Register 422 of September 28, 2001 .

Note: Number 10. added by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Register 555 of March 24, 2009 .

Note: Number 11. added by Law No. 0, published in Supplementary Official Register 160 of March 29, 2010 .

Cross-references:

CODE OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE 2000, Arts. 99
