###### 170. Art. 575-A.- Shall be punished with imprisonment from two to five years those who with the purpose of gaining personal benefit and as leaders, organize pseudo-cooperatives, and invade lands both in urban and rural areas, thus violating the right to private property.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 2969, publicado en Registro Oficial 714 de 20 de Noviembre de 1978 .

Concordancias:

CONSTITUCION DE LA REPUBLICA DEL ECUADOR, Arts. 282, 321

###### Art. 575-B.- Whoever alleging the status of member of a pseudo cooperative, invades lands located in rural or urban areas and negotiates over those or over alleged rights acquired in said lands, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years. Nota: Artículo agregado por Decreto Supremo No. 2969, publicado en Registro Oficial 714 de 20 de Noviembre de 1978 .

###### Art. 575-C.-

Nota: Artículo agregado por el artículo 82 de la Ley No. 6, publicado en Registro Oficial 97 de 29 de Diciembre de 1988 .

Nota: Artículo 82 de la Ley No. 6 sustituido por Ley No. 51, publicada en Registro Oficial 349 de 31 de Diciembre de 1993 .

Nota: Artículo derogado por Disposición Derogatoria Tercera de Decreto Legislativo No. 0, publicado en Registro Oficial Suplemento 242 de 29 de Diciembre del 2007 .

###### Art. 575-D.- Whoever fraudulently, through any act does not pay the minimum support price for the box of banana for export, set by current interministerial agreement, as well as the masterminds, accomplices and concealers, shall be punished with imprisonment from one to three years and the fine established in article 4 of the Law to Stimulate and Control the Production and Marketing of Banana. Once the criminal trial has been initiated for the infraction referred to in the preceding paragraph, the judge shall order as a precautionary measure, the suspension of the trademark and/or exporter's patent.

Nota: Artículo agregado por Ley No. 48, publicada en Registro Oficial 347 de 27 de Diciembre de 1999 .

##### CAPITULO VI

Of bankrupts and other punishable debtors

###### Art. 576.- Merchants who, in the cases provided by law, are declared guilty or responsible for bankruptcy, shall be punished:

Those of culpable bankruptcy, with imprisonment from one to three years; and, Those of absconding or fraudulent bankruptcy, with minor imprisonment from three to six years.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (TITULO PRELIMINAR), Arts. 29

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1633

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 507, 508, 519, 589

CODIGO DE COMERCIO, Arts. 85, 116

LEY ORGANICA DE INSTITUCIONES DEL SISTEMA FINANCIERO, Arts. 132

###### Art. 577.- When dealing with the bankruptcy of a company or legal entity that

engages in commerce, any director, administrator or manager of the bankrupt company or legal entity, or accountant or bookkeeper, who has cooperated in the execution of any of the culpable or fraudulent acts that determine the bankruptcy, shall be punished with the penalty for fraudulent or culpable bankruptcy, as applicable.

###### Art. 578.- When not dealing with the bankruptcy of a merchant, the guilty party shall be punished

with imprisonment of one to five years, in the case of fraudulent insolvency, and with imprisonment of six months to two years, in the case of culpable insolvency.

###### Art. 579.- They shall be punished with imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of six to

sixty-two dollars of the United States of North America.

Those who, for the benefit of the bankrupt, have removed, concealed or hidden, in whole or in part, their movable and immovable property;

Those who have fraudulently appeared in the bankruptcy, and sustained, whether in their own name or through the interposition of a person, fictitious or exaggerated credits;

The creditor who has stipulated, whether with the bankrupt or any other person, particular advantages, by reason of their votes in the deliberation relating to the bankruptcy, or who has made a private contract from which an advantage results in their favor and against the assets of the bankrupt; and, The trustee of the bankruptcy guilty of embezzlement in the performance of their duties. Note: Article amended by Art. 168 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO VII

Of usurpation

###### Art. 580.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one month to two years:

1o.- Whoever by violence, deceit or abuse of confidence dispossesses another of the possession or holding of immovable property, or of a real right of use, usufruct, habitation, easement or antichresis, constituted over an immovable;

2o.- Whoever, in order to seize all or part of an immovable, destroys or alters the boundaries or limits thereof; and, 3o.- Whoever, with violence or threats, obstructs the possession of an immovable. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 715, 725, 727, 729, 778, 825, 859

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2337

CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO PENAL 2000, Arts. 36

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, DELITOS DE USURPACION, 27-jun-1949

Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE DESTRUCCION DE SEMBRIOS, 21-mar-1959

Gaceta Judicial, JUICIO DE USURPACION, 28-may-1996

Gaceta Judicial, ELEMENTOS DEL DELITO DE USURPACION, 13-sep-2004

Gaceta Judicial, DELITOS DE INVASION DE TERRENOS O DE USURPACION, 14-oct-2004 Gaceta Judicial, DELITO DE USURPACION, 10-abr-2007

Gaceta Judicial, USURPACION, 05-feb-2009

Gaceta Judicial, VIOLENCIA, ENGAÑO, 18-feb-2009

###### Art. 581.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of fifteen days to one year:

1o.- Whoever obstructs the right that a third party has over waters; and, 2o.- Whoever, unlawfully and with the purpose of preventing the lawful use of a person with a right, dams, diverts or detains the waters of rivers, streams, canals or springs, or usurps any right whatsoever relating to the course thereof.

The penalty shall be increased up to two years, if in order to commit the offenses expressed in numbers 1o. and 2o. of this article, dikes, locks, floodgates or other similar works, made on rivers, streams, springs, reservoirs, canals or aqueducts, are broken or altered.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 612

LEY DE AGUAS, Arts. 8, 70, 77, 78

###### Art. 582.- Whoever fraudulently removes or diverts water from the public or from

private individuals, whether to take advantage of it for their own benefit, or for any other purpose, shall be punished with imprisonment of eight days to six months and a fine of five to twenty-five dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 169 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Concordancias:

LEY DE AGUAS, Arts. 35, 70, 77, 78

##### CAPITULO VIII

Of usury and pawn loan houses

###### Art. 583.- A loan is usurious in which, directly or indirectly, an interest greater than that

permitted by law, or other usurious advantages, is stipulated. Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 2102, 2108, 2109, 2110, 2115 CODIGO DE PROCEDIMIENTO CIVIL, Arts. 163

LEY PARA LA TRANSFORMACION ECONOMICA DEL ECUADOR, (TROLEBUS), Arts. 14 Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, USURA, 01-feb-1933

Gaceta Judicial, USURA, 13-mar-1934

Gaceta Judicial, PACTO USURARIO, 04-dic-1934

###### Art. 584.- Shall be punished with the penalty of imprisonment of six months to two years and a fine of

sixteen to three hundred eleven dollars of the United States of North America, whoever engages in usurious lending.

Note: Article amended by Art. 170 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 585.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of two to four years and a fine of one thousand dollars of the

United States of North America, whoever conceals, with any other contractual form whatsoever, the reality of a usurious loan.

Note: Article amended by Art. 171 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Note: Article amended by Law No. 70, published in Official Register Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

###### Art. 586.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of two to four years and a fine of five hundred

dollars of the United States of North America, whoever, being engaged in the business of loans on pledges, salaries, or wages, does not keep books, recording in them, without blanks or interlineations, the amounts loaned, the terms and interest, the names and addresses of those who receive the loan, the nature, quality and value of the objects given in pledge and the other circumstances required by the regulations or ordinances on the matter; or are convicted of falsity in the entries of said books. Note: Article amended by Art. 172 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

Note: Article amended by Law No. 70, published in Official Register Supplement 427 of December 29, 2006.

###### Art. 587.- The lender who does not give a receipt for the pledge or security received, shall

be punished with a fine of double to quintuple its value and the fulfillment of the penalty may not be suspended.

##### CAPITULO IX

General Provisions

###### Art. 588.- They are exempt from criminal liability and subject only to civil liability, for the

thefts, robberies with force against things, frauds, or damages that they reciprocally cause each other:

1o.- Spouses, ascendants, descendants or in-laws, in the same line;

2o.- The widowed spouse, with respect to things belonging to their deceased spouse, as long as they have not passed into the power of another; and, 3o.- Siblings and brothers-in-law or sisters-in-law, if they live together.

The exception of this article is not applicable to strangers, if they participate in the crime.

###### Art. 589.- In the offenses dealt with in this Title, with the exception of those detailed in

Chapter VIII, the perpetrators may be placed under the special surveillance of the authority, for two years at least and five at most.

###### Art. 590.- Whoever fraudulently has falsified or adulterated keys shall

be sentenced to imprisonment of three months to two years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

If the guilty party is a locksmith by trade, they shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years and a fine of eight to thirty-one dollars of the United States of North America.

Note: Article amended by Art. 173 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002.

###### Art. 591.- Public roads are those whose use is public.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 604

###### Art. 592.- Robbery committed during the night is that executed in the period between

seven past meridian and five in the morning.

###### Art. 593.

An inhabited house is considered to be any building, apartment, dwelling, hut, cabin, even if movable, or any other place used for habitation.

###### Art. 594.- The dependencies of an inhabited house are considered to be patios, corrals, gardens and

any other enclosed lands, as well as granaries, stables, and any other buildings contained therein, whatever their use may be, even when they form a particular enclosure within the general enclosure.

###### Art. 595.- Mobile pens, intended to contain livestock in the fields, however they may be made, are

considered a dependency of an inhabited house when they are established on the same space of land with movable cabins or other shelters intended for the guardians.

###### Art. 596.- Violence is understood to mean acts of physical coercion exercised upon

persons.

Threats are understood to mean the means of moral coercion that instill fear of imminent harm.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO I), Arts. 96, 110

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO II), Arts. 973

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1472

###### Art. 597.- Force upon things or breaking consists of any breaking,

rupture, demolition, perforation, or any other violence executed upon vessels, wagons, aerostats, walls, intermediate floors, ceilings, doors, windows, grilles, cabinets, dressers, chests, suitcases, filing cabinets or any other closed furniture; the removal of chains, bars or other instruments used to close, or prevent passage and guard things; and the breaking of straps, ropes, cords or other fastenings that protect certain effects, and the use of lockpicks.

Concordancias:

CODIGO CIVIL (LIBRO IV), Arts. 1472

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, FUERZA EN LAS COSAS, 29-abr-1997

###### Art. 598.- Assimilated to theft with force upon things is:

That of the furniture mentioned in the preceding article; and, That committed by breaking seals.

###### Art. 599.- Climbing is qualified as:

Any entry into houses, patios, corrals, or any other building, gardens, parks and enclosures, executed over doors, roofs, walls, or any other kind of enclosure; and, Entry through an underground opening, or through balconies or windows, or through any other part not intended for lawful entry.

###### Art. 600.- Lockpicks are qualified as:

Any hook, clasp, master key, imitated, falsified or altered key;

Keys that have not been intended by the owner, lessee, innkeeper or hotelkeeper for the locks, padlocks or lockings to which the guilty party has applied them; and, Lost, misplaced or stolen keys that have served to commit the act.

###### Art. 601.- A gang is the gathering of three or more persons, with the same

criminal intent, for the commission of a crime.

###### Art. 602.- The word weapon is understood to mean any machine, or any other

cutting, stabbing or blunt instrument that has been taken to kill, wound or strike, even when it is not used.

Jurisprudencia:

Gaceta Judicial, HERIDAS CAUSADAS POR ARMA CORTANTE, LESIONES, 15-jul-1953

#### Título XI

Crimes in the performance of duties by police and military officers

Nota: Título agregado por Ley No. 0, publicada en Registro Oficial Suplemento 196 de

19 de Mayo del 2010 .

##### Chapter I

On common offenses of function

Art. ... (602.3).- Insubordination.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who:

- 1. Refuses, prevents, or violently resists compliance with a legitimate service order;
- 2. Makes requests violently to a superior;
- 3. Threatens, offends or outrages a superior;
- 4. Wounds or injures a superior, in acts of service; or,
- 5. Removes armed troops from a unit, facility or installation, without legitimate superior order.

If the infraction is committed with weapons, publicity is made of it, it is committed in combat, state of exception or police operation respectively, the penalty shall be three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.

Note: Chapter and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.4).- Sedition.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, the military or police officers who, through express or tacit agreement, in number of four or more, perform any of the following acts:

- 1. Disobey legitimate orders received;
- 2. Fail to comply with service duties;
- 3. Threaten, offend or outrage a superior;
- 4. Attempt to prevent the taking of office of a superior or remove them from their function; or,
- 5. Act violently to make claims or petitions to a superior.

If the military or police officers perform any one or more of these acts in time of armed conflict, state of exception or police operation respectively, they shall be punished with three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment. If the acts take place in a situation of danger to the security of the unit or the service in the face of the seditious persons, resorting to weapons or assaulting a superior, they shall be punished with the penalty of three to six years of ordinary minor imprisonment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.5).- Conspiracy, proposal and apology of sedition.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officers who engage in conspiracy or proposal to commit the crime of sedition and those who incite members of the Armed Forces or National Police to commit this crime or make apology of it or of those who commit it.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.6).- Failure to prevent or report sedition.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who does not adopt the necessary measures or does not employ the rational means within their reach to prevent sedition in the units or services under their command or who, having knowledge that an attempt is being made to commit this crime, does not report it to their superiors.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.7).- False alarm.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who, without any justification, and with intent to cause harm, produces or spreads false alarm for preparation for conflict. Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.8).- Abuse of powers.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who, in the exercise of their authority or command:

- 1. Imposes punishments not established by law against their inferiors, or exceeds in their application;
- 2. Assumes, retains or prolongs illegally or improperly a command, service, position or military or police function;
- 3. Makes requisitions or imposes illegal contributions;
- 4. Orders their subordinates to perform functions inferior to their rank or employment; or alien to the interest of the service; or urges them to commit a crime that endangers the security of the National Police or the Armed Forces;
- 5. Obtains benefits for themselves or third parties, abusing the hierarchy, rank, function, level or prerogatives, provided that this act does not constitute another crime;
- 6. Allows persons outside or unconnected to the institution to exercise functions that correspond exclusively to members of the military or police service;
- 7. Threatens, offends or outrages an inferior; or,
- 8. Arbitrarily prevents an inferior from exercising their rights.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.9).- Military or police contempt.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who refuses to obey or does not comply with the orders or legitimate resolutions issued by civil or judicial authorities, provided that the act does not correspond to a higher penalty according to the provisions of this Code.

This crime is also configured when the military or police officer disobeys or resists complying with orders or legitimate resolutions of the Judicial Police, in their function as agents of authority and auxiliaries of the State Attorney General's Office. The contempt or resistance that the military or police officer opposes based on the rank or hierarchical level they hold, shall be considered as an aggravating circumstance.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.10).- Destruction or disabling of property.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years the military or police officer who destroys or disables property intended for public security or national defense.

The penalty shall be three months to one year, when through negligence they lose, destroy or disable the property indicated in this article.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.11).- Violation of correspondence.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who, without proper legal authorization, intercepts, examines, retains, records or disseminates correspondence or private or confidential communications of any type and by any means.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.12).- Crimes against public information not legally classified.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the military or police officer who, using any electronic, computer or similar means, obtains information to which they have access in their capacity as police or military officer, to later transfer it, publish it, disclose it, use it or transfer it under any title without proper authorization. The same penalty shall apply to whoever destroys or disables this type of information.

If the disclosure or fraudulent use are carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be punished with the maximum penalty.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.13).- Crimes against public information legally classified.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years, the military or police officer who, using any electronic, computer or similar means, obtains information classified in accordance with the law. The same penalty shall apply to whoever destroys or disables this type of information.

The disclosure or use of the information thus obtained, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years, provided that another crime of greater gravity is not configured.

If the disclosure or fraudulent use are carried out by the person or persons in charge of the custody or legitimate use of the information, they shall be punished with the maximum penalty.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.14).- Theft of police or military property.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years, the police or military officer who steals war material such as weapons, ammunition, explosives or police or military equipment. In the case of theft of medicines, clothing, food or other items that affect the employment of the National Police or the Armed Forces, they shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.15).- Robbery of police or military property.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years, the police or military officer who robs war material, such as weapons, ammunition, explosives or police or military equipment. In the case of robbery of medicines, clothing, food or other items that affect the employment of the National Police or the Armed Forces, they shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.16).- Purchase of stolen or robbed police or military property.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years, the police or military officer who acquires stolen or robbed property belonging to the National Police or the Armed Forces or intended for their employment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.17).- Theft of requisitioned property.- Shall be guilty of the crime of theft typified in this chapter, the police or military officer, who by reason of having carried out requisitions, has appropriated the requisitioned property.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Gazette Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

##### Capítulo II

Offenses Related to the Function of Police Officers

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of

May 19, 2010 .

###### Sección I

Of offenses against the duties of police service

Art. ... (602.18).- Acts against citizens.- Shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years, the female or male police officer who commits illegal deportations or transfers, illegitimate detentions, hostage taking, coercion to serve subversive groups or deprives any person of their right to be tried in an ordinary and impartial manner.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (602.19).- Evasion of responsibilities.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the female or male police officer who evades their responsibility in acts of service, when such omission causes damages.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (602.20).- Alteration of evidence.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year, the female or male police officer who puts at risk the obtaining or preservation of traces, material evidence or other elements of proof for the investigation of a crime.

The sanction shall be imprisonment of one to three years, if the female or male police officer destroys or produces any alteration of the elements of proof.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

###### Sección II

Of offenses against the duties of direction

Art. ... (602.21).- Abstention from execution of operations in internal upheaval.- Shall be punished with a penalty of imprisonment of one to three years, the female or male police officer, service chief or police unit chief who in time of internal upheaval and without the situation justifying it, fails to undertake or fulfill a mission, abstains from executing an operation when required to do so, or does not employ in the course of operations all the means required by compliance with the precepts of the law and legitimate orders received.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

###### Sección III

Of offenses against public or institutional property

Art. ... (602.22).- Offenses against institutional property.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year the female or male police officer who:

- 1. Executes or does not prevent, in a police place or establishment, acts that may produce fire or havoc, or originate a serious risk to the security of a unit or establishment of the National Police; or,
- 2. Conceals from their superiors breakdowns or serious deteriorations in facilities, supplies or logistical material in their charge that is for use by police personnel or service.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (602.23).- Exceeding authority in the execution of an act of service.- Shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, the female or male police officer who exceeds their authority in the execution of an act of service, and who as a consequence thereof, produces in a person injuries with an incapacity not greater than ninety days. If the injuries caused generate in a person incapacity greater than ninety days or permanent incapacity, they shall be punished with a penalty of ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

##### Capítulo III

Offenses Related to the Function of Military Officers

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of

May 19, 2010 .

###### Sección I

Of the particular circumstances of functional offenses of female or male military officers

Art. ... (602.24).- Territoriality.- Offenses of military function committed within the territory of the Republic or aboard military or merchant ships or aircraft mobilized for service shall be punished.

Functional offenses committed by female or male military officers abroad, based on the principle of reciprocity, shall be judged by the judges or courts determined by Ecuadorian laws.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .


This excerpt sits under: CODIGO PENAL > Libro II del Código Penal, cuando hubieren sido cometidos en contra de personas > Título XI

###### Sección II

Of crimes against the security, sovereignty and territorial integrity of the Republic

Art. ... (602.25).- Crimes of treason against the Homeland.- The military servant or server who commits any one or several of these acts, even against allied forces, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years in time of peace; and with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years in armed conflict:

- 1. Desert to the enemy forces;
- 2. Facilitate the entry of enemy forces into national territory or onto Ecuadorian or allied vessels or aircraft;
- 3. Carry out hostile actions against a foreign country with the intention of causing an international armed conflict for Ecuador;
- 4. Maintain negotiations with other States, tending to submit Ecuadorian territory in any form;
- 5. Declare himself or herself in rebellion while the Ecuadorian State faces international armed conflict;
- 6. Surrender to the enemy territory, plaza, post, position, construction, building, establishment, installation, ship, aircraft, weapons, troops or force under his or her command or defense materials; or induce or compel another to do so;
- 7. Give false or distorted news about the operations of the armed conflict;
- 8. Fail to give notice of the enemy's approach or of circumstances that directly affect the conflict, or the civilian population;
- 9. Prevent national or allied vessels, aircraft or troops from receiving the aid and news sent to them, with the intention of favoring the enemy;
- 10. Strike, order to strike or force to strike the national flag, without order from the Command in armed conflict;
- 11. Fail to comply with a legitimate order or arbitrarily alter it with the purpose of harming the Armed Forces of Ecuador or benefiting the enemy;
- 12. Disseminate news with the intention of instilling panic, discouragement or disorder in the troops, or execute any act that may produce the same consequences;
- 13. Maintain relations or correspondence with the enemy regarding the operations of the international armed conflict or of the Armed Forces of Ecuador or its allies; or, without due authorization, enter into understanding with the enemy to procure peace or the suspension of operations;
- 14. Release prisoners of war so that they may return to the enemy's armed forces, or return military equipment to the enemy;
- 15. Execute or order, within or outside the national territory, the recruitment of troops to enlist them in the enemy's ranks, seduce Ecuadorian troops for the same purpose or provoke their desertion; or,
- 16. Execute sabotage with the purpose of hindering national military operations or facilitating those of the enemy.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.26).- Attempt, conspiracy and proposition of treason against the Homeland.- In cases of treason against the Homeland, the attempt shall be punished in accordance with this Code; conspiracy and proposition shall be punished with a penalty of two thirds of that applicable to the perpetrator or perpetrators of the crime.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.27).- Prolongation of hostilities.- The military servant or server who prolongs hostilities, despite having been officially notified that peace, armistice or truce has been agreed with the enemy, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.28).- Espionage.- The military servant or server who in time of peace shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years:

- 1. Obtains, disseminates, falsifies or renders useless legally classified information and that its use or employment by a foreign country or threatens the security and sovereignty of the State;
- 2. Intercepts, removes, copies information, files, photographs, filming, recordings or others regarding troops, equipment, operations or missions of a military nature;
- 3. Sends documents, reports, graphics or objects that put at risk the security or sovereignty of the State, without being obliged to do so; or, if obliged by force, does not bring such fact to the knowledge of the authorities immediately;
- 4. Conceals relevant information from national military commands; or,
- 5. Alters, suppresses, destroys, diverts, even temporarily, information or objects of a military nature relevant to security, sovereignty or territorial integrity.

If the military servant or server performs any one or several of these acts in time of armed conflict, he or she shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

###### Sección III

Of crimes against the security of the State

Art. ... (602.29).- Rebellion.- The military servant or server who, in time of peace, commits one or several of the following acts shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years:

- 1. Rises up in arms, to overthrow the government or hinder the exercise of its powers;
- 2. Prevents the meeting of the National Assembly or dissolves it;
- 3. Prevents the convened elections; or,
- 4. Promotes, aids or sustains any armed movement to alter the peace of the State.

If the military servant or server commits any one or several of these acts in time of international or non-international armed conflict, he or she shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.30).- Omission in supply.- The military servant or server who, being obliged to do so by his or her function, refrains from supplying the troops for the fulfillment of military actions, putting the security of the State at risk, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

###### Sección IV

Of crimes against the operations and security of the Armed Forces

Art. ... (602.31).- Attack against the security of the Armed Forces.- The military servant or server who attacks the security of the Armed Forces by willful breach of his or her duties or obligations shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years, provided that the act does not constitute another crime.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.32).- Attack against the development of military operations.- The reservists who, in case of armed conflict, are called and unjustifiably do not appear within five days to perform military functions shall be punished with imprisonment of one to three years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.33).- Attack against the security of military operations.- The military servant or server who, in non-international armed conflict, surrenders or flees without having exhausted the means of defense and security required by military precepts or orders received shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years; and with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years if committed in international armed conflict.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Register Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

###### Sección V

Of the crime of desertion

Art. ... (602.34).- Desertion.- A military service member who, in armed conflict, abandons for more than five days the post, service or function that has been assigned to them, in an illegal and unjustified manner, shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to one year.

Note: Section and Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (602.35).- Failure to report desertion.- Direct superiors or heads of units or detachments who fail to report the desertion of their subordinates shall be punished with imprisonment from three months to one year.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

Art. ... (602.36).- Special circumstances of desertion.- Deserters shall be imposed the maximum penalty if the desertion is committed in conspiracy, in enemy territory, or while stealing, robbing or destroying property of the Armed Forces.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010 .

##### Chapter IV

Crimes against persons and property protected by

International Humanitarian Law

Note: Chapter added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.37).- Application of provisions in international or non-international armed conflict.- The provisions relating to international or non-international armed conflict are applicable from the day on which it takes place, regardless of formal declaration by the President of the Republic or of the decree of a state of exception throughout the national territory or part thereof, in accordance with the Constitution and the law.

The state of international or non-international armed conflict shall be deemed concluded once the state of exception has ended due to the disappearance of the causes that motivated it, due to the expiration of the term of its declaration, due to revocation of the decree that declared it; or, until the security conditions that were affected are restored.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.38).- Persons protected by International Humanitarian Law.- For the purposes of this chapter, persons considered as such by the current international instruments of International Humanitarian Law are considered protected, and in particular, the following:

- 1. The civilian population;
- 2. Persons who do not participate in hostilities and civilians in the power of the adverse party;
- 3. Medical or religious personnel;
- 4. Journalists on mission or accredited war correspondents;
- 5. Persons who have laid down their arms;
- 6. Persons who are out of combat or defenseless in the armed conflict;
- 7. Those who, before the beginning of hostilities, belonged to the category of stateless persons or refugees;
- 8. Political asylum seekers;
- 9. United Nations personnel and associated personnel protected by the Convention on the Safety of United Nations and Associated Personnel; and,
- 10. Any other person who has this status by virtue of the Geneva Conventions I, II, III and IV of 1949 and their additional protocols.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.39).- Homicide of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, kills a protected person shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.40).- Torture and cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, tortures or inflicts cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment on a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.41).- Collective punishments of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, inflicts collective punishments on a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.42).- Mutilations and experiments on protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, mutilates or performs medical or scientific experiments or extracts tissues or organs from a protected person shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.43).- Injury to physical integrity of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, affects or injures the physical integrity of a protected person, provided it does not constitute another crime of greater harm to the person, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.44).- Attack on sexual and reproductive integrity of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, injures or affects the sexual or reproductive integrity of a protected person shall be punished with special major imprisonment of sixteen to twenty-five years. This crime includes rape and other conduct that according to this Code affects sexual or reproductive integrity.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.45).- Deprivation of liberty of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, deprives a protected person of liberty shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years. This crime includes:

- 1. Taking of hostages;
- 2. Illegal detention;
- 3. Deportation or illegal transfer;
- 4. Forced displacement; or,
- 5. Delay or postponement in repatriation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.46).- Recruitment of children and adolescents.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, recruits or enlists girls, boys or adolescents in the armed forces or armed groups, or uses them to participate in the armed conflict, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.47).- Denial of judicial guarantees of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, deprives a person of the guarantees of due process or imposes or executes a penalty without having been tried in a judicial process, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.48).- Abolition and suspension of rights of protected person.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, declares abolished or suspended the rights, constitutional guarantees or judicial actions of protected persons, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.49).- Attack on protected person for terrorist purposes.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, carries out any form of attack on a protected person whose object or effect is to terrorize the civilian population, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.50).- Arbitrary contributions.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, imposes arbitrary contributions, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.51).- Omission of relief and humanitarian assistance measures.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, omits relief and humanitarian assistance measures in favor of protected persons, being obliged to do so, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.52).- Omission of protection measures.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, omits the adoption of measures for the general protection of the civilian population, being obliged to do so, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.53).- Obstruction of medical and humanitarian tasks.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, obstructs or prevents medical, health or relief personnel for the civilian population from carrying out the medical and humanitarian tasks that can and must be performed in accordance with the norms of International Humanitarian Law, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.54).- Crimes against active participants in armed conflict.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, commits any of the following acts against an active participant, shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years:

- 1. Forcing him or her to serve in any way in the armed forces of the adversary;
- 2. Depriving him or her of the right to a trial with the guarantees of due process; or,
- 3. Preventing or unjustifiably delaying release or repatriation.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.55).- Attack on protected property.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, directs or participates in attacks against the following protected property, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

- 1. Civilian objects that do not constitute a military objective;
- 2. Property intended to ensure the existence and integrity of civilian persons, such as zones and localities intended to separate them from military objectives, and property intended for their survival or care;
- 3. Property that is part of a peacekeeping or humanitarian assistance mission;
- 4. Property intended for the satisfaction of the civil and political rights of the civilian population, such as those intended for religious worship, the arts, science or charity;
- 5. Property that is part of the historical, cultural, or environmental heritage; and,
- 6. Other property protected under International Humanitarian Law.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.56).- Use of prohibited methods in the conduct of armed conflict.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, uses methods of war or armed conflict prohibited by International Humanitarian Law, and in particular, the following, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

- 1. Starvation of the civilian population, including through obstruction of supplies;
- 2. Death or injury by treachery of an enemy combatant or a member of the adverse party participating in the armed conflict;
- 3. Use of the presence of a protected person as a shield to place certain points, zones or military forces out of reach of warlike operations, or to obstruct enemy actions against specific military objectives;
- 4. The order to take no prisoners;
- 5. Attack on the civilian population as such;
- 6. Attack on civilian property; or,
- 7. Indiscriminate attack with the potential to cause death or injury to civilians, damage to protected property, or serious or disproportionate damage to the environment.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.57).- Destruction or appropriation of property of the adverse party.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, destroys or confiscates the property of the adverse party, without imperative military necessity, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of three to six years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.58).- Use of prohibited weapons.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, produces, possesses, stores, uses or distributes weapons prohibited by International Humanitarian Law, and in particular the following, shall be punished with extraordinary major imprisonment of twelve to sixteen years:

- 1. Poison or poisoned weapons;
- 2. Asphyxiating, toxic gases or other substances that produce the same effect;
- 3. Biological, bacteriological or toxic weapons;
- 4. Chemical weapons;
- 5. Bullets that expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as hard-jacketed bullets that do not completely cover the interior part or that have incisions;
- 6. Weapons whose main effect is injury by fragments that cannot be located by X-rays;
- 7. Mines, booby traps and other weapons with the same effect;
- 8. Incendiary weapons;
- 9. Blinding laser weapons;
- 10. Anti-personnel mines, except those obtained by authorized personnel of the Ecuadorian Army, when the purpose is to develop techniques for detection, clearance or destruction of mines;
- 11. Cluster munitions; or,
- 12. Other weapons that by their very nature cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering, or that have indiscriminate effects.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.59).- Environmental modification techniques for military purposes.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, uses environmental modification techniques for military, combat or other hostile purposes as a means to produce widespread, long-lasting or severe destruction, damage or harm to the environment, shall be punished with ordinary minor imprisonment of six to nine years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.60).- Use of prohibited means in the conduct of armed conflict.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, uses any other method of war or conflict prohibited by International Humanitarian Law shall be punished with extraordinary minor imprisonment of nine to twelve years.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Art. ... (602.61).- Improper use of protective signs, distinctive marks and national symbols.- A military officer or servant who, on the occasion of and in the development of armed conflict, simulates the status of a protected person or improperly uses emblems, flags, insignia or other protective signs contemplated in current international instruments, such as, shall be punished with imprisonment of three months to one year:

- 1. White flag;
- 2. National Flag, military insignia or uniforms of the enemy;
- 3. Insignia or uniform of the United Nations or other internationally recognized humanitarian organizations;
- 4. Distinctive emblems of the Geneva Conventions of 1949; and,
- 5. Emblems, denominations, distinctive signs of the Red Cross and the Red Crescent, in accordance with the Geneva Conventions of 1949 and their additional Protocols.

Note: Article added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

General Provisions

First.- The administrative disciplinary process corresponding to the same fact being investigated in the criminal process may proceed independently without the need for a final judgment in the latter.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- Execution of sentences.- Precautionary measures of a personal nature and penalties imposed on military or police officers or servants shall be carried out in places that guarantee their safety; without prejudice to the jurisdiction of prison guarantee judges.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Transitory Provisions

First.- Jurisdiction.- As of the publication of this law in the Official Registry, criminal guarantee judges and courts shall hear criminal proceedings for military and police crimes initiated prior to the effective date of this reform law, in accordance with the jurisdiction established in the Organic Code of the Judicial Function; and shall continue processing them in accordance with the procedural rules that were in force when they were initiated and with the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure, insofar as they are applicable. Such judges shall also hear proceedings for military and police criminal crimes initiated after the effective date of this reform law, and shall process them in accordance with the Code of Criminal Procedure.

The National Court of Justice, through a resolution of the Plenary, shall regulate the transfer of pending cases to the ordinary jurisdiction.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- Entry into the Judicial Function.- The members of the current military and police criminal courts and judges, as well as administrative personnel at all levels, may enter the judicial career in the Judicial Function, provided they comply with the provisions of literal e) of the fifth transitory provision of the Organic Code of the Judicial Function. In any case, no privilege shall be considered for entry into said career.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Third.- Collegiate bodies for military and police prosecution.- Immediately after the promulgation of this law, the Council of the Judiciary, through the Judicial School, must establish in the initial and ongoing training processes for prosecutors, female and male judges, training programs on police law, military law and International Humanitarian Law.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Fourth.- Personnel.- Within three hundred and sixty-five days (365) counted from the effective date of this law, the National Assembly must approve the law on police and military personnel that includes an update of the catalogs of disciplinary faults or violations, as well as the competent authority to judge and the procedures, respectful of due process, to impose administrative sanctions.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Fifth.- Disciplinary faults.- Until the National Assembly approves the law on police and military personnel that includes the classification and punishment of disciplinary faults and violations, police and military officers and servants shall be tried for the commission of disciplinary faults under the provisions currently in force.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Repealing Provisions

First.- The Criminal Code of the National Police, promulgated in Official Registry Supplement 1202 of August 20, 1960, and all its amendments are repealed.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Second.- The Military Criminal Code, published in Official Registry Supplement 356 of November 6, 1961, and all its amendments are repealed.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

Third.- All general and special provisions that are contrary to this Law are repealed.

Note: Provision added by Law No. 0, published in Official Registry Supplement 196 of May 19, 2010.

BOOK THREE

OF CONTRAVENTIONS

#### TITULO I

OF THE CLASSIFICATION OF CONTRAVENTIONS

###### Art. 603.- For the purpose of the procedure and imposition of penalties, contraventions are

divided, according to their greater or lesser severity, into contraventions of the first, second, third and fourth class; and the penalties corresponding to each of them are determined in the following chapters.

##### CAPITULO I

Of the contraventions of the first class

###### Art. 604.- Shall be punished with a fine of two to four dollars of the United States of

North America:

1o.- Those who construct chimneys, stoves or ovens, in violation of regulations; or fail to clean or maintain them, with danger of fire;

2o.- Those who, outside the cases permitted by municipal ordinances, occupy sidewalks or arcades with stoves, commercial articles and objects in general that interrupt or hinder the free transit of pedestrians, or those who transit through sidewalks or arcades on horseback or in any vehicle; or who, through them, carry objects that by their form and quality, obstruct passersby;

3o.- Those who introduce their animals into pastures, grazing lands or cultivated fields belonging to others that are fenced. The existence of this violation is presumed by the fact that the animals are found in any of said places;

4o.- Those who have left in streets, roads, plazas or other public places, pliers, crowbars, iron bars, ladders or other machines, instruments or weapons that thieves or other criminals may abuse. In addition, said objects shall be confiscated;

5o.- Those who without right have entered, or have passed or caused to pass their dogs, cattle or other animals, with or without load, through pastures or lands belonging to others that are fenced;

6o.- Those who conceal their true name and surname from authorities or police agents or other persons who have the right to demand that they disclose them, if the act does not constitute a crime;

7o.- Those who refuse to receive legitimate and admissible currency, or wish to receive it for less than the legal value it has in the Republic;

8o.- Those in charge of the care of an insane person whom they leave abandoned in public places without due supervision;

9o.- Those who go out dressed in an indecent manner or contrary to good customs;

10o.- Those who bathe violating the rules of decency;

11o.- Those who have on balconies, windows or roofs, flowerpots or other objects, without the necessary precautions to prevent their fall or nuisance to passersby;

12o.- Those who throw stones or other objects in public places, with danger to persons; or do so at houses or buildings, to the detriment thereof, or with danger to their inhabitants;

13o.- Those who cause any slight damage to public fountains, aqueducts, street lamps, or any other object of public service; or scratch, write on or dirty the walls of a building, without prejudice to civil compensation; 14o.- Those who have uninhabited and open houses or shops;

15o.-Nota: Numeral derogado por Ley No. 31, publicada en Registro Oficial 231 de 17 de Marzo del 2006 .

16o.- Those who transport objects, without police permission, during the night; 17o.- Those who tie animals of any kind to trees or railings of plaza gardens, public promenades or avenues, or to poles of telegraph, telephone or electric light lines;

18o.- Those who occupy any space of streets or roads when constructing their buildings;

19o.- Those who post notices or any paper on the walls of public buildings or private houses;

20o.- Those who do not paint or whitewash the exterior walls and balconies of their houses, in accordance with regulations;

21o.- Those who place awnings or curtains toward the street over the doors of their establishments, at a height less than two meters above the level of the sidewalk; 22o.- Those who place notices or posters outside the cases provided in municipal ordinances;

23o.- Those who do not fence the lots or lands they have within the towns; without prejudice to the sanctions imposed in municipal ordinances;

24o.- Those who, without being habitual drunkards, are found in any public place in a state of intoxication;

25o.- The owners or administrators of any industrial or commercial establishment who do not affix a permanent sign on which the name or business name and purpose thereof appear;

26o.- Those who on their shops, houses or properties in general, display inaccurate signs or inscriptions;

27o.- Those who when selling an article alter its price announced in advance to the public; 28o.- Those who detain traders, or prevent the sale of any article of commerce;

29o.- Those who carry out works that hinder traffic momentarily or perpetually, except in the case of authorization or urgent repair; without prejudice to the provisions of municipal ordinances;

30o.- Those who injure or torture an animal, even if it is to compel it to work, or for any other purpose;

31o.- Those who kill an animal, without necessity;

32o.- Those who drive animals with sharp or cutting instruments, capable of causing injuries;

33o.- Those who in service employ injured or mistreated animals;

34o.- Those who on the walls of public establishments, such as hotels, lodging houses, cafes, casinos, spas, etc., etc., write words or phrases that offend morality, or draw obscene pictures, if the act does not constitute a crime;

35o.- Those who publicly offend modesty, with indecent actions or words; 36o.- Those who do not assist or aid a person who in a public place is found alone, injured, mistreated or in danger of perishing, when they could do so without detriment to themselves, if the act is not punished as a crime;

37o.- Those who tame horses within the towns;

38o.- Those who have left wild or harmful animals at large;

39o.- Those who have incited, or have not restrained their dogs when they attack or chase passersby, even when they have not caused any harm; 40o.- Those who when encountering on foot, or on horseback, on the street, road or other public place, a person going in the opposite direction, dispute or obstruct their passage, instead of yielding to their right;

41o.- Those who tear down, break or erase notices, mail lists and, in general, any publication issued by authority or competent employee, thus causing harm to the public, if the act does not constitute a crime;

42o.- Those who close the doors of theaters and other public places, while there is attendance in them;

43o.- Those who fail in the submission and respect due to authority, even when not in the exercise of their functions, provided that in this case they have announced or made themselves known as such, if the act does not constitute a crime;

44o.- Those who cause quarrels or disturbances in a public place, during the day; 45o.- Those who, with speeches delivered in public, incite riots or rebellions, or disturb in any way the tranquility of the inhabitants, attack national prerogatives, or induce to commit any infraction, if the acts are not punished as a crime;

46o.- Those who enter another's house or dwelling to provoke a fight or quarrel, if the act does not constitute a crime;

47o.- Those who in streets and plazas set off firecrackers or rockets, or make bonfires, without special permission from the Police;

48o.- Those who remain in another's house or dwelling against the will of the owner; 49o.- Those who fly balloons with flammable substances, or burn fireworks, without police permission, when the act is not a crime;

50o.- Those who have wells without due safety measures;

51o.- Those who, by negligence or resistance, have not complied with the order given by the authority to repair or demolish buildings that threaten collapse; 52o.- Those who do not maintain due composure and moderation in temples, theaters and other meeting places; being able to be immediately removed by any of the police agents from the place of the violation;

53o.- Those who remain at the exit of temples, theaters, schools and colleges, forming groups of more than two, or cause annoyance to attendees; and, 54o.-Nota: Numeral derogado por Ley No. 31, publicada en Registro Oficial 231 de 17 de Marzo del 2006 .

Nota: Artículo reformado por Decreto Supremo 2636, publicado en el Registro Oficial 621 de 4 de Julio de 1978 .

Nota: Decreto Supremo 2636 derogado por Decreto Legislativo s/n, publicado en el Registro Oficial 36 de 1 de octubre de 1979 , que ordena volver al texto legal anterior. Nota: Artículo reformado por Art. 174 de Ley No. 75, publicada en Registro Oficial 635 de 7 de Agosto del 2002 .

##### CHAPTER II

Of second-class contraventions

###### Art. 605.- Shall be punished with a fine of four to seven dollars of the United States of

North America and imprisonment of one day, or with only one of these penalties:

1o.- Hoteliers, owners of lodging houses, lessors of furnished houses or apartments, owners and directors of gambling houses and transport operators, who have failed to record in a register kept for that purpose, the name, surname, domicile, status, dates of entry and exit of every person who has slept, spent a night in their house, lodged, attended, or traveled, as applicable;

2o.- The aforementioned individuals who fail to send daily the statements that they recorded the previous day in the register mentioned in the preceding number, to the first police authority of the place, or who fail to present that register when required by police employees or agents;

3o.- Those who mock any religious act or the external demonstrations of a cult;

4o.- Those who in any way oppose, impede or disturb the exercise and external demonstrations of a cult;

5o.- Those who have within the towns, factories or deposits of gunpowder or other explosive substances, or that produce mephitic, unhealthy exhalations, capable of damaging the air and making it annoying for the inhabitants, when the action or omission does not constitute a crime and without prejudice to what fire and health laws or regulations provide;

6o.- Those who violate the regulations and provisions of the authority on the custody of flammable or corrosive materials, or chemical products that may cause havoc;

7o.- Those who in case of war, or when the competent authority so warns, travel without the corresponding passport;

8o.- Those entrusted or committed to transport persons or things, who refuse to do so without justifiable cause;

9o.- The drivers of livestock or beasts, who for lack of precaution or foresight, are guilty of some damage; without prejudice to any other responsibility that may befall them on the occasion of the damage;

10o.- Those who, knowingly, transport in carts or other vehicles persons pursued by the authority, insane or intoxicated persons who are not heading to their domicile; unless in these last two cases they do so with permission from the authority;

11o.- The chiefs, captains, or owners of vessels, railroads or travel carriages, who do not pass to the corresponding authority a list of passengers they transport, with expression of nationality, origin and destination;

12o.- Those who carry out transactions on objects belonging to the State or intended for public use or service, such as weapons, military garments, or furniture of public establishments;

13o.- Those who violate the regulations relating to the supply of the towns; 14o.- The merchants who go about selling, through houses, or through streets, squares or any other public place, jewels, clothing, or any other furniture, without prior written permission from the police. The latter shall grant permission to persons who demonstrate honesty; 15o.- Those who for lack of care, or for not having executed works, or not having repaired those made on their respective properties, do not have clear and in good state of service the roads or public or neighborhood roads, in the parts that correspond to them; 16o.- Those who when watering their properties flood the public roads, or obstruct them with aqueducts or surface channels, or cause landslides of earth, stones, trees, etc., on said roads, if the act is not sanctioned by special laws;

17o.- Those who cause any damage to a person or property by the fall or collapse of any building or factory of their property, if the act does not constitute a crime; 18o.- Those who take as pledge furniture or livestock of others to demand the fulfillment of an obligation, or the reparation of damages;

19o.- Those who do not deliver to the police, within three days, things belonging to others found in any place;

20o.- Those who purchase outside of a fair any article of commerce or movable objects, jewels or articles of clothing, etc., from unknown persons, or who do not have the corresponding police permission, from minors under eighteen years of age, not authorized for the sale, or from domestic servants; without prejudice to the return of the purchased objects to their owner, if the seller is not such;

21o.- Those who practice the trade of divining, forecasting, explaining dreams, finding hidden treasures, or curing by means of certain artifices, without prejudice to the confiscation of the instruments or articles they use for such arts; 22o.- Those who take or remove fruits of any kind from the orchards, gardens or fields of others;

23o.- Those who build latrines, aqueducts, baths, drains, or any other similar work, without subjecting themselves to the regulations on the matter;

24o.- Those who violate the regulations issued on this matter by the authority. 25o.- Those who mistreat, injure or exercise acts of resistance against police agents in the exercise of their functions, if the act does not constitute a crime; 26o.- Those who in their taverns accept intoxicated persons, or sell them liquors of any kind, or tolerate their continuation in them;

27o.- The owners or administrators of taverns or gambling houses who admit minors in them;

28o.- Those who give alcoholic or fermented liquors to drink to a minor; 29o.- Those who provide the same liquors to persons for whom there is prior written prohibition by the police or their parents or guardians; 30o.- Those who open holes or ditches in streets, squares or roads;

31o.- Those who publicly play carnival;

32o.- Those who in the places of which they are owners, lessees, tenants, usufructuaries or users, have maliciously killed or seriously injured, to the detriment of another, a domestic animal other than those mentioned in Art. 411 of this Code; 33o.- Those who decrease the weight, quantity or measure of an article at the time of sale; and, 34o.- Those who violate the regulations relating to the manufacture of fetid or unhealthy objects, to the establishment of tanneries, fireworks factories, dyeing shops and other factories that can alter the atmosphere with mephitic exhalations and corrupted vapors harmful to the health of the inhabitants, if the act is not sanctioned by special laws.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Register 621 of July 4, 1978 .

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree without number, published in Official Register 36 of October 1, 1979 , which orders return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Art. 175 of Law No. 75, published in Official Register 635 of August 7, 2002 .

##### CHAPTER III

Of third-class contraventions

###### Art. 606.- Shall be punished with a fine of seven to fourteen dollars of the United States of

North America and with imprisonment of two to four days, or with only one of these penalties: 1o.- Those who, outside the cases provided for in Chapter VII of Title V, Book II of this Code, have voluntarily damaged or destroyed the movable property of another;

2o.- Those who have caused the death or serious injury to animals as a result of the release of other harmful animals, or by the poor direction or excessive loading of vehicles, carriages, horses, draft animals, pack animals or riding animals;

3o.- Those who, through lack of foresight or lack of precaution, cause the same damages through the use or employment of weapons, or by throwing hard objects or any substances;

4o.- Those who have caused the same accidents due to the age, deterioration or lack of repair of houses or buildings, or due to obstructions or excavations or other works made in or near streets, roads, squares or public thoroughfares, without the precautions or signs provided for in the regulations, or by custom;

5o.- Those who take to graze animals of any kind and at any time to natural or artificial meadows, plantations or seedbeds of fruit trees or other types, belonging to another, without prejudice to the corresponding civil compensation;

6o.- Those who, finding a thing belonging to another, being authorities or police agents, do not deposit it with the latter, within twenty-four hours, or who, having knowledge of a finding, do not proceed in accordance with the provisions of the Civil Code;

7o.- Those who conduct water through public roads or streets, provided that they do so through uncovered pipes, without prejudice to being compelled to cover the pipes; 8o.- Those guilty of nocturnal brawls or disturbances;

9o.- Those who form meetings or gatherings for any political, religious, etc., demonstrations, without the corresponding written permission from the police;

10o.- Those who construct projecting windows, at a height of less than two meters above the level of the sidewalk or of the streets, without prejudice to demolition;

11o.- Those who engage in bullfighting, even in cases permitted by Law, or give any other public spectacles, even those not prohibited, without prior and special permission from the police;

12o.- Those who remain for a long time and without any plausible purpose standing on street corners or other places not intended for the recreation of the inhabitants; Note: The unconstitutionality of numeral 12 of article 606 of the Penal Code is declared for contradicting the provisions of articles 76 numeral 2; 76 numeral 3; 82; 76 numeral 7; 11 numeral 2 of the Constitution. Therefore, its expulsion from the Ecuadorian legal system is determined. Issued by Resolution of the Constitutional Court No. 11, published in Supplementary Official Registry 641 of February 15, 2012.

13o.- Those who spread false news or rumors that relate to public order, the security of the State or national honor;

14o.- Those who spread false news or rumors against the honor and dignity of persons or families, or concern themselves with the intimate life of the latter, without prejudice to the action for insult;

15o.- Those who direct at another a slight non-slanderous insult;

16o.- Those who receive in their house, without the knowledge of the authority, runaway minors;

17o.- Those who violate the regulations on amusements and public spectacles; 18o.- Those who do not fence the lands they have within towns, after having been required to do so by the authorities; and, 19o.- Those who cause any damage or harm to facilities or works intended for the provision of lighting, drinking water, or to lights, lamps or lanterns, etc., intended for public service, if the act is not a crime.

... Those who violate the right to privacy, under the terms established in the Law on Electronic Commerce, Electronic Signatures and Data Messages.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 67, published in Supplementary Official Registry 557 of April 17, 2002.

Note: Article amended by Art. 176 of Law No. 75, published in Official Registry 635 of August 7, 2002.

##### CAPITULO IV

Of fourth-class contraventions

###### Art. 607.- Shall be punished with a fine of fourteen to twenty-eight dollars of the United

States of America and imprisonment of five to thirty days:

1o. Theft, provided that the value of the stolen things does not exceed fifty percent of a unified basic remuneration of the worker in general.

2o.- Ministers of a religion who, in temples or religious places, streets or squares, preach against or in favor of a specific political party;

3o.- Those who voluntarily wound, or give blows to another, causing illness or incapacity for personal work that does not exceed three days;

4o.-Note: Numeral repealed by Law No. 31, published in Official Registry 231 of March 17, 2006.

5o.- Those who establish gambling houses, without permission from the authority, and those who attend said houses;

6o.- Those who use things belonging to another without the will or without the consent of the owner, even when they do not have the intention to appropriate them;

7o.- Those who have damaged urban or rural fences belonging to another, whatever the materials of which they were made, when the value of the damage does not exceed one hundred sucres;

8o.- Those guilty of mistreatment against their domestics or servants, without prejudice to the corresponding penalty if the mistreatment constitutes a crime;

9o.- Those who fail, in any manner, with words, gestures, actions, etc., towards their ascendants, without prejudice to the corresponding penalty in case the act also constitutes another infraction.

For the imposition of this penalty, the judge shall necessarily take into account the provisions of Art. 31 of this Code;

10o.- Anyone who outrages a person with slaps, kicks, pushes, lashes, stones, sticks, or in any other manner, but without causing illness or injury, nor incapacitating them for work, without prejudice to the action for insult, in cases where there is ground; and, 11o.- Those who monopolize or attempt to monopolize negotiations on articles of daily consumption in butcher shops, market squares or other places, without prejudice to the confiscation of said articles and the other penalties imposed by municipal ordinances.

Note: Article amended by Supreme Decree 2636, published in Official Registry 621 of July 4, 1978.

Note: Supreme Decree 2636 repealed by Legislative Decree s/n, published in Official Registry 36 of October 1, 1979, which orders a return to the previous legal text. Note: Article amended by Law No. 66, published in Official Registry 395 of March 14, 1990.
